# Scenarios — `hq/`

> Module contract: what `hq/` (the Wasp app that is the org's control plane and its whole browser UI) promises its users. Flat capability list.
> This file was named `site.md` until 2026-09-26, when it took its module's name; the separate `site/` module (the Execution Site) has its own file, [`site.md`](site.md).
> **Scenario ids are unique across every file in this directory**, not per file, because a test cites a scenario by its id alone (see [`../qa-strategy.md`](../qa-strategy.md), Scenario test citation). The next free id for all of them is the `next-sc` above.
> The agent proposes changes (additions, rewordings, removals); a human reviews and approves. Each scenario carries a stable `SC-NNN` id — reference it (not the prose) from stories (`AC → SC-NNN`) and the coverage map. Ids are append-only: assigned once, never reused or renumbered. A retired scenario keeps its id, its text struck through and the reason.
> A scenario states what is promised, not whether the product keeps the promise today: the scenarios marked in [`../coverage.md`](../coverage.md) as not yet met are work still to do.

`hq/` is the entry point for web users — cross-module flows starting here own their bullets in this file. The module declares 350 Wasp operations (180 actions, 150 queries, 20 api); user-facing scenarios cluster operations into capabilities (typical ratio ~3:1). Who may do what, and how a person reaches each journey, is mapped in [`../coverage.md`](../coverage.md).

---

## Public + onboarding

- **SC-001** — The login page renders (invite-only; no public landing page). · fulfils AC-001c
- **SC-002** — User can sign in with username + password. · fulfils AC-001a, AC-001b
- **SC-003** — Self-service sign-up is disabled — an account is created only by an invite or by an admin's create-account action: no request, including one sent straight to the authentication framework's own sign-up endpoint, creates an account without an invite, before or after the first admin exists. · fulfils AC-001c
- **SC-004** — OAuth (Google / Apple) sign-in *(deferred — real SSO out of scope)*.
- **SC-005** — User can sign out. · fulfils AC-003a
- **SC-006** — While hq has no accounts the sign-in page points to the setup link, and the first-admin link — one-time, 24 hours, from HQ's own address (**SC-708**, **SC-709**) — lets the first operator make the first Admin account with their name, a username and a password of at least 8 characters, signs them in and takes them into setup. · fulfils AC-009a, AC-009b
- **SC-007** — Invitee can validate an invite code and redeem it to create an account with the invite's role. · fulfils AC-007a, AC-008a, AC-008d
- **SC-008** — A new admin completes setup once — You, Your organisation, AI and a last look (**SC-710**); the organisation name is required, vision and mission are optional, and finishing records setup done for the HQ and lands the admin on Home. Setup needs no Execution Site or agent, and no provider (**SC-1845**). *(Changed 2026-11-10: setup is four steps, with one AI step in place of the choice, model and voice steps — **SC-1184**.)* · fulfils AC-010a, AC-010c, AC-012a, AC-013a · tests `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/SetupPage.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/setup.spec.ts`
- **SC-009** — ~~Admin can test runtime connectivity during setup — validate the LLM key, ping the runtime, and see the sign-in command for the environment.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the runtime checks in setup (validate an API key, a "hello" check, the sign-in command) were removed from the wizard; model access is set on the Model Access page — see **SC-365** to **SC-374**.

- **SC-144** — A user ticking "Remember my username" has their username — never the password — saved to `localStorage` on submit (stored as `{username}` only) and pre-filled into the sign-in form on the next visit on that device. · fulfils AC-002a
- **SC-145** — A user signing in with "Remember my username" unticked has the saved username removed from `localStorage`. · fulfils AC-002b
- **SC-146** — Redeeming an invite with a username that is already taken is rejected before any account is created (400 "Username … already taken"). · fulfils AC-008b
- **SC-147** — In setup the admin goes on with each step's primary button and goes back with Back (and the browser's Back); the AI step offers Skip for now while no provider works and Continue once one does (**SC-1845**). *(Changed 2026-11-10: the AI step is one step, with no separate choice, model provider or voice step — **SC-1184**.)* · fulfils AC-271a · tests `hq/e2e/setup.spec.ts`

## Projects + boards

- **SC-017** — An admin can create a project on an available Execution Site (chosen automatically when only one exists): its key is auto-derived, 2–6 uppercase letters, unique, and immutable once any task exists; the creator becomes its project admin; anyone below ADMIN is refused with the role named. · fulfils AC-022a, AC-022b, AC-022c, AC-022d, AC-022e, AC-024b, AC-024c
- **SC-018** — User can edit project metadata. · fulfils AC-024a
- **SC-019** — An admin can delete a project; deletion cascades to its boards and tasks, and anyone below ADMIN is refused with the role named. · fulfils AC-025b
- **SC-020** — A project admin can assign agents to a project and unassign them; anyone else is refused. Agents are assigned and people are members — they are different lists (see **SC-395**, **SC-398**). · fulfils AC-026a, AC-026b
- **SC-21** — ~~A Member of the project can run all queued tasks in it;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — Run all and Run a board are replaced by an agent's Start and Start all agents in the Workroom (adr-0175); see SC-1804 and SC-1805.
- **SC-22** — ~~A project's Boards tool lists its boards, Project Tasks first, archived boards only on request.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-23** — ~~User can create a board of kind Workflow or Sprints with named columns, from a template or with the defaults.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-24** — ~~User can edit a board's name and columns;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-25** — ~~A board's auto-queue column queues the tasks already in it that have an agent and no run pending, on enable, and releases its queued tasks on disable;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-026** — ~~User can complete a board: non-done tasks return to the backlog and a rollover board is created in the same series.~~ **Retired 2026-10-12** — a board is a lasting workflow, archived instead of completed, and a sprint is finished instead of a board (adr-0097); see **SC-544**, **SC-548**.
- **SC-027** — ~~User can view a board's series (rollover) history.~~ **Retired 2026-10-12** — there are no series; past sprints are read from the board (see **SC-550**).
- **SC-28** — ~~User can delete a board; its open tasks go to Project Tasks' Backlog and its done tasks to Project Tasks' Done (not deleted);~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-29** — ~~An admin can create, edit and delete org board templates (system templates are protected);~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — board templates are replaced by task templates, which hold task types with their columns and custom fields (adr-0174, adr-0176); see SC-1826 and SC-1827.
- **SC-30** — ~~A Member of the project can run all tasks on a board;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — Run all and Run a board are replaced by an agent's Start and Start all agents in the Workroom (adr-0175); see SC-1804 and SC-1805.
- **SC-31** — ~~User can move a task between columns of a board, to its Backlog, or to another board (where it lands in that board's Backlog);~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a task is on no board: it has a type, a column of that type, a task state and a place in the project's one order (adr-0174); see SC-1784 and SC-1785.

- **SC-158** — With no projects, the list shows a first-project empty state; when nothing matches, the line names the search ("No project matches “intra”") or the product or programme chosen, never "filters". *(Changed 2026-11-06: the no-match line names what excluded the projects.)* · fulfils AC-023c · tests `hq/src/projects/screen/__tests__/projectsScreen.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/projectsBoards.spec.ts`

- **SC-173** — ~~Deleting a built-in (system) board template is rejected.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — board templates are replaced by task templates, which hold task types with their columns and custom fields (adr-0174, adr-0176); see SC-1826 and SC-1827.

## Tasks

- **SC-032** — User can add a task to the project backlog (auto-generated `taskKey` per project). · fulfils AC-040a, AC-040b
- **SC-033** — User can edit task fields inline. · fulfils AC-041a, AC-041c
- **SC-034** — A Member of the project can run a single task with its assigned agent; its Viewer, or a person who is not a member of a private project, is refused. · fulfils AC-045a, AC-045b
- **SC-035** — User can reassign a task to a different agent. · fulfils AC-041b
- **SC-36** — ~~User can return a task to its board's Backlog;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a task is on no board: it has a type, a column of that type, a task state and a place in the project's one order (adr-0174); see SC-1784 and SC-1785.
- **SC-37** — ~~User can move a task to another board; it lands in that board's Backlog and a run queued for it is withdrawn;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a task is on no board: it has a type, a column of that type, a task state and a place in the project's one order (adr-0174); see SC-1784 and SC-1785.
- **SC-038** — A Member of the project can archive a task; its Viewer is refused, told they are its Viewer. · fulfils AC-043a, AC-043b
- **SC-039** — A Member of the project can permanently delete a task after a confirming second click; its Viewer is refused, told they are its Viewer. · fulfils AC-042b
- **SC-040** — ~~User can clear a task's chat session (blocked while the task is running).~~ **Retired 2026-11-06** — clearing a task's session went: a task's runs are kept as long as the task, and only deleting the task deletes them. · fulfils AC-048a, AC-048b
- **SC-041** — User can comment on a task; updates appear live to other viewers (socket). · fulfils AC-049a, AC-049b
- **SC-042** — A member can attach a file (any type, up to 10 MB) to a task; only members of the task's project can list or download it, by a link to hq's relay that hq signs only after checking membership at every opening (**SC-1516**, **SC-1518**); a private project's attachment answers not-found to anyone else. *(Changed 2026-10-27: a download is a ticket on hq's relay, no longer a request answered by hq.)* · fulfils AC-050a, AC-050b, AC-050c
- **SC-043** — A person browses a project's tasks in its Tasks tool (see **SC-513**) and, in Your tasks, the tasks done on their behalf across every project they can see (see **SC-517**), searching by title or key and filtering by agent, board, priority and project. · fulfils AC-051a, AC-051b

- **SC-174** — ~~Creating a task with no column places it in its board's Backlog (a task created queued, with no column, goes to the first column);~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a task is on no board: it has a type, a column of that type, a task state and a place in the project's one order (adr-0174); see SC-1784 and SC-1785.
- **SC-175** — Deleting a task requires a second confirming click (the button arms to "Confirm"); the server `deleteTask` performs no confirmation of its own. · fulfils AC-042a
- **SC-176** — ~~Reviving a task whose board is not COMPLETED (or that has no board) is rejected with "Task is not on a completed board".~~ **Retired 2026-10-12** — a board is never completed, so there is nothing to revive (`reviveTask` is gone; adr-0097).
- **SC-177** — Opening a task by key parses `KEY-NN` (2–6 uppercase letters, dash, digits); a malformed key returns 400, a well-formed key for a missing project or task returns 404. · fulfils AC-051c

## Agents + runs

- **SC-044** — An admin can create an agent (name, handle, role, model); avatar and cover images are set on the agent's detail page; anyone below ADMIN is refused with the role named. · fulfils AC-052a, AC-052b, AC-052c, AC-062a
- **SC-045** — An admin can edit an agent in place — its name, handle and role; its model, runtime, approval, allowed tools and command patterns; and its instructions document — and each saved change is what the agent's next run uses. · fulfils AC-053a, AC-053c, AC-056a, AC-056b
- **SC-046** — User can set an agent's avatar/cover image by upload or preset. · fulfils AC-055a, AC-055b
- **SC-047** — User can preview an agent's assembled prompt for chat or task mode. · fulfils AC-057a, AC-057b
- **SC-048** — User can view an agent's statistics (session/run counts, token usage, task breakdown). · fulfils AC-062a, AC-062b
- **SC-049** — ~~User can configure an agent's heartbeat (enable, interval), fire a manual pulse, and view recent heartbeat runs.~~ **Retired 2026-10-05** — an agent has no schedule of its own: the heartbeat, its settings, its manual pulse and its tab are gone (adr-0087). Work starts on its own only from a boundary's automation — see **SC-451** to **SC-455**.
- **SC-051** — An admin can export and import an agent archive; an import replaces that agent's content and settings in place and never replaces the kernel agent. · fulfils AC-060a, AC-060b, AC-061a
- **SC-052** — An admin can delete an agent; its sessions, runs and transcripts on every site, its stored content and its memory go with it, and the kernel agent cannot be deleted. · fulfils AC-054a, AC-054b
- **SC-054** — A run never waits for approval: a tool call flagged as risky that the run was not approved for is refused and listed as a request — a deferred run carries on, an immediate run stops (**SC-768**) — and approving it starts the next run (**SC-640**, **SC-769**). A Member or Admin of the project approves or rejects the request; its Viewer is refused. · fulfils AC-063a, AC-063b, AC-064a
- **SC-055** — Risk-rule violations cause auto-rejection without reaching the user. · fulfils AC-070a, AC-070b, AC-070c
- **SC-056** — User can cancel a running agent run from any pre-terminal state. · fulfils AC-065a, AC-065b
- **SC-057** — Stopping an agent (an admin's act) cancels its pending and running runs and reports how many; it switches nothing off, because an agent has nothing scheduled to switch off. · fulfils AC-059a
- **SC-058** — A person can view a task's run history, newest first (`getTaskRuns`). A person's own runs, across conversations and tasks, are on the Runs screen — see **SC-647**. The recent-runs list that belonged to the debug page is gone. · fulfils AC-068b
- **SC-059** — User can inspect a run's tool-call log (tool, target, risk level, blocked + reason). · fulfils AC-069a, AC-069b
- **SC-060** — ~~User can clear their own run history.~~ **Retired 2026-10-12** — "clear my run history" lived on the debug page and was removed with it (`clearAgentRuns` is gone); the Runs screen is a record that cannot be cleared. (Story US-067 retired with it.)

- **SC-178** — Renaming an agent handle to the reserved `@kernel` handle is rejected (403); renaming to a handle already taken by another agent currently fails on the database unique constraint rather than a clean validation message. · fulfils AC-053b
- **SC-179** — Stopping an agent returns the count of pending/running runs it cancelled (`cancelledRuns`) with `ok: true`. · fulfils AC-059b
- **SC-180** — Importing an agent archive reports only whether the agent as a whole was created or updated (`{ agentId, handle, created }`) — no per-file breakdown. · fulfils AC-061b
- **SC-181** — Importing a malformed archive (no root dir, missing/invalid `agent.json`, or missing `name`/`handle`) is rejected with a specific message. · fulfils AC-061c
- **SC-182** — Opening an agent by an unknown handle returns 404; the detail page renders an "Agent not found" state. · fulfils AC-062c
- **SC-183** — Approving a run whose `manifestStatus` is not `PENDING_APPROVAL` is rejected (400). · fulfils AC-063c
- **SC-184** — Rejecting a task run's manifest posts a `__MANIFEST_REJECTED__` comment and blocks the task ("manifest rejected — awaiting human review") without starting it again, so it waits for a person; rejecting a chat run records a transcript entry. · fulfils AC-064b
- **SC-185** — Stopping all sessions org-wide returns the total number of runs cancelled (`{ cancelled }`). · fulfils AC-066b

## Skills + templates

- **SC-061** — User can browse the skills library (platform and org). · fulfils AC-071a, AC-071b
- **SC-062** — User can create, edit, and delete org library skills (platform library skills are read-only). · fulfils AC-072a, AC-072b, AC-073a, AC-073b, AC-074a
- **SC-063** — User can install a library skill onto the organisation (active for agent runs). · fulfils AC-075a, AC-075b
- **SC-064** — User can save a skill directly on an agent (without the library). · fulfils AC-076a, AC-076b
- **SC-065** — User can browse and apply agent templates (creating a fully configured agent). · fulfils AC-077a, AC-078a
- **SC-066** — User can review and tailor a workforce template before applying (toggle teams/agents/skills/projects, resolve handle collisions). · fulfils AC-079a, AC-079b, AC-080a, AC-080b
- **SC-067** — User can apply a workforce template, save a draft, and confirm an agent-proposed one; confirming applies it and finishes the proposal with no run queued (`hq/src/library/__tests__/actions.test.ts`, `libraryQueries.integration.test.ts`). · fulfils AC-081a, AC-082a, AC-083a, AC-083b, AC-084a
- **SC-068** — User can see pending agent-proposed workforce templates awaiting review. · fulfils AC-085a

- **SC-186** — Applying an agent template with a handle that already exists is rejected (409) and no agent record or workspace is created. · fulfils AC-078b
- **SC-187** — Applying a workforce template creates its teams with `enabled: false`; its agents have no schedule to switch on, and no task starts by itself (see SC-1801). · fulfils AC-081b
- **SC-188** — Workforce application is not atomic — a mid-way failure leaves already-created agents (and their workspace dirs), teams, and projects behind; the error surfaces on the review page. · fulfils AC-081c
- **SC-189** — Confirming a reviewed agent-proposed workforce proposal returns the user to `/org` on success. · fulfils AC-084b

## Sessions + chat

- **SC-069** — ~~User can create a session and chat with an agent. · fulfils AC-086a, AC-087a~~ **Retired 2026-10-18** — no chat session is created any more; the `conversations` flag and the chat path it kept are removed: a conversation is held in hq and starts as **SC-940** says, and a chat from before is as **SC-1019** says.
- **SC-070** — A new conversation auto-names itself from the first user message. · fulfils AC-088a, AC-088b
- **SC-071** — User can rename a session and change the model it uses. · fulfils AC-089a, AC-089b
- **SC-072** — User can archive a session. · fulfils AC-090a
- **SC-073** — ~~User can browse their own chat sessions and open a session transcript, while the new conversations are off; with them on, a chat is from before them and is neither listed nor opened (**SC-1019**). · fulfils AC-091a, AC-091b~~ **Retired 2026-10-18** — chat sessions are no longer listed or opened; a place lists its conversations as **SC-1012** says. (Criteria AC-091a and AC-091b retired with it.)
- **SC-074** — User can delete a session (cancelling its active runs first). · fulfils AC-093a
- **SC-075** — User can stop a task's running session. · fulfils AC-092a
- **SC-076** — Stopping every run is an admin's act: it cancels the active runs on every registered, non-revoked Execution Site, and anyone below ADMIN is refused with the role named (see **SC-415** and **SC-416** for confirmation, the result shown and staying stopped). · fulfils AC-066a
- **SC-077** — A chat reply streams over the socket: its text grows as it arrives, and the finished reply shows its Copy, Usage and Details actions. · fulfils AC-087a
- **SC-078** — Speech to text works in a conversation — dictation into the message box (**SC-1003**) and voice (**SC-995**) — through a short-lived token for the organisation's speech-to-text provider *(Inworld, AssemblyAI or Agenthix Cloud)*. *(Reworded 2026-10-17.)* *(Changed 2026-10-23: the provider is whichever speech-to-text provider the resolver picks for the agent (**SC-1205**).)* *(Changed 2026-11-09: Inworld is one of them and the default way to hear, with AssemblyAI the fall-over — **SC-1835**.)* · fulfils AC-094a

- **SC-190** — ~~Creating a chat session against a non-existent agent id is rejected (404 "Agent not found"); no session is created. · fulfils AC-086b~~ **Retired 2026-10-18** — no chat session is created any more, so there is nothing to refuse; the `conversations` flag and the chat path it kept are removed: a conversation is held in hq and starts as **SC-940** says, and a chat from before is as **SC-1019** says.
- **SC-191** — Sending a message into another person's conversation is rejected (404); only its owner can send. A task's session is shared with its project instead (see **SC-518**). · fulfils AC-087b
- **SC-192** — Renaming a session or changing its model on a session the user does not own is rejected (404); a model change takes effect on the next message. · fulfils AC-089c
- **SC-193** — Archiving a session the user does not own is rejected (404). · fulfils AC-090b
- **SC-194** — Requesting voice input with no working listening provider returns an error with a clear message; when the provider is unreachable/rejects the key returns 502 with the upstream reason. *(Changed 2026-10-23: with no listening provider working it answers 503 saying voice is down and who has been told — **SC-1205**.)* *(Changed 2026-10-28: with no listening provider ever added it says voice is not set up, rather than down — **SC-1667**.)* · fulfils AC-094b

## Settings + integrations

- **SC-079** — ~~Admin can configure a GitHub PAT and verify it.~~ **Superseded** by the GitHub App model — GitHub is connected via the App Manifest flow, not a PAT. See **SC-256**.
- **SC-080** — ~~User can see the current GitHub connection status; admin can disconnect it.~~ **Superseded** — connection status is now the GitHub App's registration + installations. See **SC-256** / **SC-258**.
- **SC-081** — ~~User can create, link, and unlink a GitHub repo for a project. a deploy key is provisioned automatically (deploy keys retired — access is via the GitHub App's scoped tokens; see **SC-257**, **SC-259**).~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — project repositories are attached to storage, not to a project record, and no deploy keys are provisioned — see **SC-240** and **SC-241**.
- **SC-082** — ~~User can create, link, and unlink a wiki repo for a product, and browse a product's wiki.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the product wiki was never built as a feature and is gone (**SC-253**).
- **SC-083** — ~~Admin can generate / reissue an MCP key.~~ **Retired 2026-10-05** — the org-wide MCP key is gone; a tool outside the product connects with a connection its own person creates (adr-0090) — see **SC-478** to **SC-480**.
- **SC-084** — An external MCP client authenticates with a person's connection token and reaches the tool catalogue, narrowed to that connection's permission groups (see **SC-479**). · fulfils AC-134a, AC-134b
- **SC-085** — An external MCP client can list and propose a workforce template, creating an in-review item for a human. · fulfils AC-135a, AC-135b
- **SC-086** — Admin can update HQ's own settings (its name and description, under Organisation › Infrastructure › HQ, **SC-533**). · fulfils AC-123a
- **SC-087** — Admin can update org risk rules. · fulfils AC-124a, AC-124b
- **SC-088** — ~~Admin can test an external LLM provider.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — testing a provider key from settings was removed with the setup checks; a stored key is not tested when saved.
- **SC-089** — ~~Admin can configure voice providers.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — voice providers are added on Manage organisation › Providers like any provider (**SC-1170**), and the listening and speech models are chosen on Models (**SC-1200**, **SC-1203**).
- **SC-090** — Admin can preview and list available text-to-speech voices, and remove a voice provider. *(Changed 2026-10-23: voices are listed by the voice dialog (**SC-1204**) and a voice provider is removed on Providers (**SC-1174**).)* · fulfils AC-127a, AC-128a
- **SC-091** — User can set an agent's voice (voice id). *(Changed 2026-10-23: there is no switch: every agent has a voice, chosen from the provider's list, and the model is locked to Inworld TTS 2 — **SC-1201**, **SC-1203**.)* · fulfils AC-129a
- **SC-092** — Admin can view environment and version/build status. · fulfils AC-125a, AC-125b
- **SC-093** — User can pair a mobile app with this HQ. · fulfils AC-130a
- **SC-094** — The mobile app exchanges a pairing code for a device session that renews while it is used and lapses after 30 days unused (**SC-1100**). · fulfils AC-131a

- **SC-195** — ~~A product's wiki sync interval must be 0 (off) or at least 5 minutes, else it is rejected.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the product wiki was never built as a feature and is gone (**SC-253**).
- **SC-196** — ~~Creating a product wiki repo whose GitHub repo already exists links to it (GitHub 422) instead of failing.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the product wiki was never built as a feature and is gone (**SC-253**).
- **SC-197** — ~~Linking an existing repo as a product wiki rolls back the stored URL if deploy-key provisioning or clone fails.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the product wiki was never built as a feature and is gone (**SC-253**).
- **SC-198** — ~~Unlinking a product wiki when none is linked is rejected (400); the GitHub repo is not deleted (deploy key revoked, URL cleared, local mirror removed).~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the product wiki was never built as a feature and is gone (**SC-253**).
- **SC-199** — ~~The wiki view shows a "No wiki linked" empty state when none is linked, and an "empty or not yet synced" message when the synced tree is empty.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the product wiki was never built as a feature and is gone (**SC-253**).
- **SC-200** — ~~Creating a project GitHub repo whose repo already exists links to it (GitHub 422) instead of failing.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a project's repository is attached to its storage by connecting a repository (**SC-240**, **SC-241**); there is no per-project repository record, no deploy key and no rollback of a stored URL.
- **SC-201** — ~~Creating a project repo is rejected when GitHub is not configured (422) or the project already has a repo (400).~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a project's repository is attached to its storage by connecting a repository (**SC-240**, **SC-241**); there is no per-project repository record, no deploy key and no rollback of a stored URL.
- **SC-202** — ~~Linking an existing repo to a project rolls back the stored URL if deploy-key provisioning fails.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a project's repository is attached to its storage by connecting a repository (**SC-240**, **SC-241**); there is no per-project repository record, no deploy key and no rollback of a stored URL.
- **SC-203** — ~~Linking a repo to a project is rejected when the project already has a repo (400) or GitHub is not configured (422).~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a project's repository is attached to its storage by connecting a repository (**SC-240**, **SC-241**); there is no per-project repository record, no deploy key and no rollback of a stored URL.
- **SC-204** — ~~Unlinking a project repo when none is linked is rejected (400).~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a project's repository is attached to its storage by connecting a repository (**SC-240**, **SC-241**); there is no per-project repository record, no deploy key and no rollback of a stored URL.
- **SC-205** — Non-admins cannot manage members: `getOrgPeople` and `updateUserRole` return 403, and Organisation › People shows "Only an organisation admin manages the organisation's people." · fulfils AC-122c
- **SC-206** — Listing or previewing voices fails clearly when no text-to-speech provider works. *(Changed 2026-10-23: it answers 503 with the outage message — **SC-1206**.)* · fulfils AC-127b
- **SC-207** — A mobile pairing code is single-use, valid for 5 minutes, shown as a QR + copyable link with a countdown, and regenerable when expired. · fulfils AC-130b
- **SC-208** — Exchanging a mobile pairing code is rejected with the matching reason: 404 invalid, 400 already used, 400 expired. · fulfils AC-131b
- **SC-209** — ~~Once an MCP key exists, the summary query returns only its prefix and creation time, never the full key.~~ **Retired 2026-10-05** — there is no org-wide key; a connection's list shows its name, prefix, permissions, projects, last use and expiry and never its token (see **SC-478**).
- **SC-210** — Proposing a workforce template via MCP with an unknown id returns `{ success: false, error: "Template '…' not found" }`. · fulfils AC-135c
- **SC-211** — Starting an HQ connection rejects a non-http(s) URL (400) and clears any existing connection before creating the new one. · fulfils AC-136b *(Removed 2026-08-11 — the browser-approval link flow was superseded by Execution Site registration; see ADR-0044.)*
- **SC-212** — HQ provision-user and create-session requests are rejected (401 "Invalid connection token") when the bearer does not match the stored connection token. · fulfils AC-142c *(Removed 2026-08-11 — the browser-approval link flow was superseded by Execution Site registration; see ADR-0044.)*

## Public API

- **SC-110** — `/health` returns 200 with `{"status":"ok"}` and nothing else — no version, commit or build time (adr-0106, **SC-723**).
- **SC-111** — `POST /mcp` serves the tool catalogue to authorized MCP clients. · fulfils AC-134a
- **SC-112** — `/api/auth/bootstrap-status` reports whether first-admin bootstrap is required (no users yet). · fulfils AC-009a
- **SC-113** — `/api/auth/validate-invite` returns metadata for a valid token; distinct errors for invalid / revoked / used / expired; IP rate-limited. The validation answers who invited the person, the role, note, projects and the organisation as shown before sign-in, and an expired, used or withdrawn invitation answers 410 naming who to ask (**SC-718**). · fulfils AC-007a, AC-007b, AC-007c
- **SC-114** — `/api/auth/accept-invite` consumes a token, creates the user with the invite's role, and signs them in. · fulfils AC-008a, AC-008c, AC-008d

## Internal runtime (unit-tested)

> Internal units below the user-flow layer. Promoted from orphan tests F-420..F-457 (coverage audit).
> These assert runtime behaviour, not user scenarios; covered by Vitest unit tests.

### Agent runtime
- **SC-115** — TaskRunner: atomic claim of an agent's one slot per project (two claims racing for the same agent and project cannot both win), trigger-next, end-of-run settlement (none, failed, needs you), deferred-tool manifest approval.
- **SC-116** — dispatchChatRun: builds the chat context (session context) and the run's package content, named by digest, honours the session's model and a boundary's model override, mints the run's capability, and dispatches to the site that holds the session's storage.
- **SC-117** — ~~HeartbeatManager: one heartbeat per enabled agent, dedupe by id, stopAll, pulse delegation.~~ **Retired 2026-10-05** — there is no heartbeat manager; the scheduler that replaced it is **SC-454**.
- **SC-118** — DockerSpawner: builds `docker exec -i -w <cwd>` spawn fn; strips app env (only PATH passed); no secret leakage into the container.
- **SC-119** — ClaudeSDKRuntime: maps SDK stream events to text_delta/tool_call/tool_result/status; resume vs new-session selection; workspace-scoped cwd + skills tmpdir; injects docker spawner when containerName is set.
- **SC-120** — ~~NativeRuntime: streams text deltas via the Vercel AI SDK; emits completed/failed status with model + error~~ **Retired 2026-09-30** — NativeRuntime was deleted and OpenCode replaced it (adr-0073); the OpenCode runtime is covered by **SC-449**.
- **SC-121** — AgentContainerManager: ensureRunning (inspect/rm/run), concurrent-start dedupe, the run's credential written into the boundary's home before every run, one container per storage location and mode, orphan removal by owner label and by name prefix; no secrets in `docker run` arguments.
- **SC-122** — manifestParser: manifestNeedsApproval; merge base + extended tools/bash patterns into the approved manifest.

### Prompt builders
- **SC-123** — AgentPromptBuilder (site): assembles identity, the agent's instructions (its one `instructions.md`), a Memory section that names the memory tools and carries no item, since memory is recalled and never sent whole (**SC-992**), and an agenda section; chat and task variants; editable-section ordering; the instructions section is left out when the agent has none; a run's prompt states the date and time in the timezone of the person it is for (see **SC-482**).
- **SC-124** — ~~AgentPromptBuilder voice mode: appends spoken-rhythm / no-markdown instruction block only when isVoiceSession.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the site no longer has a voice mode of its prompt: the spoken register is the conversation's own, written by hq (**SC-996**).

### Voice
- **SC-125** — ~~VoiceRunner: drives chat in voice mode, accumulates sentences, synthesises each via TTS, onAudioChunk/onAudioEnd, abort handling, listener cleanup on error.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the voice runner is gone; a spoken turn is an ordinary turn whose reply hq speaks sentence by sentence (**SC-995**).
- **SC-126** — ~~Voice socket handler: session_start requires an active TTS provider; turn flow emits tts:turn_start → tts:chunk → tts:turn_end.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the voice socket handler is gone; the conversation's voice events are **SC-994** to **SC-1000**.
- **SC-127** — ~~TurnDetector: trigger-word vs silence mode; clean-transcript extraction; max-silence / silence-threshold end conditions.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — turn detection by trigger word or silence threshold is gone; a message is sent when the person pauses, by the speech service's own end of turn (**SC-995**).
- **SC-128** — ~~SentenceAccumulator: emits complete sentences on boundary, buffers partials, flush, ignores lone punctuation.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the sentence accumulator is gone; speech units are cut from the reply's stream (**SC-995**).
- **SC-129** — InworldTtsAdapter: synthesize (base64→Buffer), synthesizeStream with word timings + abort, listVoices mapping, error propagation.

### HQ sync
- **SC-130** — ~~deltaSync.runDelta: cursor vs cold-start queries; emits hq_index.push upserts (projects/tasks) and deletes (tombstones); advances cursor to latest mutation.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the index-sync frames (`hq_index.push`) were removed with the pre-split link to a central service; nothing in either app sends them.
- **SC-131** — ~~hqPush: emits hq_index.push with live siteTotal counts on entity create/delete; no-op without an open socket; updates lastPushAt.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the index-sync frames (`hq_index.push`) were removed with the pre-split link to a central service; nothing in either app sends them.
- **SC-132** — HqConnectionManager.sendDetach: sends `site.detach` only when the socket is open and the site id is set; a safe no-op, swallowing send errors, otherwise.
- **SC-133** — Site connection status: the canonical set (LINKING, PENDING, ACTIVE, DEGRADED, VERSION_MISMATCH, REVOKED) with a case-sensitive type guard.

### MCP
- **SC-134** — MCP tool handlers: task fetch/details, task create/update patch semantics, agent comments, file attach; an agent has no tool for a task's run state, so there is no `update_task_state`. (catalogue exposure: see `/api/mcp` bullet under Public API)

### Dashboard
- **SC-135** — ~~Dashboard socket subscriptions: add/remove subscriber, emit snapshot to all, hasSubscribers.~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — the dashboard page that subscribed to this push is gone (**SC-012**, **SC-149**); no client subscribes, and Today is its own query (**SC-668**).
- **SC-136** — ~~composeSnapshot: totals, run rollups (completed/failed/running), financial token + cost estimate, top agents with active-session counts, recent projects, task counts.~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — the dashboard page that subscribed to this push is gone (**SC-012**, **SC-149**); no client subscribes, and Today is its own query (**SC-668**).
- **SC-137** — ~~Dashboard debounced push: coalesces rapid triggers into one emit; skips compose+emit when there are no subscribers.~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — the dashboard page that subscribed to this push is gone (**SC-012**, **SC-149**); no client subscribes, and Today is its own query (**SC-668**).

### Misc
- **SC-138** — kernelGuards: kernel agent is protected — reserved handle, cannot modify/delete, protected-field guard allows only defaultModel changes.
- **SC-139** — WorkspaceService: workspace/project/product path derivation; path-traversal containment (validatePath rejects `..`/absolute escapes); virtual-path mapping.
- **SC-140** — Session naming (on the site): generateSessionTitle derives a title from the first 6 words of the first message (an ellipsis when longer), persists it, returns null for blank input; hq's session copy takes the title from the site's frame (**SC-345**).
- **SC-141** — ~~wikiMirrorService: product wiki mirror path derivation (host vs container-isolation); readWikiFile/listWikiFiles with traversal blocking and dotfile skipping.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the product wiki mirror was never built and is gone (**SC-253**).
- **SC-142** — ~~File-serving handlers: an artefact (`/files/artefact/<ownerType>/<ownerId>/…`) is served only to a person entitled to that owner, and a task attachment (`/files/task/<taskId>/…`) only to a member of the task's project; both need a signed-in session (401), refuse a traversal — lexical or through a link — with 404, and keep the read inside the owner's `artefacts/` or the task's own uploads.~~ **Retired 2026-10-27** — hq-server serves no file bytes: an upload opens through hq's relay on a ticket hq signs after the same access check (**SC-1518**, **SC-1520**, **SC-1519**), and an artefact through its site (**SC-1513**).
- **SC-143** — ~~RightSidebar panel state: toggleChat cycles null→side→full→null; panelMode preserved across panel switches; openChat keeps mode; close resets to side.~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — there is no right panel and no chat panel (**SC-011**); a conversation is its own page.

### Misc (added 2026-06-21 — F-018 NO-SC-MATCH tests)
- **SC-216** — agendaService (site): derives the path of a person's agenda file in a storage location and says whether one is there (`getAgendaFilePath` / `hasAgendaIn`).
- **SC-217** — agentUtils: `resolveAgentContext` guards agent/session existence; `shapeAgentReply` suppresses NO_REPLY and trims/blank-handles replies.
- **SC-218** — ~~VoiceCallProvider: `startCall` opens the given session's conversation page (`/sessions/<id>`).~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the call screen is gone; voice is a mode of the conversation and belongs to the whole app (**SC-994**, **SC-1001**).

> Re-mapped to existing bullets (no new entry): `riskRating` → risky tool-call approval + risk-rule auto-rejection + org risk rules; `mcpTools`/`mcpServer` → `/api/mcp` catalogue (Public API); `identifiers` (`deriveProjectKey`/`deriveSlug`) → project-key contract under Projects + boards.

## Foundations

- `data-testid` attributes on key components (login form, drawer, task list, kanban lanes) where a role or text query is not enough.
- A dedicated test database, `agenthix_test` for hq (see the strategy, Test data), with a seeded account per role for the browser suites.

## Responsive (mobile web)

- **SC-219** — On a phone-width viewport (360–414px), the authenticated HQ app renders in a single column with no horizontal overflow. · fulfils AC-146a
- **SC-220** — Below the `lg` breakpoint, the left navigation is hidden and opens as an off-canvas drawer via a hamburger control; choosing a destination navigates and closes the drawer. · fulfils AC-146b
- **SC-221** — Below `lg`, Notifications and Runs sit at the top right of the phone header, and their panels fill the screen, closed from their own header (‹ Notifications, ‹ Runs). · fulfils AC-146c · tests `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/MobileHeader.test.tsx`, `ShellPanels.test.tsx`
- **SC-222** — ~~Below `lg`, table list views render as stacked cards (label:value rows); at `lg` and above the same views render as tables, unchanged. · fulfils AC-146d~~ **Retired 2026-11-11** — its only component, `ResponsiveRows`, rendered nothing in hq and was removed at the close of the unit *a softer, more polished design system*; a list's rows take one shape by the list's width instead.
- **SC-223** — On a phone (375px) the operator can complete the core remote loop: open chat, message an agent, open a board, and open a task. · fulfils AC-146e
- **SC-224** — At `lg` and above, the desktop shell renders the full layout — the navigation inline (or the place's sections, **SC-1021**), the screen bar above the screen, a panel opening beside the navigation over the content — with no top bar, and nothing on the right but the open conversation (**SC-1026**, **SC-1038**); the phone header and drawer do not apply. *(Changed 2026-10-18: the docked conversation.)* · fulfils AC-146f · tests `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/AppLayout.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/mobile-responsive.spec.ts`
- **SC-225** — The Notifications and Runs buttons are beside the logo in the navigation on a wide screen and at the top right of the phone header on a narrow one; there is no top bar, and the only rail on the right is the open conversation put away (**SC-1033**). *(Changed 2026-10-18: the docked conversation.)* · fulfils AC-146g · tests `hq/e2e/mobile-responsive.spec.ts`

## Remote operations — live preview (Phase 8)

*Retired on 2026-11-02 with the old preview path: live previews on branches replace it, under "Live preview" below.*

- **SC-226** — ~~the preview proxy never resolves an upstream address: it forwards over a stream the site holding the preview opened outward, whose far end connects to the preview's port inside the container; a `PreviewSession` records the site and the preview's id, with no host port~~ **Retired 2026-11-02** — the preview proxy and `PreviewSession` are gone: a live app is carried by the site's relay over the site's line, entered with a single-use ticket hq signs (**SC-1705**, **SC-1749**, **SC-1750**).
- **SC-227** — ~~starting a preview persists a `PreviewSession` with an unguessable token and serves lookups from an in-memory cache, falling back to the database on a miss~~ **Retired 2026-11-02** — `PreviewSession` and its tokens are gone: a live preview is hq's `LivePreview` row and an app's address comes from a random id kept across restarts (**SC-1694**, **SC-1707**).
- **SC-228** — ~~a preview is a process run inside the existing container of its storage boundary — the one held for the runner the session runs for — never a second container, and publishes no port; with no running container there it is refused with the reason~~ **Retired 2026-11-02** — a live app no longer runs in the boundary's container: it runs in the live preview's own container, which binds only the branch's copy (**SC-1733**, **SC-1734**).
- **SC-229** — ~~`start_preview` starts the preview process in that container, waits for the dev server to become ready, and returns the preview URL; it errors if the server never becomes ready~~ **Retired 2026-11-02** — `start_preview` is gone: the agent starts an app with `start_app` in its branch's live preview (**SC-1701**, **SC-1702**, **SC-1716**).
- **SC-230** — ~~a preview is served only from its own host, `p-<id>.<preview domain>`, never from hq's own origin, and each request is authorized against the person viewing, not merely the address (adr-0071): with no preview cookie a page request is redirected to hq's access check, which signs the person in if needed and returns them to it, and mints a one-time ticket only for a person entitled to the preview's storage boundary (403 otherwise); the preview host exchanges the ticket once, within a minute, for a cookie bound to that host; every request and WebSocket upgrade verifies that cookie and re-runs the requester check (403 for another preview's or an altered cookie, or a person no longer entitled · 404 unknown · 410 ended · pass for an entitled person, relayed at the host's root with the preview cookie removed); a deployed install with no preview domain has previews off~~ **Retired 2026-11-02** — previews are no longer served through hq's access check: a live app is entered at the relay with a single-use ticket and a short host-only cookie, and hq's word on the ticket is asked on every new stream (**SC-1705**, **SC-1706**, **SC-1749**, **SC-1750**).
- **SC-231** — ~~previews past their expiry are stopped by hq's sweep — the site stops the process and leaves the container — and marked expired once the site confirms the stop; until then the preview answers as ended~~ **Retired 2026-11-02** — there is no preview expiry sweep: a live preview stops when unused for its site's idle time (**SC-1699**).
- **SC-232** — ~~the operator-facing preview URL is environment-aware: with a published host port (dev) it is a direct-port URL on the host the site advertises (`localhost`, or the mDNS/LAN host in LAN mode, inherited from `WASP_SERVER_URL`); with no host port (containerized) it falls back to the proxy path on the site origin~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — a preview has no host port and is served only from its own host, `p-<id>.<preview domain>` (**SC-230**).

## Remote operations — GitHub Actions (Phase 8)

- **SC-233** — the project Operations section lists the repo's GitHub Actions workflows and recent runs (name, run number, short sha, status/conclusion) read live from the Actions API, each linking to the run on GitHub *(Changed 2026-10-25: a connected workspace's About lists each GitHub workflow with its last run — **SC-1321**)* · fulfils AC-148a
- **SC-234** — ~~the operator can trigger a `workflow_dispatch` run for a workflow from Agenthix, on the repo's default branch~~ **Retired 2026-10-25** — there is no Run button for a GitHub workflow; one is started by an approved workflow with `actions: write`, or on GitHub (**SC-1321**). · fulfils AC-148b
- **SC-235** — ~~setting a secret seals the value against the repo's public key (libsodium sealed box) and writes it to GitHub Secrets via the API; Agenthix persists no value~~ **Retired 2026-10-25** — a secret is kept in the place's Secrets and reaches GitHub only as an environment secret, through the approved `set-github-secrets` workflow (**SC-1324**, **SC-1325**). · fulfils AC-148c
- **SC-236** — the Operations section shows which secrets are set on the repo (name + updated-at) without exposing any value — GitHub Secrets are write-only and never returned *(Changed 2026-10-25: each also says whether it came from Secrets or was set outside HQ — **SC-1322**)* · fulfils AC-148d

## Out of scope

- Real OAuth provider integration (use stubs).
- Payment provider integration (no live Stripe calls).
- Push notification delivery.
- Tunneling / NAT scenarios.

## Files and Workspaces (ADR-0039)

- **SC-237** — `locationDir` derives a nested, id-keyed storage path (`{ownerType}s/{ownerId}/files` or `…/workspaces/{id}`); names are display labels only and path-traversal is contained · fulfils AC-149b
- **SC-238** — creating a Workspace inserts a DB row and makes its nested directory; the owner is immutable; there is no `isDefault` · fulfils AC-149b
- **SC-239** — an owner's `files/` directory is provisioned (with the owner / lazily) and has **no** Workspace record — it is the owner's intrinsic dir · fulfils AC-149a
- **SC-240** — connecting a repo to a workspace **or** an owner's files creates a `RepoAttachment` — git reaches the repository with the GitHub App's short-lived tokens (**SC-257**), with no deploy key — and lands `.git` in the target dir (init-push when the dir has content, link-pull into an empty dir) *(Changed 2026-10-25: one connect flow checks before it creates, and an existing repository is taken only when empty or sharing the files' history — **SC-1315** to **SC-1317**, **SC-1358**)* · fulfils AC-149c
- **SC-241** — disconnecting a repo deletes the `RepoAttachment`; the directory and its files remain · fulfils AC-149c
- **SC-242** — Operations (workflows, runs, secrets, dispatch) resolve the repo via its `RepoAttachment` · fulfils AC-149c
- **SC-243** — at most one repo per workspace and one per owner's files (uniqueness enforced) · fulfils AC-149c
- **SC-244** — a session resolves a primary **location** (`ownerType`/`ownerId`/optional `workspaceId`); the run's cwd is that nested dir and it is mounted into the container · fulfils AC-149e
- **SC-245** — a general (no-project) chat resolves the operator's `users/{id}/files/` as its primary location, provisioned lazily · fulfils AC-149e
- **SC-246** — ~~session scratch is a subdirectory of the primary location; the agent builds there by default and building creates no Workspace~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — an agent has no working directory of its own and there is no per-session scratch inside a location; a run's working directory is its primary location, and a process ends with the conversation that started it — see **SC-450**.
- **SC-247** — ~~the **Storage** page lists every reachable storage location grouped by owner — each owner's files pinned first, then its workspaces — and is searchable~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — there is no Storage page; workspaces across places are **Workspaces** in the navigation (**SC-657**) · fulfils AC-149d
- **SC-248** — ~~a workspace has its own root page (`/workspaces/:id`) with Overview / Code / GitHub / Settings; the owner is shown as a badge (flat breadcrumb, permanent ownership)~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — a workspace's address opens its place's Workspaces tool with the workspace open (**SC-659**) · fulfils AC-149b
- **SC-249** — a container's **Files** tool (a project's or a person's own, in a place's Files section) renders the explorer inline with a **Full view** affordance that opens a deep-linkable route · fulfils AC-149a
- **SC-250** — the explorer renders a file tree and minimal markdown/code content (stub); other types offer download · fulfils AC-149a
- **SC-251** — New-workspace infers the owner from context (a project's Workspaces tool → project-owned; Chat's → user-owned); New workspace on the Workspaces page asks for a place first (**SC-658**) · fulfils AC-149b
- **SC-252** — ownership is permanent — no promotion / owner-flip actions or agent tools exist · fulfils AC-149e
- **SC-253** — the wiki is gone — a product's docs are its `files/`; `Product.wikiRepoUrl` / `ProductSshKey` are removed · fulfils AC-149a
- **SC-254** — the default-Workspace concept is gone — no `isDefault`, no auto-created/unremovable Workspace; the container's `files/` is its home · fulfils AC-149a
- **SC-255** — hq's schema has no `Project.repoUrl`, `Product.wikiRepoUrl` or their SSH-key models, and no `Workspace` or `RepoAttachment` either: repositories attach to storage through those two models in site's schema · fulfils AC-149b
- **SC-265** — ~~User selects a file in the Files explorer and its content renders by type: markdown rendered, code syntax-highlighted, image shown.~~ **Retired 2026-11-04** — the file content service its tests lived in was removed; ui's one frame and its renderers draw every file by kind — see **SC-1578** to **SC-1590**.
- **SC-266** — ~~Reading a file is path-safe (rejects `..`/absolute paths) and non-previewable files (binary, or over the size cap) fall through to a "can't preview" notice.~~ **Retired 2026-11-04** — hq reads no file's bytes now; the open action and the frame's states (a kind with no renderer, past the cap, not text) are **SC-1578** to **SC-1590**.
- **SC-267** — With a file open, an Expand control opens a full-bleed reader overlay rendering the file in full view (dismissible); width is keyed to kind — prose capped to a reading measure, code scrolls wide, image fits the viewport with an actual-size toggle. · fulfils AC-151a

## Integrations — GitHub

- **SC-256** — Operator registers GitHub via the App Manifest flow; credentials are stored, the App private key encrypted at rest. · fulfils AC-150a
- **SC-257** — ~~The accessor resolves a repo → installation and mints an installation token scoped to that one repo (~1 hr).~~ **Retired 2026-10-25** — every installation token goes through the mint gate, which takes the repository from the workspace's own connection by GitHub's ids and names the permissions each time (**SC-1305**, **SC-1306**). · fulfils AC-150b
- **SC-258** — Operations (workflows/runs/secrets/dispatch) resolve their credential through the App accessor — no PAT. *(Changed 2026-10-25: through the mint gate, for the target's own connection, with only the permissions asked for, the token ended afterwards, also when the operation fails — **SC-1306**)* · fulfils AC-150c
- **SC-259** — Server-side git (connect / push-on-approval) authenticates with an ephemeral App token; nothing persisted in `.git/config`. *(Changed 2026-10-25: there is no push-on-approval; server-side git is connecting a repository, its token from the mint gate — **SC-1316**, **SC-1317**)* · fulfils AC-150d
- **SC-260** — The App private key round-trips through encryption; plaintext is never stored. · fulfils AC-150a
- **SC-261** — ~~An agent run's in-container git obtains a repo-scoped token via the credential-helper callback; the container holds no durable GitHub credential.~~ **Retired 2026-10-25** — the agent's container gets no GitHub token: its only credential helper tells git to stop and names the `push` workflow (**SC-1354**). · fulfils AC-150e
- **SC-262** — User can disconnect the GitHub App (platform forgets the credential); refreshing installations prunes any GitHub no longer reports. · fulfils AC-150a
- **SC-263** — ~~The git-credential endpoint mints a repo-scoped token for an authorized agent+repo (valid run token, agent has owner access, repo attached).~~ **Retired 2026-10-25** — the git credential endpoint and hq's `resolve_git_credential` are removed (**SC-1354**). · fulfils AC-150e
- **SC-264** — ~~The git-credential endpoint denies: missing or unknown run token → 401; no attachment for the repo → 403; agent lacks owner access → 403.~~ **Retired 2026-10-25** — the git credential endpoint and hq's `resolve_git_credential` are removed (**SC-1354**). · fulfils AC-150e
- **SC-268** — ~~The agent-operations deploy loop runs end-to-end: a deploy workflow + app is pushed to the repo; the operator relays the deploy secret (VPS SSH key) to GitHub Secrets and dispatches the workflow from the Operations panel; the GitHub Actions run deploys the app to the VPS (Traefik-routed at a smoke subdomain, isolated from production) and the run's status is visible in the panel via auto-poll.~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — it described a one-off end-to-end check against the maintainers' own test server, not a behaviour of the product; dispatching a workflow and reading its runs are **SC-233** and **SC-234**. · fulfils AC-150c

## Identity & workspace access (hq/site split §2, §2a, §2b; execution-runtime design §H)

- **SC-269** — hq generates a signing keypair on first boot (private key encrypted at rest) and serves the public key at `/.well-known/jwks.json` with a stable key id. · fulfils AC-154a
- **SC-270** — A logged-in user can mint a short-lived (60 min) hq-signed identity token carrying user id, role, and org; it verifies against the published keys. · fulfils AC-154a
- **SC-271** — The site module verifies hq-issued tokens locally against hq's published keys — no per-request call to hq; expired, tampered, or wrong-issuer tokens are rejected. · fulfils AC-154a
- **SC-272** — Every agent run starts by minting a signed capability (user, runner, agent, project, workspace, task, expiry, pre-resolved tool permissions); the run's permission context is built from the verified capability, not a parallel lookup. · fulfils AC-154a
- **SC-273** — A capability is renewed at 80% of its 15-minute TTL without interrupting the run; renewal re-resolves permissions and policy fresh. · fulfils AC-154b
- **SC-274** — When renewal fails (hq unreachable), the current capability is honoured until its actual expiry while renewal keeps retrying. · fulfils AC-154c
- **SC-275** — ~~Blocking agent access on a workspace's page prevents any agent run from starting there — the run fails with an explicit policy error.~~ **Retired 2026-09-28** — the workspace access policy is removed (ADR-0079); access to a boundary is access to all of it, and a workspace is guarded by its tool settings (**SC-301**).
- **SC-276** — ~~The workspace block is checked before — and cannot be overridden by — the agent's own allowedTools/allowedBashPatterns/autonomy.~~ **Retired 2026-09-28** — the workspace access policy is removed (ADR-0079).
- **SC-277** — ~~A task run's container mounts exclude any project workspace that has a block policy.~~ **Retired 2026-09-28** — a container binds its boundary's folder once, every workspace included (ADR-0079).
- **SC-278** — A person's conversation — its messages, its activity, its page and its live room — is readable only by the person who opened it, and so is a chat session of theirs (a conversation's standing worker) and its transcript; any other person — an ADMIN included — gets a not-found that is the same as for one that does not exist, and subscribing to its room gives them none of its events. *(Changed 2026-10-18: written for the conversations held in hq, which replaced the chats.)* · fulfils AC-153a
- **SC-279** — ~~A free-running heartbeat session (no task) and its transcript are readable by any signed-in person, as org-wide activity; a chat session is never one (see **SC-278**), and a task session is not (see **SC-281**).~~ **Retired 2026-10-05** — no run is free-running any more: every session is a chat or a task's, and each is readable under **SC-278** or **SC-281**.
- **SC-280** — No listing shows another person's conversation or its runs: a place's conversations and its archived ones, recent activity, an agent's detail and an agent's page leave them out, and the run feeds leave out another person's chat-session runs. *(Changed 2026-10-18: written for the conversations held in hq, which replaced the chats.)* · fulfils AC-153c
- **SC-281** — A task session and its transcript are readable only by members of the task's project: a person outside a private project gets a not-found for its transcript, runs, tool-call log, pending manifest and live session room.
- **SC-282** — The Execution Sites page (Organisation › Infrastructure › Execution Sites, organisation admins only) lists every registered site with its label, registration date, and connection state. · fulfils AC-155a
- **SC-283** — ~~Generating a registration token shows the token once, with its expiry; it is not retrievable afterwards.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a site registers by device approval (ADR-0064); hq no longer issues a registration token from any screen — see **SC-420**.
- **SC-284** — ~~A site redeeming a valid token is recorded in the registry and receives a machine credential; the token cannot be redeemed a second time.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a site registers by device approval (ADR-0064), not by redeeming a token — see **SC-420**. The token-redemption endpoint was removed (ADR-0115).
- **SC-285** — ~~Redemption with an unknown, expired, or already-redeemed token is refused with the same error in all three cases.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — refusal of a wrong code is now stated for the device-approval flow — see **SC-420**.
- **SC-286** — Revoking a site causes its machine credential to stop authenticating. · fulfils AC-155e
- **SC-287** — Creating a project without choosing an Execution Site places it on HQ's own built-in site; the created project records the site it was placed on. · fulfils AC-155f
- **SC-288** — ~~A user's first project-less chat picks the site that holds their personal files — silently when exactly one site is registered, by prompt when more than one is.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — a person's first project-less chat never prompts for a site: their storage is placed once, at the default site (**SC-317**, **SC-318**).
- **SC-289** — A token minted for one Execution Site is refused by another. · fulfils AC-155h
- **SC-290** — An Execution Site's connection is accepted only when it presents the machine credential it was issued; a wrong or revoked credential is refused. · fulfils AC-156a
- **SC-291** — A site states its protocol version in `X-Site-Protocol-Version` when it dials, and hq applies the one compatibility rule (**SC-1127**): a site at hq's major is accepted at the lower of the two minors, one major behind at the site's own version, and one major ahead at hq's, the site adapting; a site two or more majors apart, or one that states no version or one that cannot be read, is refused with 426 stating hq's own version in the same header, recorded against the authenticated site with what it presented, and logged naming the side to upgrade. Every frame hq sends on a connection carries the version it speaks there, and a frame two or more majors apart, or with no readable version, is dropped. A site so refused records the mismatch with the words of which side must upgrade, keeps its credential, and is shown in hq as needing an upgrade of the site or of HQ, never as revoked or offline. · fulfils AC-156b, AC-348a · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteUpgrade.test.ts`, `hq/src/sites/channel/__tests__/siteChannelServer.test.ts`, `site/src/server/hq/__tests__/versionMismatch.test.ts`, `core/src/protocol/__tests__/connection.test.ts`
- **SC-292** — A connected site is shown as online, and its last-seen time advances while it stays connected. · fulfils AC-156c
- **SC-293** — When the connection drops the site is shown as offline; a site that has never connected is still shown as registered, not offline. · fulfils AC-156c
- **SC-294** — hq restarting shows every previously-online site as offline, since no connection survives a restart. · fulfils AC-156c
- **SC-295** — hq can ask a connected site for its details over the existing connection and receive its answer; a question the site does not recognise is answered with an explicit error. · fulfils AC-156d
- **SC-296** — A request to a site that is not connected, or one in flight when the connection drops, fails immediately with a stated reason instead of hanging. · fulfils AC-156e
- **SC-297** — A second connection from the same site replaces the first, and the displaced connection does not report the site offline. · fulfils AC-156c
- **SC-298** — A capability minted for a chat run carries the invoking person's role; a capability minted for a task run carries no role. · fulfils AC-157a, AC-157b
- **SC-299** — MCP tools that mutate the organisation itself (org, teams, products, projects, agents) refuse a capability with no role, and enforce each tool's minimum role when one is present. · fulfils AC-157a, AC-157b
- **SC-300** — `archive_task` and `delete_task` refuse tasks outside the capability's project on task-minted capabilities, and require the run's person to be a Member or Admin of the task's project on chat-minted ones. · fulfils AC-157a, AC-157e
- **SC-301** — A workspace's allowed-tools list narrows the toolset of a run working in that workspace — after the agent's own permissions and the project's override — and of no other run: a run working in another workspace, the project's files or a task folder is not narrowed by it. An unconfigured workspace narrows nothing; an empty list yields an empty effective toolset; a workspace that cannot be read refuses the mint. · fulfils AC-157c
- **SC-302** — Agent runs authenticate to the MCP endpoint with a run-scoped token that resolves to the run's live capability and dies with the run; agent containers hold only that token, never a permanent org-wide credential. · fulfils AC-157d
- **SC-303** — The MCP handler derives tool filtering and agent identity from verified capability claims rather than a self-asserted handle header. · fulfils AC-157d
- **SC-304** — `createProject` lets every organisation Member make a project, in the browser and over MCP alike, and refuses a person with no organisation role; naming its billing profile, product or programme is refused to anyone but an organisation Admin. *(Changed 2026-11-06: members make organisation projects.)* · fulfils AC-157a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectCreate.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/mcp/__tests__/mcpTools.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/rolePolicy.test.ts`
- **SC-305** — `complete_org_setup`, `create_product` and `update_product` are off by default and must be granted explicitly; the install bootstrap deliberately grants `complete_org_setup` to the kernel agent. · fulfils AC-157b
- **SC-306** — MCP tool data operations run through the control-plane service layer, which enforces role floors and project scope itself; no gate lives only in the MCP wiring. · fulfils AC-158a
- **SC-307** — The data endpoint refuses calls without a valid machine credential, and refuses a capability whose audience names a different site. · fulfils AC-158b
- **SC-308** — Task-minted runs are confined to their capability's project across the whole tool surface — task reads and writes, boards, comments, attachments and `run_task` alike. · fulfils AC-158c
- **SC-309** — The acting agent comes from verified claims, never from a tool argument: queue fetches and comment authorship are pinned to the capability's agent. · fulfils AC-158c
- **SC-310** — Browser actions carry the same role floors as their MCP twins: org and team mutation ADMIN, agent creation and presets ADMIN, products ADMIN, project creation MEMBER; task archive and delete need a Member or Admin of the task's project. · fulfils AC-158d
- **SC-311** — A workspace's allowed-tools list is editable by someone who works where the workspace is (a Member or Admin of its project, or its own person), validated server-side — `null` clears the cap, a string list caps, and any other shape (including the legacy per-tool object form) is refused. · fulfils AC-158e
- **SC-312** — The capability-renewal endpoint authenticates the calling site by machine credential and verifies the capability's audience is that site. · fulfils AC-158b
- **SC-313** — A missing user-settings row never grants elevated authority: identity tokens default to the MEMBER role, the least organisation role. · fulfils AC-158d
- **SC-314** — Every workspace carries its Execution Site, set when it is created: a project's workspaces on the project's site, a person's on the site holding their storage. · fulfils AC-159a
- **SC-315** — ~~Adding an agent to a project anchored to a different Execution Site is refused with an explained error; the same-site case succeeds.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — hq no longer refuses an agent on a project whose site differs; a run is placed on the site that holds its storage (ADR-0056).
- **SC-316** — ~~Assigning a task to an agent whose site differs from the project's — at creation or by update — is refused with an explained error.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — hq no longer refuses an assignment across sites; a run is placed on the site that holds its storage (ADR-0056).
- **SC-317** — ~~Starting a personal session never prompts for a site choice: a person's storage is placed once, at the default site, and later sessions follow it; with no Execution Site connected the person is told so and nothing is written. · fulfils AC-159c~~ **Retired 2026-10-18** — a personal chat session is no longer started. (Criterion AC-159c is served by **SC-1042** since then.)
- **SC-1042** — A person never chooses a site for their own storage: the first time a conversation's worker is made in the person's own space, their storage is placed on the default site without asking them, and the worker is made on that site; later work in their space follows the placement, which stays where it is when another site becomes the default. · fulfils AC-159c · tests `hq/src/conversations/work/workers/__tests__/workerPlacement.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/storage/__tests__/storageSite.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-318** — The capability's site is the site that holds the run's storage: a project's site, a workspace's recorded placement, or a person's placement; `UserSettings` carries no home-site field. · fulfils AC-159d
- **SC-319** — ~~Deleting a workspace removes its access-policy rows, so a blocked state cannot outlive the workspace it blocked.~~ **Retired 2026-09-28** — there are no access-policy rows (ADR-0079).
- **SC-320** — Workspace and repo-attachment rows live in the owning site's database; hq reads them over the connected channel and holds no copy of their content. · fulfils AC-159e
- **SC-321** — hq's placement index records each workspace's site at creation and forgets it at deletion; hq is its only writer. · fulfils AC-159e
- **SC-322** — With the owning site's channel down, workspace reads fail with an explicit error naming the site, and the mint of a run working in a workspace refuses rather than minting without that workspace's tool list. · fulfils AC-159f
- **SC-323** — ~~The one-time re-key adopts the registered site app as the local site: every placement reference leaves the hq placeholder, idempotently, in both id spaces (registry ids and logical site ids).~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — the one-time re-key no longer exists; hq's default site is its configured, paired site and adopts the site's id at its first connection (**SC-356**).
- **SC-324** — Site's own database harness proves its storage models against a real database, including the workspace–repo-attachment delete cascade. · fulfils AC-159e
- **SC-325** — A container belongs to one storage location — one person's or one project's — for its whole life and is never rebound: two sessions in the same boundary share one container, a run for another owner gets a different one, and a boundary holds at most two (read-write and read-only). It is named `<prefix>-user-<personId>-<mode>` for a person's own storage, and `<prefix>-project-<projectId>-<runner>-<mode>` for a project, where the runner is `user-<personId>` or `project` (ADR-0074). · fulfils AC-160a
- **SC-326** — Container context rides in labels, never in the name: the agent, session, invoker, task and the owning boundary are searchable by Docker filters; an unattended run's container carries no invoker or task label. · fulfils AC-160a
- **SC-327** — A boundary's container idle past the teardown window is stopped, and never while a run is active or a start is in flight; the next run in that boundary recreates it and the conversation resumes unbroken. · fulfils AC-160a
- **SC-328** — Agent containers run with memory and process-count limits applied. · fulfils AC-160a
- **SC-329** — ~~A chat run mounts exactly the invoking person's memory partition at `/memory`; another person's chat with the same agent sees only their own.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — what an agent knows about a person is held by hq and delivered in the run's context, not mounted from a site's disk (ADR-0058) — see **SC-426** and **SC-427**.
- **SC-330** — ~~Task and heartbeat runs mount no `/memory` at all.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — what an agent knows about a person is held by hq and delivered in the run's context, not mounted from a site's disk (ADR-0058) — see **SC-426** and **SC-427**.
- **SC-331** — ~~Memory written under the old in-workspace convention is migrated into the per-person partitions once, idempotently, and reads back at `/memory`.~~ **Retired 2026-09-26** — what an agent knows about a person is held by hq and delivered in the run's context, not mounted from a site's disk (ADR-0058) — see **SC-426** and **SC-427**.
- **SC-332** — A run's working directory is its primary storage location; the agent has no working directory of its own, the prompt describes container paths, and no container path or prompt line contains the site's host root. · fulfils AC-160c
- **SC-333** — The skills a run's package carries are available to a containerized run: they are staged in the run's home, and removed by the run that staged them. · fulfils AC-160c
- **SC-446** — Each run records the fingerprint (digest) of the agent, organisation and platform content it ran with; a site fetches content it does not hold once, verifies it against that fingerprint and never alters it, reuses its cache while the content is unchanged, and fetches again after an edit; hq serves a run exactly the content it named at its start, and keeps content no package points at any more for a day before removing it, never removing current content; a run whose content is not in the site's cache fails rather than run any other content. · fulfils AC-170c
- **SC-447** — A skill placed once in a boundary's shared skills folder (`.agenthix/skills/<slug>/` in a project's files, or in a person's own space) reaches every agent whose run works in that boundary, as `shared-<slug>`, beside any one agent's own boundary skill of the same slug and replacing neither; and a folder that leads out of the boundary's files is refused. · fulfils AC-186a, AC-186b
- **SC-334** — When write tools fall out of a run's effective set, its work-location mounts are read-only and an in-container write fails — including when approval is bypassed. · fulfils AC-160d
- **SC-335** — A write-capable run keeps read-write location mounts for its files and workspaces; knowledge is never among them (**SC-1384**) and changes only through the knowledge tools. *(Changed 2026-10-26: the knowledge directory is no longer mounted into runs.)* · fulfils AC-160d
- **SC-336** — ~~A run mounts its owner's knowledge directory: a project's shared notes for a run in that project, a person's own notes for a run in their space; the prompt directs learnings there.~~ **Retired 2026-10-26** — no run mounts knowledge (**SC-1384**): a run searches and reads its place's, the organisation's and the platform guide's pages through the knowledge tools (**SC-1408**), and its prompt points it to `search_knowledge` (**SC-1396**). · fulfils AC-160e

## Execution dispatch — the topology cut (hq/site split plan 5d-2)

- **SC-337** — A chat message sent from hq dispatches over the control channel, is acknowledged with the created run id within the ack timeout, executes on site, and the completed reply appears in the hq session view. · fulfils AC-161a
- **SC-338** — Site's runtime code holds no Prisma model for any control-plane entity (Task, Project, Agent, User, …): the site schema defines only site-owned models, and every control-plane need is met by the dispatch payload or a data-path call. · fulfils AC-161b
- **SC-339** — A task run's state advances on hq driven by status frames from site: queued → running, and at the end the system alone settles it — none when the run finished or was stopped, Run failed when it failed, Needs you when it ended on a limit or a tool request — and the same end starts the agent's next queued task, whatever the agent did to the task during the run. Late, duplicate and early frames never overwrite a newer run. · fulfils AC-161a
- **SC-340** — With the site offline: a chat dispatch fails with an explicit site-unreachable error; a task dispatch leaves the task queued and hq dispatches it when the site reconnects. · fulfils AC-161c
- **SC-341** — hq restarting mid-run does not kill or reset the run: the site continues, and on the site's reconnect hq reconciles task state from `run.list` instead of blindly resetting RUNNING tasks. · fulfils AC-161c
- **SC-342** — Site restarting mid-run fails the orphaned run in site's boot sweep, and the resulting status frame moves the hq task to Run failed with the orphan reason. · fulfils AC-161c
- **SC-343** — Engine stream events (deltas, tool calls, thinking) reach the browser's session room through the site→hq push *(Reworded 2026-10-17: the in-process voice consumers are gone; a conversation's reply is spoken from its own stream — **SC-995**)*. · fulfils AC-161a
- **SC-344** — Transcript and run reads in hq's session UI are served over the channel from site's database; with the site down they fail with the explicit site-unreachable error, never stale or empty-as-if-none. · fulfils AC-161c
- **SC-345** — The session copy obeys one-writer-per-field: hq writes creation fields once; runtime fields (title, status, token totals) change only via site and replicate back — a rename or archive from hq's UI round-trips through site. · fulfils AC-161f
- **SC-346** — Deleting a session deletes site's row (cascading runs, transcripts, tool-call logs) and hq's copy; a half-completed delete is detected by the dangling-copy check rather than silently orphaning either half. · fulfils AC-161f
- **SC-347** — The four runtime models live in site's schema with their delete cascades intact, and hq's schema has none of them. · fulfils AC-161b
- **SC-348** — A capability whose audience names a different site is refused by this site's MCP and git-credential endpoints (the audience pin, reinstated). · fulfils AC-161b
- **SC-349** — An expired or missing credential is detected at container start: the run fails with the remediation message, the site pushes a credential-health event, and every admin sees one notification per site, agent and state per day. · fulfils AC-161e
- **SC-350** — ~~Heartbeats are hq-triggered pulses over the channel: timers live on hq, the site executes a roleless heartbeat run, and disabling the agent's heartbeat on hq stops the pulses.~~ **Retired 2026-10-05** — the heartbeat and its dispatch kind are gone; a boundary's pulse starts tasks through the ordinary task dispatch — see **SC-453** and **SC-454**.
- **SC-351** — ~~`read_file`/`write_file` work for native-runtime agents against the run's real workspace, and a relative path escaping into another agent's tree or memory partition is refused by scoped path validation.~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — NativeRuntime was removed (**SC-120**) and memory partitions with it (**SC-329**); a run's file tools are bounded by the harness path rule (**SC-768**). · fulfils AC-161b
- **SC-352** — A bypassed agent whose non-null tool list omits a write tool cannot escape a workspace cap: the mint derives the omission into the effective set so mounts go read-only and neverTools carries the cap. · fulfils AC-161b
- **SC-353** — ~~A deferred tool manifest raised on site reaches hq for approval, and approving it on hq resumes the run on site with the extended manifest.~~ **Retired 2026-10-13** — a run is never resumed (**SC-770**); approving a request starts the next run (**SC-640**). · fulfils AC-161a
- **SC-354** — Chat privacy survives the move: another user — an admin included — requesting a chat session's transcript or joining its session room is refused by hq from its own session copy, without consulting the site. · fulfils AC-161d
