- **SC-355** — hq validates every `run.stream` frame at intake: an unknown event name, a malformed room key, a non-`delta` bus channel, or a session id hq has no copy of is dropped and logged — never emitted into a browser room. · fulfils AC-161g

---

## First install and first sign-up (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-356** — With the same `HQ_PAIRING_SECRET` (at least 32 characters) in hq's and its own site's settings, the site connects the first time it starts, with no registration or approval, and the Execution Sites page shows one site, marked Default, as Online ("Connected. Agents run here.") with a recent last-heard-from time. · fulfils AC-162a
- **SC-357** — When the site is not paired or not connected, the Execution Sites page never shows Online and names the cases it can know — no pairing secret configured, a secret shorter than 32 characters, the site service not running, the site refusing to pair, hq's address unreachable from inside the site's container — with the fix for each; the default site offers "Check connection" like any other, and the site's own `status` command says the same on the machine. · fulfils AC-162b
- **SC-358** — hq refuses to start when a required setting is missing and names the setting where the person will look — in the compose error for every required setting — never booting into a half-working state that answers with a bare gateway error. · fulfils AC-162c
- **SC-359** — The commands hq's screens and the deployment guide print for using a site day to day — start it, register, status, unregister — work exactly as printed on a fresh install, with the settings file supplied the way the guide's own start command supplies it. · fulfils AC-162d
- **SC-360** — At its first start hq creates its organisation, its default site record (when paired) and the built-in Kernel agent, once; a second start creates none of them again; a person can chat with the Kernel agent as soon as a model credential and a connected site exist. · fulfils AC-162e
- **SC-361** — Once any account exists the first-admin link is refused for everyone and says HQ is already set up, and no route other than a valid invitation or an admin's create-account action creates an account — including the authentication framework's own sign-up endpoint, which refuses an uninvited request before and after the first admin exists and never adds a person to the count that decides how a shared credential may be used. · fulfils AC-009c
- **SC-362** — Two people presenting the first-admin link at the same moment produce exactly one Admin; the other is refused. · fulfils AC-009d
- **SC-363** — A person is never asked to spend a one-time sign-in code, or to save a credential, on an install that cannot store it: with the secrets master key missing or malformed, hq says so before the code is used, in a readable message naming the setting, and the deployment guide and settings example describe the key as required for runs. · fulfils AC-163d
- **SC-364** — ~~Setup's review tells the admin what is still open — model access when none is set, with Skip for now, and HQ's own site when it is not connected — and links to each; it never asks for something already done (**SC-710**, **SC-711**).~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — the last look no longer lists model access as open: a provider for models and voice is required to finish setup (**SC-1185**), and it shows where models and voice come from and, while HQ's own site is not connected, where agents will run (**SC-711**).

## Model access (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-365** — ~~An organisation API key an admin saves is stored encrypted and never sent back to any browser; the Model Access page then says only that a key is stored, and the missing-credential banner clears.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — organisation, project and person keys are gone; providers hold the organisation's keys, managed by organisation admins and never read back (**SC-1170** to **SC-1175**).
- **SC-366** — ~~Model credentials are managed by their owner and no one else: the organisation's by an org admin, a project's by that project's admin, a person's by that person alone; anyone else is refused with the role or membership named, and a status read reveals only presence, expiry and whether a run can use it.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — the keys it managed are gone; a provider is written only by an organisation admin, HQ's machine or Agenthix Cloud's controller (**SC-1172**), and a person's own Claude login is theirs alone (**SC-1197**).
- **SC-367** — Signing in with Claude (for a person): the person first accepts a plain warning that a subscription covers one person's own use; hq has a connected site start a throwaway login; the person opens the link on Claude's own page, pastes the short code back and sees the credential stored with its expiry; the credential is stored encrypted, the throwaway container and its folder are deleted, an abandoned login is removed after about ten minutes, and with no site connected the person is told a site is unreachable. *(Changed 2026-10-23: only a person signs in with Claude, for their own place; the organisation and a project hold no subscription — **SC-1197**.)* · fulfils AC-164a, AC-164b
- **SC-368** — ~~A run uses the model access of the person it is for, wherever it runs, unless its project holds its own: the project's, then the person's own login, then their own key, then the organisation's (**SC-701** to **SC-704**); a person's login only for a run that runs as them, their key for a task's run in a project; a subscription held by the organisation or a project only while the organisation has one person.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — a run no longer takes a credential in an order; the one resolver chooses a model via a provider for every call (**SC-1192**, **SC-1196**, **SC-1197**).
- **SC-369** — ~~A run with no usable credential is refused, and the person is told where they are looking — in the conversation, or on the task — what to do: add their own login or key in their Settings, ask an admin to set the organisation's key, or, in a project, ask that project's admin; when a stored shared subscription was skipped because the organisation has several people, the sentence says so.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — a call with nothing to run on is answered with the resolver's one problem, naming what is missing and who adds it (**SC-1195**).
- **SC-370** — ~~The number of people in the organisation is counted from accounts each time it is asked: a pending invite is not a person, and an account created by any route is.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — the count of people no longer decides any credential.
- **SC-371** — ~~While shared runs have no usable credential, every admin sees a Needs attention item on the Organisation page, linking to Organisation › Settings › Model access, and anyone who cannot talk sees a line under Home's message box (**SC-666**, **SC-722**); there is no banner on every page. It goes as soon as a usable credential exists.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — a provider that stops working is a notice to organisation admins (**SC-1177**), and Home says under its message box when no model works (**SC-722**).
- **SC-372** — ~~A stored organisation subscription that has passed its expiry is treated as not usable — by the banner, by the Model Access page ("sign in again") and by the choice of credential — rather than looking healthy until a run fails.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — only a person's own subscription can expire, and it is treated as unusable by the resolver (**SC-1197**).
- **SC-373** — ~~A model credential never outlives its owner: removing a person deletes their own credentials, and deleting a project deletes its credential.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — the organisation, project and person keys are gone; a person's own Claude login is removed with the person.
- **SC-374** — ~~In a project chat and on a task the person is told which credential the run will use — their own, the project's or the organisation's — so a subscription used happily in their own space is not a surprise failure in a project.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — a picker shows each model with its provider, and the problem says why a model cannot be used (**SC-1198**, **SC-1195**).

## Passwords (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-375** — A signed-in person changes their own password by giving the current one; the change takes effect at once and their other sessions end. The change is under Settings › Sign-in (**SC-532**). · fulfils AC-167a
- **SC-376** — An org admin makes a one-hour, one-time reset link for another person in Organisation › People; the person chooses their own password through it and every sign-in they had ends; an admin cannot read anyone's password (**SC-716**). · fulfils AC-167b
- **SC-377** — `agenthix hq reset-password <username>` on HQ's machine makes the same link for any person, including the last admin; the first-admin link never doubles as a recovery link (**SC-721**). · fulfils AC-167c

## People and invitations (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-378** — An invite is single-use, carries the role it was created with, expires seven days after it was created, and its link is shown once — only its hash is kept; a used, revoked, expired or unknown code creates no account and tells the invitee which of the four it is. · fulfils AC-004a, AC-004b, AC-007b, AC-008c
- **SC-379** — The invite link an admin copies opens on the colleague's computer: it is built from hq's configured public address rather than the address in the admin's own browser, and the admin is warned when that address is one only their own machine can reach. The same holds for password reset links and the first-admin link, all built from HQ's configured address (**SC-716**, **SC-717**, **SC-708**). · fulfils AC-004d
- **SC-380** — An invited person who has not yet signed up cannot sign in, appears in the admin's pending list with the days left, and is not counted as a person; the admin can revoke the invite. · fulfils AC-006c
- **SC-381** — Only an org admin can invite, revoke an invite, list invites or people, change a role, remove a person or create an account by hand; anyone else is refused with "Admin access required" and is shown none of the Users page's contents; nobody can change their own role or remove their own account. · fulfils AC-122d
- **SC-382** — A role change or demotion applies to the person's very next action: the role is read fresh on every call and is not carried in the browser session. · fulfils AC-122e
- **SC-383** — Removing a person from the organisation deactivates them and deletes nothing (see **SC-606**–**SC-609**): it is refused, naming the projects, while it would leave any project without an admin, and needs a choice for their open tasks in every project with any; erasing is a separate act on someone who has left. · fulfils AC-122f
- **SC-384** — An account with no role record is treated as an organisation Member by every check — in the browser, over MCP and in identity tokens — so a missing record never grants authority. · fulfils AC-168d
- **SC-385** — Opening an invite link while signed in as someone else warns on the accept page, names who is signed in, and offers to continue as the new person or cancel; it never replaces the session silently. (**SC-719**) · fulfils AC-008e

## Roles and refusals (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-386** — The accept page and the profile tell a person which role they hold and what it allows in plain words (the organisation's roles are Admin and Member; what they may do in a project is their role there). · fulfils AC-168a
- **SC-387** — ~~A project's Viewer can read what a member reads and cannot start a run there by any route — chat message, task run, run all, run a board, voice turn, approving a tool, turning automation on — and the…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a Viewer starts nothing by any route; the routes are now Run, Start, Start all agents, a start time and a routine; see SC-1809.
- **SC-388** — ~~A project's Viewer cannot cause agent work there indirectly either: they cannot queue a task for an agent (by assigning it, queueing it, moving it into an auto-queue stage or turning auto-queue on), c…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a Viewer starts nothing by any route; the routes are now Run, Start, Start all agents, a start time and a routine; see SC-1809.
- **SC-389** — A project's Viewer cannot stop, rename, delete or reconfigure a run, session, transcript, board, task or workspace of the project, nor comment or approve; the refusal says they are its Viewer. · fulfils AC-169c
- **SC-390** — Shaping the organisation's agents is the org admin's: creating, renaming, changing handle, role or image, deleting or stopping an agent, changing its settings, instructions or skills, applying a preset or template, importing or exporting an agent, and creating, changing or deleting the org's library skills and board templates each need ADMIN; anyone else is refused with the role named, and the kernel agent stays protected. · fulfils AC-170a
- **SC-391** — ~~Each change to an agent's behaviour, instructions or skills is a new version that records who made it; a run records the version it used; an unchanged save publishes nothing; a non-admin's behaviour change is refused before any part of the save is written.~~ **Retired 2026-09-27** — an agent is one current definition edited in place, with no version number and no publish step (ADR-0072) — see **SC-445** and **SC-446**.
- **SC-445** — A change to an agent's settings, instructions or skills is saved in place and reaches the agent's next run on every site, with no publish step and no version number; a run already started keeps the definition it started with and is never failed by an edit; the agent's settings are held once, on its record, and every reader uses that one copy; an unchanged save changes nothing; a non-admin's settings change is refused before any part of the save is written. · fulfils AC-170c
- **SC-392** — An organisation Member is never offered an admin act — creating or deleting a project, connecting or revoking a site, stopping everything, changing an agent: the control is hidden or disabled with the reason on screen, and forcing the request is refused naming ADMIN. · fulfils AC-168b
- **SC-393** — Every refusal a person meets says in plain words which role or membership is needed and shows where they acted — never only as a tooltip, never silent, never with an internal operation name in the sentence — and an action that is refused or cannot reach its site (start a chat, run, run all, stop, delete) never simply re-enables its button. · fulfils AC-168c

## Projects, membership and privacy (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-394** — A new project starts open to join — anyone in the organisation may preview it and Join — and its creator is its first project admin and, with anyone who joins, its listed members. · fulfils AC-172d
- **SC-395** — Only the people on a project's member list are its members, open or private. Someone who is not a member cannot open it, start a run in it, read its files, boards, tasks, comments, attachments, transcripts or runs, or write anything it owns — on any page, in any list, count or live room; an open project shows a non-member only its preview. · fulfils AC-171a
- **SC-396** — A private project a person is not on answers by key exactly like a project that does not exist, and a task key inside it exactly like a task that does not exist — same status, same words — including a real project key with an invented number. · fulfils AC-171b
- **SC-397** — Reading something a person may not see never confirms that it exists (a not-found identical to a missing thing's), and acting on something they can see but may not change is refused with the membership or role needed. This one rule covers chat sessions and task sessions alike. · fulfils AC-171c
- **SC-398** — A project admin decides the project's members and their roles, its open or private setting, its agents and its repository attachments; an org admin may add people and appoint a project admin in any project they can see, file the project under a product or programme and delete it, and can read a private project only by adding themselves — visibly, in its member list. · fulfils AC-172a
- **SC-399** — A project always has an admin: removing or demoting its last admin is refused ("Appoint another admin first"), from the project screen and by removing the person from the organisation alike. · fulfils AC-172b
- **SC-400** — Changing a project's access, or removing someone from it, takes effect at once: the person can no longer send a message or start a run that touches the project — including from a chat opened there earlier — and no longer sees it anywhere. · fulfils AC-173a
- **SC-401** — A project meant to be private can be made private when it is created, and switching an open project to private first says who will lose access. · fulfils AC-172c
- **SC-402** — Product and programme lists and pages, the agent detail page (its recent runs with their inputs and outputs, and its assigned tasks), the Execution Sites list and every count on them include only projects the person may open. · fulfils AC-174a
- **SC-403** — ~~The dashboard's project, task, session, run and cost totals count only what the person may open, so a number never reveals hidden work and agrees with the lists it summarises; a site that did not answer is named and the totals are marked incomplete rather than shown as zero.~~ **Retired 2026-10-12** — the dashboard whose totals counted only what the person may open is gone; Today (**SC-668**) is organisation-wide and admin-only.
- **SC-404** — A person who belongs to no project sees an empty state that says they have no access yet, not one that invites them to create the first project. · fulfils AC-174c
- **SC-405** — Deleting a project needs ADMIN and asks first, naming what will be lost — boards, tasks, files and workspaces; it stops the project's running work before removing anything and then removes the project's storage on its site. · fulfils AC-025d
- **SC-414** — A person removed from a private project keeps read access to their own old chat transcripts there — a chat is its owner's alone — but cannot continue them (see **SC-400**). · fulfils AC-173b

## Tasks, runs and stopping work (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-406** — ~~A task shows where it is — its board column, and its run state: none shown, Queued, Throttled, Running, Needs you or Run failed — and, for Needs you, Run failed and a task held for its site, the reaso…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the run states are replaced by the execution state, set by the system and apart from the task state (adr-0174); see SC-1798.
- **SC-407** — The task page shows what the agent concluded (the task's result) beside its comments and its transcript link, and an attachment's download control is visible without hovering. · fulfils AC-175b
- **SC-408** — Stop halts the actual run on its site from every place that offers it — the task, the Runs panel and the conversation — and afterwards the task says it was stopped by a person rather than failed. · fulfils AC-176a
- **SC-409** — A task queued for a site that is offline or needs an upgrade shows "Held" with the reason, and starts by itself when the site returns. · fulfils AC-175c
- **SC-410** — A chat message is stored before its run starts and survives every failure; a failed run's reason is kept and shown in the conversation and on the agent's Runs tab after a reload. · fulfils AC-087c
- **SC-411** — A chat that cannot start — its site unreachable or revoked, the person's role refused, no credential, no storage boundary — shows the reason where the person tried to start it. · fulfils AC-086c
- **SC-412** — When a run in a project has to wait behind another person's run in the same project, the chat and the task say it is waiting. · fulfils AC-087d
- **SC-413** — Deleting a session, a workspace or a project asks for confirmation that names what will be lost, works by keyboard and touch as well as by hover, and a failure is shown and never leaves the person believing something was deleted. · fulfils AC-177a
- **SC-415** — Stopping everything (an admin's act) asks first, cancels every active run on every connected Execution Site, then reports how many runs it stopped and names any site it could not reach; one unreachable site never stops the sweep of the others. · fulfils AC-176b
- **SC-416** — ~~Stopping everything (an admin's act) cancels the runs and nothing more: queued tasks stay queued, and a boundary's pulse would start them again.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — Stop every run takes waiting tasks out of line and nothing starts by itself afterwards (adr-0175); see SC-1808.
- **SC-417** — A person can cancel a chat run only if it is their own chat, and a task run only if they are a member of the task's project; nobody outside a private project can cancel work in it. · fulfils AC-176d

## Honesty about unreachable machines (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-418** — A machine that cannot be reached is never shown as an empty list or a silent no-op: the file explorer, the Runs panel and its session view, the conversations lists, and the workspace and repository panels say "Execution Site '<name>' is unreachable" (or revoked, or needs an upgrade), and a partial view names which machines did not answer. · fulfils AC-178a · tests `hq/src/shared/components/RunsPanel/__tests__/RunsPanelList.siteUnreachable.test.tsx`, `RunsPanelSession.siteUnreachable.test.tsx`
- **SC-419** — Any signed-in person can see whether each Execution Site is online, without being an admin and without any secret; advice to "register a site from Settings" is given only to a person who can do it. · fulfils AC-178b
- **SC-425** — The Execution Sites page explains each state in a sentence — registered but never connected, offline, needs upgrading (with the version it runs and the version hq needs), revoked — and for the default site names every cause it can know rather than one. · fulfils AC-178c

## Execution Sites: approving, revoking, rotating (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-420** — A further site registers by device approval: the site asks hq for a code and prints it, an admin types the code shown on that machine into hq's "Connect a site" page (or the approval page) and approves, and the site collects its machine credential once; a wrong, expired or already-used code gets one identical refusal; Deny needs no code; a person who is not an admin can neither list, look up, approve nor deny a request. · fulfils AC-179a
- **SC-421** — The approval shows the admin what the site says about itself (its name and host) beside what hq saw (the address it connected from), with a warning to approve only a machine just set up, and warns when the same site registered before ("approving replaces its credential; its projects stay with it"). · fulfils AC-179b
- **SC-422** — Revoking a site refuses its connection at once, fails its in-flight and held tasks with a stated reason, marks its in-flight chats failed and stops dispatching to it; the confirmation names how many projects are placed on it; the site can register again. · fulfils AC-180a
- **SC-423** — ~~Rotating a site's credential is an admin's act, sent over the live channel: the holder of the old credential is disconnected and the site reconnects with the new one unaided; a site that is offline is told to try again once it is online.~~ **Retired 2026-10-21** — rotating a site's credential is gone from hq, the site and the protocol at the version baseline: hq has no rotation operation or route (**SC-926**), and a machine gets a new credential only by leaving the fleet — `agenthix console disconnect` on the machine (**SC-848**) or a fleet admin removing it — and joining again (**SC-845**). · fulfils AC-180b
- **SC-424** — HQ's own site, paired by the shared secret, cannot be removed or leave by any route, and every message about it says so and names `HQ_PAIRING_SECRET`, to be changed in both environment files before a restart. · fulfils AC-180c

## What agents remember about a person (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-426** — ~~What an agent knows about a person is held by hq in two layers — global, read by every agent the person invokes, and per agent, read by that agent only — and is delivered to the chat runs that person invokes; a task receives neither; another person's memory, or another agent's, never reaches a run.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the two layers are no longer delivered whole to chat runs; what agents know about a person is memory in three scopes, recalled by meaning (**SC-986** to **SC-992**).
- **SC-427** — ~~A person can list, add, edit, delete and promote items in their own memory only; an agent adds to and forgets from only its own layer; each item records whether the agent or the person wrote it; deleting an agent deletes its partitions.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the person's memory is curated on the memory screen in all its scopes (**SC-991**), and an agent writes through `remember` and `forget` under the rule of **SC-986** and **SC-990**.

## Internal runtime (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-428** — ~~Between runs a shared container is emptied of what the run left: stray processes are killed first, then the writable scratch directories are cleared~~ **Retired 2026-09-30** — nothing is reset when a turn ends; a process belongs to the conversation that started it and ends with the conversation or after it has been idle (adr-0083) — see **SC-450**.
- **SC-429** — A refreshed subscription token from a long run reaches hq over the channel: hq accepts it only from the site that holds the claimed person's storage, only as a plausible and newer credential, and stores it for its holder; the site writes it nowhere on its own disk.
- **SC-449** — The OpenCode runtime runs a chat turn on the OpenCode server in the conversation's container: text deltas, tool calls and results stream back as the same events the Claude runtime emits, every tool call passes the per-call check before it runs, and the turn ends in a completed or failed status carrying the model and the error. A provider key is never in the container: model requests go through the site's relay, which adds the key.
- **SC-450** — A process an agent starts belongs to the conversation that started it: it ends when the conversation is closed or deleted, or has been idle for the machine's set time, and never merely because a turn ended or another conversation in the same container went quiet; a conversation's staged skills are removed with it.

## Automation, schedules and limits (added 2026-10-05)

The unit *Agents act on their own schedule* (adr-0087 to adr-0092). A **boundary** is a project or a person's personal project. Where a scenario names an automated test, the test asserts it today without carrying its `@SC` tag yet.

### Automation, the pulse and the throttle

- **SC-451** — ~~Automation is a switch on each boundary, off by default.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-452** — ~~The organisation sets each automation value once — whether automation is allowed, maximum depth (default 3), queue limit (default 20), whether every boundary must throttle, the interval (default 30 mi…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-453** — ~~With automation on, each agent's queued tasks in a boundary form one line, highest priority first, then longest queued first, and the pulse starts the first when the agent is free there;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-454** — ~~One scheduler in hq starts every automated start: at the top of every minute it asks the database what is due and starts it, closing finished rounds and moving routines on before the pulse.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-455** — ~~The throttle is optional and off by default.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-456** — ~~The throttle fires on the leading edge and measures from the end of a round: a task queued into a boundary whose last round finished at least an interval ago starts a round at once;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-457** — ~~Depth bounds chains of agent-queued work.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — agents start work only with `run_task`, bounded by depth, and there is no queue limit (adr-0175); see SC-1810 and SC-1811.
- **SC-458** — ~~Depth is counted on every act of queueing, not on creating a task: creating a task queued, moving it into a column that queues automatically, moving a finished task back into one, and starting it with…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — agents start work only with `run_task`, bounded by depth, and there is no queue limit (adr-0175); see SC-1810 and SC-1811.
- **SC-459** — ~~The queue limit applies to agents only. When agents in a boundary already hold the limit's worth of queued tasks (default 20, across the boundary, not per agent), a task an agent creates, or moves int…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — agents start work only with `run_task`, bounded by depth, and there is no queue limit (adr-0175); see SC-1810 and SC-1811.
- **SC-460** — ~~`run_task` is the one way an agent starts another task.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — `run_task` is reworded for how work starts (adr-0175); see SC-1810.

### Start times and routines

- **SC-461** — ~~A task with a start time starts at most once, at that time, through the same path as a person pressing Run, on behalf of the person who set the time, at depth 0 — it does not wait for the pulse, and i…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a start time is a Run at… time that starts the task once and nothing earlier starts it (adr-0175); see SC-1803.
- **SC-462** — ~~A column never starts a task early. A task whose start time is still to come is not queued by being created in, or moved into, a column that queues automatically — it shows when its time is;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-463** — ~~A column never queues a task that has no agent: it stays with no run pending rather than waiting forever for something to run it;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-464** — ~~A start time or a routine can be set by the person the task is done on behalf of or by an admin of its project — the same people who may reassign it — and by nobody else.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a start time is a Run at… time that starts the task once and nothing earlier starts it (adr-0175); see SC-1803.
- **SC-465** — ~~A routine is a schedule (every N minutes, hours or days, or at a time on given days;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a routine is a repeatable run on a cron schedule that makes no task per round (adr-0177); see SC-1816 to SC-1825.
- **SC-466** — ~~A routine's round starts at its time, as if its person pressed Run, whatever its column or board — the routine does not depend on a column that queues;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a routine is a repeatable run on a cron schedule that makes no task per round (adr-0177); see SC-1816 to SC-1825.
- **SC-467** — ~~Editing a routine updates its waiting round;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a routine is a repeatable run on a cron schedule that makes no task per round (adr-0177); see SC-1816 to SC-1825.
- **SC-468** — ~~When the person a routine is done on behalf of loses access to its project, the routine is paused with the reason and its waiting round is blocked like any of that person's tasks;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a routine is a repeatable run on a cron schedule that makes no task per round (adr-0177); see SC-1816 to SC-1825.
- **SC-469** — ~~A routine opens inside its place's Automation tool, and **Routines** in the navigation lists routines across places (**SC-684** to **SC-688**);~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a routine is a repeatable run on a cron schedule that makes no task per round (adr-0177); see SC-1816 to SC-1825.

### What a task shows, and what a person may do about it

- **SC-470** — ~~A task's board column and its run state are separate, and the system alone sets the run state: agents have no tool for it.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the run states are replaced by the execution state, set by the system and apart from the task state (adr-0174); see SC-1798.
- **SC-471** — ~~A queued or throttled task says why it has not started, wherever it is shown: its place in its agent's line, the time of the next round, or that the boundary's automation is off;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the run states are replaced by the execution state, set by the system and apart from the task state (adr-0174); see SC-1798.
- **SC-472** — A person with the right to start runs can clear a failed run, or a blocked one that is not a loss of access, back to no run pending with **Dismiss**, or run the task again over it; Run on a task whose run is already queued, throttled or going is refused and starts nothing; the project's Viewer is refused, told they are its Viewer. · fulfils AC-189d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/runAgainOrDismiss.test.ts`
- **SC-484** — ~~The board refreshes on every run-state change without a reload: a task queued, throttled, started, finished, failed, blocked, withdrawn or dismissed, and a throttled boundary's round opening or closin…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.
- **SC-485** — ~~Each time a pulse fires, hq records which boundary it belonged to, when, and which tasks it started with which agents;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the column that starts work, the pulse, the throttle, the queue limit and the automation switch are gone, and work starts only by an explicit act (adr-0175); see SC-1801.

### On behalf of a person

- **SC-473** — Every task records the person it is done on behalf of, and who queued it: a person who creates it is that person; an agent that creates it in a run is the person that run was for, followed up the chain, so it is always a person; a task with a start time is the person who set it up, or who asked the agent that did; a task created by confirming a workforce template is the person who confirmed it. A task's page shows the person, and for an agent's work the agent and run that queued it. · fulfils AC-193a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskQueuedBy.test.ts`, `onBehalfOfOperations.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runOnBehalfOf.test.ts`
- **SC-474** — When a run defers a tool it was not allowed, the person the run is on behalf of — whoever started it, or the task's person for a run nobody started (see **SC-520**) — is asked and gets a notification in hq that links to the request, as well as the comment on the task; anyone on the project who may start runs can approve it (see **SC-521**); nobody is notified when that person has been removed; a failure to notify never loses the request. · fulfils AC-193b · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/approvalNotification.test.ts`, `approvalNotification.integration.test.ts`, `taskMachineOnBehalfOf.test.ts`
- **SC-475** — When the person a task is done on behalf of loses access to its project, every task done on their behalf there is blocked at once, with a reason that says so, and a run about to start is blocked rather than failed; Run on it is refused, Dismiss does not clear it, and no tool request from it asks anyone. Only an admin of the project can assign someone who can reach the project, which releases the task; it is never routed to another person automatically. · fulfils AC-193c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/lossOfAccess.test.ts`, `onBehalfOfOperations.test.ts`, `runAgainOrDismiss.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/taskMachineOnBehalfOf.test.ts`

### Run limits, personal projects, connections and the rest

- **SC-476** — Every run has a time limit, and a run no person started — one a schedule started, or one an agent started with `run_task` — also has a turn limit; a run a person started has only the time limit. The organisation's values (default 60 minutes and 100 turns) are set by an org admin, a project's admin may only tighten them for the project, the stricter value applies at use and an org that tightens below a project's choice wins without rewriting it, and a person's personal project takes the organisation's values. A run stopped by a limit ends with a reason naming the limit, and its task is Needs you with that reason, not retried. · fulfils AC-188c, AC-190a, AC-190b, AC-190c · tests `hq/src/agents/runLimits/__tests__/runLimitsService.test.ts`, `runLimitsActions.test.ts`, `core/src/protocol/__tests__/runLimits.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/runStateMachine.test.ts`
- **SC-477** — Every person has a personal project, created with their account (and for accounts that had none), named "My tasks", private to them, one member who is its admin and who cannot add others, leave it or delete it — each refused act names the act — and with no site or storage of its own. It is shown as Chat, the person's own space (see **SC-507**), and reuses the project's tasks, boards, routines and Workroom. *(Changed 2026-10-23: a person can make further personal projects — **SC-1207**; this scenario describes the space, marked as such, whose storage is the person's own.)* · fulfils AC-194a, AC-194b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProject.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-481** — A task in a personal project runs as the person, not as a project: its runner is that person, it runs in their own storage with their own Claude subscription and their own agent settings, it uses the organisation's run limits, and no one else can see the project, its tasks or its runs. *(Changed 2026-10-23: it runs in the project's own storage for a personal project other than the space — **SC-1210**; the cost goes to the default billing profile.)* · fulfils AC-194c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProject.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/runLimits/__tests__/runLimitsService.test.ts`
- **SC-478** — A person creates their own connections to hq, each with a name, permission groups (Read on; Change tasks, Start work and Manage off), all their projects or a chosen list of ones they belong to (never one they cannot reach, never an empty list), and an expiry (90 days by default, renewable). The token is shown once and only its hash is stored; the list shows each connection's name, permissions, projects, last use and expiry and never a token; revoking ends it at once and a revoked one cannot be renewed; another person's connection, an admin's request included, is not found. · fulfils AC-195a, AC-195b · tests `hq/src/connections/__tests__/operations.test.ts`, `connectionAuth.test.ts`
- **SC-479** — A connection acts as its person and never as more: it reaches only the projects its person reaches, narrowed to its own list, with the person's role read at every call (a demotion applies at once), and a tool whose permission group is off is refused naming the group to turn on — a read-only connection refuses every write. Anything it creates or changes is recorded as done by that person through that connection; a run it starts counts as started by a person, and a task it queues is at depth 0 and never limited by the agents' queue limit. · fulfils AC-195c · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/connectionGroups.test.ts`, `taskOps.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/mcp/__tests__/mcpServer.authorization.test.ts`, `hq/src/connections/__tests__/connectionAuth.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-480** — `/mcp` refuses an unknown, expired or revoked connection token, a person no longer in the organisation, a person with no role, and the retired org-wide key, all with the same 401 so the response says nothing about which. · fulfils AC-195d · tests `hq/src/connections/__tests__/connectionAuth.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/mcp/__tests__/handler.test.ts`
- **SC-482** — A person's timezone is stored: the browser's, filled in when they have none and followed when they move, until they choose one (clearing it hands it back to the browser); a value that is not a real zone is refused. The times an agent's tools read — a start time or a routine's schedule with no offset — are read in the timezone of the person the run is for, an explicit offset or zone wins, a person with none is read as UTC and the result says so, and a run's prompt states the person's local date and time. · fulfils AC-196a, AC-196b · tests `hq/src/settings/__tests__/timezoneActions.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/scheduleTimezone.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/runContextPerson.test.ts`, `core/src/shared/__tests__/timezone.test.ts`
- **SC-483** — A task that has sat in Done with no run pending for longer than its project's "Archive finished tasks after" setting (off, 1 day, 7 days or 30 days) is archived automatically, staying searchable; the organisation's value (1 day out of the box, set by an org admin) is only the default each project starts with, so a project's own choice stands whatever the organisation sets, and anyone who can change a task can still archive it by hand. · fulfils AC-188c, AC-197a · tests `hq/src/projects/finishedArchive/__tests__/archiveSweep.test.ts`, `archiveSetting.test.ts`, `doneAt.test.ts`
- **SC-486** — An agent has no schedule of its own and no tool to set a task's run state: there is no heartbeat setting, document, tab, manual pulse or session type, and no `update_task_state`; stopping an agent switches nothing off; nothing starts an agent's work except an explicit act (see SC-1801) or a routine's firing. · fulfils AC-438a · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/taskOps.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/mcp/__tests__/mcpTools.test.ts`

## Conversation-first hq (added 2026-10-07)

The unit *Agenthix is usable — every screen reviewed* (adr-0095, adr-0096). A **place** is where a conversation belongs: a project, or a person's own space, shown as **Chat**. A place's **tools panel** sat beside its conversations and held its facts and tools; since 2026-10-18 a place's sections are in the left panel and the conversation is docked beside every page (*The desktop shell*, below), and the tools panel and the layouts are retired (**SC-501**, **SC-505**, **SC-506**). The shell's own scenarios that this unit changed are updated in place above (**SC-010**, **SC-011**, **SC-014**, **SC-150** to **SC-156**, **SC-221**, **SC-224**, **SC-225**).

### The shell

- **SC-489** — hq is light by default; a person chooses dark (or light again) under Appearance in their Settings, the choice is remembered in their browser and applied before the first screen is drawn, and every colour comes from the design tokens so both themes hold. · fulfils AC-209a · tests `hq/src/shared/__tests__/theme.test.ts`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/AppearanceSection.test.tsx`
- **SC-490** — Organisation links Products, Programs and Library for everyone; their pages and addresses are unchanged, and the Projects screen filters by product and programme. The Organisation page lays them out as cards (see **SC-534**, **SC-664**). · fulfils AC-016d · tests `hq/src/org/pages/__tests__/OrgPage.test.tsx`

### Pins

- **SC-491** — A person pins a project, a board or an agent with the pin button in its header, and unpins it the same way; pinning an item again changes nothing, and a pin of another kind with the same id is a different item. Pins are stored in hq per person, in the order they were pinned, so they follow the person to another device; a person's pins are never listed to, or changed by, anyone else. · fulfils AC-199a · tests `hq/src/pins/__tests__/pins.integration.test.ts`, `PinButton.test.tsx`
- **SC-492** — The Pinned list, which the foot's Pinned row opens (**SC-1045**), shows each pin as a link with its avatar — only the avatars, named by their title, when the navigation is collapsed — highlights the pin of the page that is open (the most specific when several match), and shows nothing when there are no pins. With Edit on, each pin has a handle to drag it and a button to unpin it; the new order shows at once and is stored, and pins left out of an order, hidden ones included, keep theirs after it. · fulfils AC-199b · tests `hq/src/pins/__tests__/PinnedNav.test.tsx`, `pinOrder.test.ts`, `pins.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/AppSidebar.test.tsx`
- **SC-493** — Pinning a private project the person is not on, a board in a project they cannot see, or someone else's personal project is refused as if it did not exist; a pin to something they can no longer see, or that is gone, is kept but not shown, and can still be unpinned. · fulfils AC-199c · tests `hq/src/pins/__tests__/pins.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-494** — A project and a board have a default avatar: the project's initial, or a board mark, on a colour derived from its id — the same every time, different between items, and readable behind white lettering in both themes. · fulfils AC-199d · tests `hq/src/shared/__tests__/placeAvatar.test.tsx`

The screen bar reads *Projects / <project>*, and the project's avatar, name and pin are at the head of the left panel (**SC-1024**), its description on its Overview (**SC-1046**). A former page's address (`/projects/:key/tasks`, `/routines`, `/github` and the rest) opens the project on that section. *(Changed 2026-10-18: a project opens on its Overview, **SC-1023**, and its conversations are a section in the left panel.)* · fulfils AC-200a, AC-023d · tests `hq/src/places/__tests__/conversationGroups.test.ts`, `PlaceScreen.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/AppLayout.test.tsx`

A closed one keeps its history and no message box, and its *Start a new conversation* opens the message box of the place it worked in (Chat when it was in none) with the same agent picked. · fulfils AC-200e · tests `hq/src/shared/components/ChatPanel/__tests__/PanelChatView.closed.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/providers/__tests__/VoiceCallProvider.test.tsx`

### Chat and Home

- **SC-507** — Your space (`/space`) is the person's own space as a place — their personal project — with its own entry in the navigation, after Home and Chat, highlighted there. It opens on its Overview; its sections are Overview, Conversations, Tasks, Boards, Runs, Automation, Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces and Agents, with no Members or Settings, and its panel reads *Your space*. Its conversations are those in no project, in the person's own storage or in their personal project, and its message box offers every agent in the organisation. Its facts say who sees it (only you), which agents (any), the Site — shown only once the person's storage is placed on one, and never placing it — and Files (your personal files); its groups are Work (Tasks, Boards), Agents at work (Runs, Automation, Run all, Stop all) and Files (Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces). Chat (`/chat`) is only the list of your space's conversations, headed *Chat*, with the message box, and the main navigation stays in the left panel; a tool's address there (`/chat?tool=automation`) opens that section of your space. A project's conversations are listed in neither. *(Changed 2026-10-18: Chat was the space itself, with its tools beside its conversations.)* · fulfils AC-203a, AC-203b, AC-329d · tests `hq/src/chat/__tests__/chatPlace.test.tsx`, `storageSiteQuery.integration.test.ts`, `placeConversationAddresses.test.tsx`

- **SC-509** — Home (`/`) greets the person by the time of day and their name and asks what they would like to work on, with the message box focused on a wide screen; its first message starts a conversation in the person's own space as Chat's own box does (**SC-940**) and opens it in the dock, Home staying in the canvas (**SC-1037**). Under the box, three buttons say how many notifications need the person (opening Notifications), how many of their runs are going (opening Runs) and how many of their routine rounds and start times fall due before the end of their day (opening your space's Automation), each saying so plainly at zero. There is no box to type into a run. The page's **Comments** tab holds the task's comments threaded one level (a reply to a reply goes under the thread's top comment), written in Markdown; **Activity** lists what happened to the task newest first, one line each, a run's or comment's line opening its tab. ### Shared task sessions

- **SC-518** — A task's session is shared with its project: every member who may see the project reads it, and anyone on the project who may start runs can send into it, interrupt or stop its run, start one, and approve its requests; a viewer only watches. A message sent into an idle task's session starts a run at once. Someone outside a private project gets the same answer as for a task that does not exist. · fulfils AC-207a · tests `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/sessionReads.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchOnBehalf.integration.test.ts`, `sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/pages/__tests__/tasksToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-519** — Every person's message in a session records who sent it, on the site, and hq shows it with their name; the viewer's own message reads "You", and one whose sender is no longer in the organisation reads "Someone". · fulfils AC-207b · tests `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/chatExecution.author.test.ts`, `rows.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/transcriptAuthors.test.ts`, `sessionReads.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/ChatPanel/__tests__/PanelChatView.look.test.tsx`
- **SC-520** — A run is on behalf of whoever started it (adr-0095). Run, Run again, Run all, or a message into the task's session is on that person's behalf: their access to the project is checked before it starts, its times are read on their clock, its tool requests notify them, and a task its agent creates is on their behalf, because hq signs the person into the run's capability and carries them through its renewal. A run nobody started — a column, a start time, a routine, the pulse — is on behalf of the task's person, whatever the task last recorded, and a token that names no person is never read as a person's start. A run whose starter has left the project fails with that reason rather than blocking the task for its own person; a sender who is not on the task's project is refused and nothing is dispatched. A dispatch to a site too old to take the person leaves it out; the capability carries it all the same. · fulfils AC-207c, AC-193a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchOnBehalf.integration.test.ts`, `taskSlotClaim.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runOnBehalfOf.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/capabilityRenewal.integration.test.ts`, `core/src/protocol/__tests__/capability.test.ts`, `sharedSessions.test.ts`, `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/taskExecution.comments.test.ts`
- **SC-521** — Anyone on a task's project who may start runs may approve its tool request, whoever the run is for; rejecting needs a role that may change work, and a viewer may do neither. Approving re-runs the task as its starter's, and a run nobody started as nobody's; approving a request of a run that a message into the session started starts the session's next run, as that message's sender, and not once the sender has left the project (**SC-641**). The request notifies the person whose message started the run, or the task's person when that sender has left; a frame from a site that does not hold the session is ignored. · fulfils AC-207d, AC-193b · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`, `taskSessionApproval.integration.test.ts`, `approvalNotification.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-522** — Each task run is given the comments written since its agent last ran on the task, oldest first: hq sends the newest 20, each cut to 2,000 characters, leaving out the agent's own and the approval markers, and the site keeps those written after that agent's last run started (all of them for an agent that has not run on the task). The prompt marks them new and says `get_task_details` returns the rest, which it does, oldest first; a run with no new comments has no comments section. · fulfils AC-207e · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchOnBehalf.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/taskScope.integration.test.ts`, `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/taskExecution.comments.test.ts`, `site/src/agents/__tests__/AgentPromptBuilder.test.ts`, `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/dispatchParams.drift.test.ts`
- **SC-523** — Who stopped or interrupted a run is recorded: Stop on a task or a conversation, Stop all, and a new message that interrupts a run send the person's name with the cancel, and the run's error reads "Stopped by <name>" or "Interrupted by <name>"; the session notes it too when the turn had produced no text. With no name — a site too old to take one — the run reads "Cancelled by user" or "Interrupted by a new message", as before. · fulfils AC-207f · tests `site/src/agents/runtime/__tests__/AgentEngine.cancelRun.test.ts`, `turnStop.test.ts`, `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/dispatchHandler.test.ts`, `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/siteRuntimeStore.test.ts`

### Settings, the organisation's settings and Infrastructure

- **SC-531** — Settings, at the navigation's foot, is one page of the signed-in person's own, under their name and picture with their organisation role and when they joined: Profile (their handle and timezone), Appearance, Conversations (sending into a busy one, and their primary agent), Your Claude subscription, Which models may run for you, Memory (with the new conversations on: **SC-991**), Devices and connections (a phone, and connections for tools that act for them, each opening its own page one step under Settings) and Sign-in (change the password, sign out). Nothing for admins is reachable from it. *(Changed 2026-10-17: Voice, the person's voice preferences, left Settings.)* *(Changed 2026-10-23: the sections are Profile, Appearance, Conversations, Primary agent, Your Claude subscription, Which models may run for you, Memory, Devices and connections and Sign-in; the person's own keys are gone.)* · fulfils AC-210a · tests `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/SettingsPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-532** — A person changes their own password under Settings › Sign-in: the current password, then the new one twice. The current one is checked with Wasp's own password check, and after five wrong ones in 15 minutes no attempt is tried, even with the right one, until the window ends; the new one must meet the sign-up rule (at least 8 characters) and match its repeat; a refusal changes nothing and says why. On success the new password is stored hashed by Wasp, the old one no longer signs in, and every other session of theirs — other browsers and paired phones — ends with every unspent phone pairing code, while the session the change was made from stays signed in; no one else's sessions are touched. · fulfils AC-210b · tests `hq/src/auth/__tests__/changePassword.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/ChangePasswordForm.test.tsx`
- **SC-533** — Organisation › Settings (`/org/settings`) holds the organisation's settings as a workplace — Web search, GitHub (installing the App on the organisation's accounts), Sites for projects, Automation, Runs (run time and turn limits, archiving finished tasks) and Tool risk rules — and Organisation › Infrastructure (`/org/infrastructure`) what everything runs on — HQ (this installation's name and description, version, address and environment), every Execution Site, read-only (**SC-637**), storage as hq knows it, registering the GitHub App, and Logs and diagnostics (**SC-724**). Both are for organisation admins: anyone else who opens one of their addresses is told only an organisation admin can see it and is shown nothing of it. *(Changed 2026-10-23: the organisation's settings moved under Manage organisation (`/admin/settings`, `/admin/infrastructure` — **SC-1189**, **SC-1190**); Model access there is the organisation's web-search key, and Voice, Model access and the provider keys moved to Providers and Models.)* *(Changed 2026-10-25: registering the GitHub App and its accounts are one page, Settings › GitHub, which Infrastructure's GitHub App card links to — **SC-1308**.)* · fulfils AC-210c · tests `hq/src/org/pages/__tests__/OrgAdminPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/sites/pages/__tests__/ExecutionSitesPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/model-access/pages/__tests__/ModelAccessPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/settings/__tests__/settingsAdmin.integration.test.ts`

## Boards, sprints, plans and the timeline (added 2026-10-12)

### Boards as lasting workflows

- **SC-540** — ~~Every project is made with Project Tasks, its default board, and so is a person's own project;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-541** — ~~A task is always on a board: one made with no board named — by a person's form, the service, an agent's tool in a person's chat or a person's connection, or a routine whose template names none — lands…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-542** — ~~A board's columns each have a name, a label and a state (To do, In progress or Done), kept in order;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-543** — ~~A task in a board's Backlog is To do and starts nothing.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-544** — ~~An archived board is read-only and left out of the boards list unless asked for;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-545** — ~~A board shows its own Backlog always and, besides, the Backlogs of the project's other unarchived boards the person chose — only this project's, remembered for that person;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-546** — ~~The boards list puts Project Tasks first, with each board's column counts, its Backlog count, how many of its tasks need a person, and — for a sprint board — its current sprint and days left;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.

### Sprints

- **SC-547** — ~~A Sprints board starts in Sprint 1 of the length asked for (14 days when none), and its dates are whole days.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a sprint belongs to the project, not to a board, and is finished only explicitly (adr-0174); see SC-1795 to SC-1797.
- **SC-548** — ~~Finish sprint takes a required destination for the unfinished tasks — the next sprint or the Backlog — and hq refuses, changing nothing, when it is missing, in the browser's action and in the agents'…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a sprint belongs to the project, not to a board, and is finished only explicitly (adr-0174); see SC-1795 to SC-1797.
- **SC-549** — ~~A sprint finishes once when two people finish it at the same moment;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a sprint belongs to the project, not to a board, and is finished only explicitly (adr-0174); see SC-1795 to SC-1797.
- **SC-550** — ~~Renaming a sprint keeps its number and reads "Sprint 12 · Pricing push";~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a sprint belongs to the project, not to a board, and is finished only explicitly (adr-0174); see SC-1795 to SC-1797.
- **SC-551** — ~~Finish sprint on screen is in place: it says what stays recorded, offers the two destinations with nothing chosen and its button disabled, enables it only once one is chosen and sends that one, and sh…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a sprint belongs to the project, not to a board, and is finished only explicitly (adr-0174); see SC-1795 to SC-1797.

### A task's plan, epics and milestones

- **SC-552** — A task's plan is a start day and a finish day, set and cleared by day and kept apart from the time an agent starts it; a finish before the start, or a date that is not a day, is refused. Changing a plan, an epic or a milestone writes no run, start time or column and never starts, stops or queues anything; only a member of the project may change it. · fulfils AC-222a, AC-222b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlan.integration.test.ts`, `planSpan.test.ts`
- **SC-553** — A task belongs to at most one epic, an epic to none, and only an epic has a milestone (making a task an ordinary task again clears it, and an epic that still groups tasks cannot become one); an epic or milestone of another project is refused. Deleting a milestone keeps its epics, and deleting an epic keeps its tasks, in no epic. · fulfils AC-222c, AC-222d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlan.integration.test.ts`, `planSpan.test.ts`
- **SC-554** — An epic's span is its own plan when it has one, else the span of its tasks' plans, filling the end it lacks from its tasks, and nothing when neither has a plan. · fulfils AC-222e · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/planSpan.test.ts`
- **SC-555** — A task shows its plan's dates and its epic; the person changes the dates, picks or clears the epic, and gives an epic a milestone instead, through the plan; the server's refusal is shown; someone who may not change the work reads it only. · fulfils AC-222f · tests `hq/src/projects/components/__tests__/taskPlanFacts.test.tsx`
- **SC-556** — ~~Agents read and set boards and plans through their tools: `list_boards` and `get_board` give a board's kind, columns and current sprint (archived only when asked), `create_board` takes a kind, sprint…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — the agents' board and plan tools are reworded for types, columns, views and sprints; see SC-1828.

- **SC-557** — ~~The Boards tool lists Project Tasks first with its Default marker and a sprint board with its sprint and days left, says how many tasks need a person on a board's first line and nothing when none do,…~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-558** — ~~New board asks, in place, for a name, Workflow or Sprints, and columns from a template, and waits for a name.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.

- **SC-560** — ~~"Starts work" is shown beside a column that starts it and is switched from the column's own menu;~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-561** — ~~Edit columns refuses a column named Backlog, will not remove a column that still holds tasks, and saves the columns in the order and with the states chosen.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-562** — ~~The Backlog folds like Done: Hide folds it, Show brings it back, side by side it folds to a slim strip with its count, and the choice is remembered per board and works without browser storage.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.
- **SC-563** — ~~The Boards tool's screens are in the address — the list, archived, new, a board, its timeline and finish screens, a task and its session — and do not read the Tasks tool's own parameters.~~ **Retired 2026-11-08** — a board is a saved view of the project's tasks and owns no columns, Backlog, template or sprint (adr-0176); see SC-1791 to SC-1794.

### The Timeline

- **SC-564** — The timeline of a project holds, a lane per task type (Epic aside), the tasks that have a plan overlapping the range (its finish day counts), a start time in it, or work in it — never the Backlog, which each lane counts — the epics with their span, each in the first lane holding one of its tasks, and the milestones dated in the range; a routine makes no task, so none is on it (adr-0177). A range is two dates, at most 400 days, and an empty, inverted or unreadable one is refused. · fulfils AC-224a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardViews.integration.test.ts`, `timelineRange.test.ts`
- **SC-565** — The timeline reads the runs' slices from the project's site in one read, asking only for the tasks it shows; a run still going is open-ended; it says when the site could not be asked, is truncated only when those tasks' runs are themselves over the page size, and asks a site too old to take a task filter without it. · fulfils AC-224a, AC-224e · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/timelineRuns.integration.test.ts`, `boardViews.integration.test.ts`, `timelineRange.test.ts`
- **SC-566** — The chart has three scales — Week (a column a day), Month (a column a week) and Quarter (a column a month) — scrolls across everything known with room either side of today, and draws a plan from its first day to the end of its last (one date is one day, clipped and said so when it runs past), a start time or a round as ▶, the runs as darker slices, a task that needs a person outlined, an epic marked and a milestone as a flag on its date with its epics under it. Show puts tasks (a lane per task type, the project's sprints as a band in the first, tasks under their epic), epics only, or milestones with their epics; **Types** chooses which lanes show. · fulfils AC-224b · tests `hq/src/projects/timeline/__tests__/timelineLayout.test.ts`, `timelineRows.test.ts`, `timelineView.test.tsx`
- **SC-567** — Dragging a bar changes only the plan (both dates, keeping the length), dragging an end changes only that end and never passes the other, a plan with one date is written with both, and dragging ▶ changes only the start time by whole days keeping its time of day; a routine's round, an epic whose span comes from its tasks, and any bar where the change is not given cannot be dragged. A click opens the task and the end of a drag does not. · fulfils AC-224c, AC-224d · tests `hq/src/projects/timeline/__tests__/timelineLayout.test.ts`, `timelineView.test.tsx`
- **SC-568** — The timeline says quietly when runs could not be read and when older work is left out, and when the project reaches further than a timeline can show. · fulfils AC-224e · tests `hq/src/projects/timeline/__tests__/timelineView.test.tsx`
- **SC-569** — Narrow, the timeline is an agenda — This week, Next week, Later, each task with its date — keeping a plan still running in this week and leaving out one that is over and any task without dates; a task opens from its line. · fulfils AC-224f · tests `hq/src/projects/timeline/__tests__/timelineRows.test.ts`, `timelineView.test.tsx`
- **SC-570** — A task opened from the timeline, and its session, are in the address; the address does not read the Tasks or Boards tools' names. · fulfils AC-224g · tests `hq/src/projects/timeline/__tests__/timelineTaskUrl.test.ts` · no automated test that a board's own Timeline opens on Week without a lane header

## Agents in projects and in the organisation (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-571** — A project's admin may assign an agent to the project, and an organisation admin may assign one to any project, a private one they are not in included, but never to a person's own space; anyone else is refused, and an agent that does not exist answers 404. · fulfils AC-225a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/agentAssignments.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-572** — Removing an agent from a project requires saying where its open tasks (and routines) there go — another agent of the project or none — and is refused, changing nothing, without the choice; its done tasks and everything in other projects are left alone; only the project's admin may. Removing the project's default agent also requires a new default, or none, and an agent that is not the default needs none. · fulfils AC-225b, AC-225c, AC-225d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/removeAgentFromProject.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-573** — A project's agents read says what each agent is doing here — needs you before running before idle with what starts next, a start time or a routine's next run, whichever is sooner — with the task and its tasks here by state, shows every member the project's narrowing of it, lists its recent runs in this project only, and answers a private project the person is not on, or an agent not assigned to it, as not found. · fulfils AC-226a, AC-226e · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/projectAgentQueries.integration.test.ts`, `agentNow.test.ts`
- **SC-574** — The Agents tool's list names what every agent follows, opening in Files, shows each agent's now-line, tasks by state and model — marked when narrowed here — opens the agent from the whole row, offers ＋ Assign only where the person may assign, and, among other members, marks the default agent and names the teams that bring an agent here. · fulfils AC-226a, AC-226d · tests `hq/src/projects/agents/__tests__/agentsToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-575** — An agent opened in the tool shows In this project to a member without Edit, the model beside the agent's own, and Permissions here to every member; the project's admin also gets Edit and Remove from this project, edits the model only, and saves the permissions the narrowing already holds as they are; an agent here only through a team offers no Remove and says to change it in the team. Start a conversation and Give it a task (with the title typed) work from it, and Tasks, Runs, Files and the agent's place open from where they are shown. · fulfils AC-226b, AC-226c · tests `hq/src/projects/agents/__tests__/agentsToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-576** — Remove from this project counts the open tasks and chooses nothing for the person, keeping Remove disabled until a choice is made, then sends the agent chosen, or null for leaving them unassigned; it cannot hand them to another agent when there is none. Removing the default agent asks for the new default too, with Remove disabled until both are chosen, and is not asked for any other agent. Assign lists the organisation's agents that are not here, with their role, and assigns one. · fulfils AC-225b, AC-225d, AC-226d · tests `hq/src/projects/agents/__tests__/agentsToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-577** — An agent's instructions in a place are read from that place's Files: the organisation's, then the project's AGENTS.md, its skills and the agent's own file there; in Chat, the caller's own Files, saying when a file is missing; in a personal project, its person's own Files. A non-member is refused a project, open or private, before the site is asked; another person's personal project is never read; an unknown agent answers 404. · fulfils AC-226f · tests `hq/src/storage/__tests__/placeInstructions.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-578** — The organisation's agents read says nothing of a private project's work to someone not on it, counts only the projects the person is listed on (not an open one they have not joined), gives an agent's place the person's own conversations and narrowing and nobody else's, and gives the Projects screen's card line what needs a person, what is running and when it last ran. · fulfils AC-227c, AC-227f · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/agentPlaceQueries.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-579** — Usage for an agent and a period counts a run for its person, its project and the organisation, within the period, priced by model and saying how many runs it could not price; an organisation admin also gets the usage by project with people's own spaces summed together and private projects they are not on summed into one line without naming them; a project's usage is refused to someone not on it. · fulfils AC-228f, AC-227d · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/agentPlaceQueries.integration.test.ts`, `usageTotals.test.ts`
- **SC-580** — An agent's place (`/agents/<handle>`) shows the agent, a box that starts a conversation with it in Chat (**SC-496**), and tools: Now with Coming up, Projects, Runs, Usage with two rows — Your usage and Organisation usage — each with its estimate (the organisation's alone on the settings page) and saying when a row has no usage or a model had no price, Your instructions, Memory and Your settings. · fulfils AC-227c, AC-227d · tests `hq/src/agents/pages/__tests__/agentPlaceParts.test.tsx`
- **SC-581** — Organisation › Agents shows only organisation admins its page: a member is told so and sees nothing of it, and nothing is shown while the role is not yet known. An agent's ⋯ menu has Stop its runs and Delete, and no Delete on the built-in agent. · fulfils AC-228a, AC-228e · tests `hq/src/agents/pages/__tests__/agentPlaceParts.test.tsx`
- **SC-582** — An organisation admin reads an agent's assignments: the projects it is in that the admin is a member of, with its narrowing there; a private project the admin is not on is counted without naming it or its narrowing, and a person's own space never; Assign offers only the projects the admin is a member of; anyone who is not an organisation admin is refused. · fulfils AC-228b, AC-228c, AC-228d · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/agentAssignments.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-583** — The shared card of the Agents and Projects screens has a cover above the avatar when it has one, the name, a line, a now-line led by its state, its numbers and one action that runs without opening the card, and no badge; below the wide breakpoint it is a compact row of the avatar and three single lines — who, what it is doing, the facts — and the row is hidden where the card shows. An agent says Needs you with the task that wants something (and how many more), Running with the task and how long, or Idle with what starts next, and says so when it is in no project; a project says Needs you, Running or Quiet with its last run. · fulfils AC-227f · tests `hq/src/agents/pages/__tests__/placeCard.test.tsx`, `agentCardView.test.ts`
- **SC-584** — An agent's card opens from its name, beside what was tapped on a wide screen and as a sheet from the bottom on a phone, shows who it is, what it is doing, its projects and its model, and closes on a click outside it or Escape.
