- **SC-585** — In a project the card shows Permissions here and Its tasks here with their counts, Permissions here opens the agent in the Agents tool at its permissions and closes the card, Its tasks here opens it at its tasks, and New conversation opens the project's message box with the agent picked. In Chat the card shows Your permissions in Chat, and New conversation opens Chat's message box with the agent picked, as it does where there is no place. · fulfils AC-227b · tests `hq/src/agents/profile/__tests__/AgentProfileCard.test.tsx`
- **SC-586** — The agent's card, read where it is opened, gives in a project the project's narrowed count and the agent's open tasks there, in Chat the person's own narrowed count and no tasks, and refuses a project the person is not on. · fulfils AC-227b · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/permissionQueries.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-587** — Chat's Agents fact opens the organisation's agents in the panel, though it has no row of its own among the tools; Chat's Agents tool lists the agents, each opening in the panel, and shows the person's permissions in Chat. · fulfils AC-227e · tests `hq/src/chat/__tests__/chatAgentsFact.test.tsx`, `chatAgentsTool.test.tsx`

## Membership, people and leaving (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-588** — Only a listed member is a member of a project, open or private; an organisation admin who is not listed is not, and a personal project's owner alone is, whatever its access level or member rows say, and not once they have left. An open project is visible to anyone and a private one to its members only; someone else's personal project is never visible; the projects a person is entitled to are the listed ones that are not personal and their own personal project. · fulfils AC-229a, AC-229b, AC-229c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/membershipRules.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-589** — A project's admin passes the checks for managing its people and a plain member does not; an organisation admin manages the people only of a project they can see; a resource is reached through the project that owns it, refusing someone who has not joined an open project, and a non-member of a private project gets the same 404, with the same wording, as for something absent. · fulfils AC-229a, AC-229d, AC-230c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/membershipRules.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-590** — Projects lists every open project and the private ones the person is in, marking which they are in. A non-member sees an open project's preview — what it is, who is in it and how busy it is — with Join, found by key as well as id, and not its work; a private project the person is not in answers as missing, and a member is told they need not join; someone who has left the organisation is left out of the list of people. · fulfils AC-229e, AC-229g · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectPreview.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-591** — Joining an open project makes the person a Member, once however often they ask, and never promotes a Viewer who asks again; a private project, or someone's own, answers as missing. Leaving a project leaves the leaver's open tasks waiting and is refused for the project's last admin. · fulfils AC-229e, AC-229f · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectMembers.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-592** — The preview on screen shows what the project is and how busy it is without its work, joins from the button, and offers no Join where the project cannot be joined. · fulfils AC-229e · tests `hq/src/people/__tests__/peopleViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-593** — A project's admin, and an organisation admin in a project they can see, add people (a Member by default), promote, demote and remove them; a caller who is neither is refused; re-adding the only admin leaves them admin; someone who has left the organisation cannot be added; the project's last admin cannot be demoted or removed, naming what to do first; answering 404 for someone who is not a member. An organisation admin may add themselves to a private project they are not in, as its admin, and nobody else. · fulfils AC-230a, AC-230c, AC-230d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectMembers.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-594** — Removing a person from a project takes where their open tasks go as a required choice — a member, and only a member — and removes nobody without one. A project's access level changes only by its admin, never by an organisation admin, and ends nobody's access either way. · fulfils AC-230d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectMembers.integration.test.ts`, `openTasksDestination.test.ts`
- **SC-595** — Removing a member of an open or a private project holds `memberLeftProject` for each site holding their home and leaves their tasks waiting; it succeeds when the site is slow to take it, the instruction staying held for its reconnect, and a removal that fails holds nothing and blocks nothing. A personal project keeps exactly one member: adding anyone, changing the member's role, the owner leaving it and changing its access level are all refused. · fulfils AC-230f, AC-229c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectMembers.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-596** — A project's People read lists its members, admins first, with what waits on each — on whoever started the run, else the task's person — what they are doing and when they were last here from their conversations and comments; one member reads with their organisation role and when they joined; someone not in the project gets 404, and a non-member is answered as if the project did not exist; the list for adding people offers everyone still in the organisation, marking who is here, with role and project count, to those who may change the people. · fulfils AC-230a, AC-230b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectPeople.integration.test.ts`, `personNow.test.ts`
- **SC-597** — People in a project on screen show each member with their role beside the name and one status line saying who a task waits on or what they are doing and when they were last here, with a count of what waits at the right, a plain row whose button covers it, and a header only when not among other members; Add is offered only to those who may manage, and a person opens from the row. · fulfils AC-230a · tests `hq/src/people/__tests__/peopleViews.test.tsx`, `peopleRows.test.tsx`, `personLines.test.ts`
- **SC-598** — A person's view in the project offers Member, Viewer and Admin and changes the role from the select, offers Leave not Remove for yourself, withholds role, Remove and Leave from the only admin saying why, shows no controls to someone who may not manage, changes no role and removes no one who is here only through a team and says where to change it, and goes back to the view it came from. - **SC-603** — Organisation › People on screen lists waiting invitations first, with Revoke, puts people who left at the foot, searches by name or handle with or without @, offers the organisation roles Admin and Member for others and never for yourself, opens a person from anywhere on the row while the role select stays its own target, shows a person's roles in each project, Viewer among them, and shows nothing to someone who is not an admin. · fulfils AC-231a, AC-231b, AC-231f · tests `hq/src/org/pages/people/__tests__/orgPeoplePages.test.tsx`, `peopleFormat.test.ts`

## An agent's permissions, and who a run is for (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-611** — Permissions are held for an agent at the organisation, at a project (a narrowing) and for a person in their Chat (a narrowing), each with a state per tool — Always, Ask or Never — and an approval; for each tool and approval the strictest wins: Always < Ask < Never, and bypassed < deferred < immediate. A narrowing's Never refuses a tool the organisation allows, its Always never loosens the organisation's Ask or Never, its Ask holds a tool the organisation allows always, and it never grants a tool the organisation's list leaves out. The always-injected tools stay fixed at the organisation, and with no narrowing the organisation's own applies. · fulfils AC-233a, AC-233b · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/effectivePermissions.test.ts`
- **SC-612** — For a command the strictest level wins: a command a pattern blocks only at the narrower level is refused, a narrower Always pattern never loosens an organisation's Ask, the organisation's own patterns keep their order (the first match decides at its level), and a narrower catch-all counts for every command. A command no pattern matches follows Any other command — the Bash tool's own state at that level — running when it is Always, asking when Ask and refused when Never; a pattern only a narrower level names takes the organisation's Any other command. · fulfils AC-233c · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/effectivePermissions.test.ts`, `permissionViews.test.ts`
- **SC-613** — Approval is Bypassed, Deferred or Immediate, loosest first. When the organisation bypasses and nothing narrower sets an approval, every tool is Always, no saved list applies, the organisation's Never is switched off for platform tools too — administrative ones included — and a saved list that omits tools counts them Always; a narrowing that sets Deferred or Immediate brings the tools back, from Always, and applies on top; a workspace's cap on its tools still applies after everything. · fulfils AC-234a, AC-234c · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/effectivePermissions.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/capabilityClaims.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-614** — A run's capability carries the strictest level: a project's Never refuses a tool the organisation allows, a narrowing's Always never loosens the organisation's Ask or Never, with the organisation bypassing and a project at immediate the project's tools are enforced, a person's narrowing applies in their Chat and not in a project, a project's narrowing does not reach a person's Chat, a task session gets its project's narrowing, and a command no pattern matches gets the Bash tool's state at the strictest level. The run is dispatched with the effective tools, so its approved tools are built from what its boundary allows. · fulfils AC-233b, AC-233c, AC-233d · tests `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/capabilityClaims.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/overrides/__tests__/agentOverrideService.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-615** — A person's, or a project's, narrowing of an agent is stored and read back, replacing the stored one rather than adding a second and removed when every field is cleared; it keeps the last state of a repeated tool, takes a missing field as the organisation's and validates a model, an approval, and a state per tool and per pattern; a model that costs more than the agent's own, or that its runtime does not offer, is refused (an approval-only edit may resubmit the stored model); another person's boundary is refused (403), an unknown agent answers 404, nobody signed in 401, an unknown boundary type 400, and a personal project takes none. · fulfils AC-233e · tests `hq/src/agents/overrides/__tests__/overrideOperations.integration.test.ts`, `agentOverrideService.integration.test.ts`, `agentOverrideService.test.ts`, `modelOverrideChoices.test.ts`
- **SC-616** — A project's admin changes the project's narrowing and every member reads it; a member who is not its admin is refused (403), and someone outside the project is refused, an organisation admin included; a private project's overrides are not readable to someone not on it; the agent must be assigned to the project. · fulfils AC-233f · tests `hq/src/agents/overrides/__tests__/overrideOperations.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/permissionQueries.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-617** — The permissions read answers organisation admins with every tool, the approval and a summary per group, and refuses anyone else; for a project it gives every member the organisation's setting beside the project's and a narrowed count, and the project's admins may change it; for a person in Chat it answers only that person. Each tool is in its group, a built-in the list leaves out is unavailable, a platform tool is asked about; a narrower view counts only what is stricter and nothing while the run stays bypassed, and starts every tool at Always when the organisation bypasses. · fulfils AC-233a, AC-235b · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/permissionQueries.integration.test.ts`, `permissionViews.test.ts`
- **SC-618** — The permissions view puts approval first as three cards, Bypassed marked as a warning, then presets, then every tool in groups; the preset that matches is selected, Custom once a tool is changed; Bypassed hides the tool list and asks for confirmation before saving, another approval saves without asking, and a new command pattern saves with the list; it summarises approval, preset and each group, with a warning mark when bypassed. · fulfils AC-234b, AC-235a · tests `hq/src/agents/pages/__tests__/agentPermissionsView.test.tsx`, `permissionsModel.test.ts`

## Execution Sites (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-628** — A persistent notification is raised for each person under Needs you, once per subject: raising it again updates the open one rather than making a second, and counts again on the bell when its title changes; it clears itself when its subject is addressed — one subject, or every subject under a prefix — and the same one reopens, as new, if the problem returns; syncing one type leaves exactly the notifications given open, for exactly those people. · fulfils AC-238a, AC-238b · tests `hq/src/notifications/__tests__/persistent.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-629** — A persistent notification of a kind that may not be dismissed refuses dismissal, which only its subject being addressed clears; a dismissible one may be dismissed and then stays dismissed for that subject. In the Notifications panel an open one sits under Needs you with no ✕ when its kind cannot be dismissed, a dismissible one shows ✕ under Needs you and dismisses, and a fixed one that moved to Earlier shows none. · fulfils AC-238c · tests `hq/src/notifications/__tests__/persistent.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/NotificationPanel/__tests__/persistentNotices.test.tsx`
- **SC-630** — Execution Sites' notifications go to the organisation admins who are still here and to nobody else; an admin made later is told at the next pass, and one no longer an admin stops being. A machine asking to connect is one notification naming it, several requests are one notification, it clears once every request is decided, a long machine name is clipped, and an expired request stays as an expired one, dismissible, until the machine asks again. · fulfils AC-238d, AC-239a, AC-239d · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteNotifications.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-631** — A site that needs upgrading is told at once, with its version and what it stops, clearing when it connects on a matching one; a site not connected while work waits says what waits and clears when it connects, and says nothing for a site away with nothing waiting; HQ's own site not running is told once past the grace and clears when it connects, and not while hq has only just started; Docker not running is told and cleared by the next good report, a nearly full disk (under 10 GiB free, or under 10 % of a disk under 100 GiB) is told, and a malformed report is dropped, keeping the last good one. · fulfils AC-239a, AC-239b, AC-239c · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteNotifications.integration.test.ts`, `siteStanding.test.ts`
- **SC-632** — A site's standing decides where it is listed — Running when connected with nothing wrong, Never connected once approved and not yet heard from, Not connected when away with nothing waiting, Removed unless projects are still on it — and what needs an admin: needing an upgrade at once, work waiting past the grace, HQ's own site not running past the grace (counted from hq's own start, which forgets every connection), Docker down or a nearly full disk from a connected site's report and not from the last report of one that is away; it is undecided for a site away less than the grace and when hq has only just started. · fulfils AC-239b, AC-239c · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteStanding.test.ts`
- **SC-633** — There is one notification for one site and one problem: it stays open, the same one, through a restart of hq while the site is still down and while the site drops again moments after it was last heard from; a problem that returns after it was fixed reopens the same notification under Needs you; a missed event cannot leave one stuck, because a removed site has none at the next pass. · fulfils AC-238b, AC-238e · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteNotifications.integration.test.ts`

## A project's Settings (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-639** — A project's Settings make each part a field only for those who may change it: the project's own fields (name, key, description, lead, access, default agent, where it runs, runs and finished tasks) for its admins, filing under a product or programme and deleting for organisation admins, and text for everyone else, with the project's admins named (one, two or three) and nothing about deleting; a personal project keeps only its own fields. The key is a field until the project has a task, then fixed text saying why ("Fixed — its 3 tasks are numbered KEY-1 onwards"), and must be 2 to 6 letters. Each part saves on its own when the field is left, saying Saved, sends nothing when nothing changed, refuses a value that fails its check without calling the server, and shows a server refusal beside the part keeping what was typed. The default agent is chosen among the project's own agents, or none: a project admin may set and clear it, an agent not in the project is refused (400) leaving the default as it was, and a member who is not an admin is refused (403). · fulfils AC-242a, AC-242b, AC-242c, AC-242d, AC-242e, AC-242f · tests `hq/src/projects/pages/settings/__tests__/settingsRights.test.ts`, `TextPart.test.tsx`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectDefaultAgent.integration.test.ts`


## Approvals, runs, organisation, conversation and getting in (added 2026-10-12)

The unit *Agenthix is usable — every screen reviewed*, from *an approval is for the next run only* (plan 3.27) to *logs and diagnostics* (plan 3.40). Scenarios here describe hq as it is built; those that the unit's earlier scenarios (**SC-489** to **SC-534**) overlap are named in the changes at the end.

### An approval is for the next run only

- **SC-640** — Approving a task's tool request marks it approved on the site that holds it first, then queues the task's next run as the person who approved it at depth 0 — keeping what started the earlier run — tells its board, and starts that run only after both; that run alone is given what was approved, and the run after it asks again. · fulfils AC-250a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifestApproval.integration.test.ts`, `sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-641** — Approving a conversation's request marks it approved on the site that holds it and starts the conversation's next run with a hidden "Please continue." — as the conversation's owner, or, in a task's session, as the sender of the message that started the earlier run, and not once that sender has left the project; denying starts the next run, hidden, told what was denied and why, so the agent answers without it, and sends no message of the person's. A site that says the request is no longer pending answers 409 and starts nothing. · fulfils AC-250a, AC-250d · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifestApproval.integration.test.ts`, `sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-642** — Only the newest run's request can be answered. One the site marked replaced, one that a newer run of its task or conversation overtook (it is then marked replaced), and one whose task has a newer run queued or going are refused, approving or denying alike, with 409 "This request was replaced by something newer in its task or conversation, so it can no longer be approved or denied."; nothing starts, nothing already going is overwritten, and no comment is written. The task's approval card shows a replaced request without Approve or Deny. · fulfils AC-250b · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifestApproval.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/components/__tests__/ManifestApprovalComment.test.tsx`
- **SC-643** — Anything newer replaces an unanswered request: Dismiss and Run again mark it replaced on the site and handle its notification, before a new run starts; a message into a task's session replaced it as its run started, and a task held only for that approval is released with no run pending; failing to reach the site is logged and never undoes what the person did. The replaced request leaves Needs you (a tool-request notification of a task that is no longer blocked, or whose newest run is not pending approval, is handled when the list is read), the Runs panel and the Runs screen (finished, not waiting), and the conversation's card reads "Not answered — replaced by a newer message", without a reload. · fulfils AC-250c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskRunControls.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/notifications/__tests__/staleRequests.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/runs/__tests__/getMyRuns.integration.test.ts`, `runsView.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/ChatPanel/__tests__/PanelChatView.approval.test.tsx`
- **SC-644** — A run that asked for approval and was also stopped at its time or turn limit leaves its task blocked with both reasons, "Awaiting tool approval — <the limit>", so the person sees why it stopped as well as what it wants. · fulfils AC-250e · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/taskRunEnd.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-645** — A conversation's request is answered by the conversation's owner alone (anyone else is refused with 403 and nothing starts), and approving it also needs the owner still to belong to the project the conversation is bound to ("You no longer have access to this project"); a task's request is answered by anyone on its project who may work (see **SC-521**), and an outsider is refused. · fulfils AC-250a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifest.integration.test.ts`, `manifestEntitlement.integration.test.ts`

### The Runs screen

- **SC-646** — The Runs screen is at `/runs`, reached from **Show all ›** in the Runs panel; one run is at `/runs/<run>?session=<its conversation's or task's session>`, the session telling hq which site to ask. At a desktop, arriving opens the newest run (replacing nothing the address already names); on a phone the list is shown alone, then a run alone with a way back to Runs. A run with no session in its address, or one the person may not see, reads "This run isn't available". An admin, and no one else, is offered Stop all. · fulfils AC-251a, AC-251d, AC-251f, AC-252e · tests `hq/src/runs/pages/__tests__/RunsPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunPane.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/components/RunsPanel/__tests__/RunsPanelList.siteUnreachable.test.tsx`
- **SC-647** — The list holds the person's runs — those of their own conversations, and of tasks they started or that are done on their behalf, in projects they can see, never another person's — newest first, 25 to a page with **Older runs ›**, in the groups Waiting for you, Running, Queued and Finished. A queued task stands at the top of the first page only. A page asks each site once for all the sessions it covers, never once per conversation or task, and reads on up to four times when the filters leave it short. A site that could not be asked is named once above the list, and its runs are missing from it. · fulfils AC-251a, AC-251e · tests `hq/src/runs/__tests__/runsScreen.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunsList.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/components/RunsPanel/__tests__/RunsPanelList.siteUnreachable.test.tsx`
- **SC-648** — The list narrows by whose doing it was (All of mine, Started by me, On my behalf), place (a project, or Chat), agent, how it ended (Completed, Failed, Stopped) and when (24 hours, 7 days, 30 days), and by a search of the task's or conversation's title, a task key such as WEB-27 included; a value hq does not know is no narrowing, never an error. With nothing to show it says "No runs yet" and offers a conversation; under filters it says "No runs match" and clears them. · fulfils AC-251b · tests `hq/src/runs/__tests__/screenFilters.test.ts`, `runsScreen.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunsList.test.tsx`
- **SC-649** — A row is two lines and one action: the task's key and title or the conversation's, then its agent, place, how it ended (a failure with its reason in plain words) or why it waits, how long it took, how many risky calls (High and Flagged) it made, and who started it when that was not the person; a waiting run's one button is **Approve** where the person may work in its place, which approves at once and opens the run if that fails. · fulfils AC-251c · tests `hq/src/runs/__tests__/runFormat.test.ts`, `runsView.test.ts`, `taskFacts.test.ts`, `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunsList.test.tsx`
- **SC-650** — One run opened shows its title (with the task's key), **Open the task** or **Open the conversation**, **Stop** while it goes and **Approve** and **Deny** while its request can still be answered (where the person may work in its place), how it ended and when, and its facts: agent and place, who started it and for whom, its site (HQ's own as "HQ · Built-in site"), the approval, runtime and model, usage, and the error of a run that failed. · fulfils AC-252a, AC-252d · tests `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunPane.test.tsx`, `hq/src/runs/__tests__/runsScreen.integration.test.ts`, `taskFacts.test.ts`
- **SC-651** — The Approval fact words what the run asked for and how that was answered: "Asked for <what> — waiting for you", "— approved by <name> at <time>, for the next run", "— denied …" or "— replaced by something newer"; the run that followed an approval shows what the earlier run's approval gave it and who approved. · fulfils AC-252b · tests `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunPane.test.tsx`, `hq/src/runs/__tests__/runsScreen.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-652** — A run lists every tool call it made, in order, each with its risk label, and narrows to the High and Flagged ones; a call opens to its input and the output found by the call's own id, cut to its first lines when long (the browser is sent up to 1,000,000 characters, and told when more was cut); a call from before ids were kept reads "Output not recorded"; a call that asked first says who approved it. · fulfils AC-252c · tests `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/ToolCalls.test.tsx`, `hq/src/runs/__tests__/runsScreen.integration.test.ts`, `runFormat.test.ts`

### The Projects screen

### A routine's round

- **SC-692** — The message box stays open while the agent works, with **Stop** beside Send. - **SC-693** — **Send now** stops the running reply for a waiting message, recorded as interrupted by the sender, and the waiting message goes next, other waiting messages staying; **Cancel** withdraws a waiting message before its run starts and it leaves the conversation, and only its sender can. - **SC-694** — A failed run stays in the conversation where its reply would be, with its reason in plain words ("The site lost its connection to the model." — never a raw error) and one **Try again** while it is the latest reply; once a newer message is sent it folds to one line, "Failed · …", with no button. - **SC-695** — **Try again** sends the same message again in a fresh run, hidden, with a note only the agent sees that its last attempt did not finish; it is the sender's run, refused once a newer message was sent, while the agent is working, and to anyone who may not send into the conversation (as if it did not exist). · fulfils AC-264b · tests `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/conversationActions.integration.test.ts`, `conversationRecord.test.ts`

Once answered it folds to one line ("Approved by Avery · <time>") that opens to the same card, "for this run only", with View on the run ›; one a newer message replaced reads "Not answered — replaced by a newer message", and it clears without a reload when a newer run starts. · fulfils AC-266a, AC-266b, AC-266c · tests `hq/src/shared/components/ChatPanel/__tests__/PanelChatView.records.test.tsx`, `PanelChatView.approval.test.tsx`, `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/conversationRecord.test.ts`

### Which model access a run uses

- **SC-701** — ~~A run uses model access in this order: its project's own (a run in a project), then the person's own login, then the person's own API key, then the organisation's — in a project as in Chat. A project's subscription beats its own key only in a one-person organisation; in a larger one the project's key is used and its subscription skipped.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — the model and provider are chosen by the one resolver (**SC-1192**), and the key by the provider the choice names (**SC-1170**).
- **SC-702** — ~~The person a run is for is the one **SC-520** names: whoever started it, or sent the message into the task's conversation, or else the person of the task (for a run nobody started) or of the routine; a routine's run in a shared project uses its person's key and in their personal project their login; a run on behalf of nobody uses the organisation's alone.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — a run's person decides whose subscription and lists apply, not an order of credentials (**SC-1192**, **SC-1197**).
- **SC-703** — ~~A person's own Claude login goes only to a run that runs as them — their Chat, their personal project, their own conversation in a project — never to another member's conversation, and the grant is recorded against their login so a refreshed token comes back to theirs. A task's run in a project runs as the project in a shared home and is given the person's own API key instead, never the login, and a message that cannot find a key says why the login was not used.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — see **SC-1197**.
- **SC-704** — ~~A person's own accepted Claude subscription is used on the Claude runtime ahead of their key, at any organisation size, and never offered to OpenCode; the organisation's or a project's subscription is used only while the organisation has exactly one person; a credential stored in the clear is not used. The development switch `HQ_DEV_SHARED_SUBSCRIPTION` shares one subscription among users in a development build only, is ignored in any other build (production included), and hq logs an error at start when it is set but ignored.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — see **SC-1197**; the organisation and a project hold no subscription.

## The user guide's screenshots and printed compose commands (added 2026-10-12)

The user guide (`docs/guide/`) is content, not behaviour; what is promised here is the tooling that makes its screenshots (`hq/scripts/guide/`, `hq/e2e/guide/`, the `guide:screenshots` and `guide:compare` scripts, the two guide workflows) and the settings file named in every printed `docker compose` command.

- **SC-800** — The name check (`node scripts/guide/check-names.mjs`, the first step of `guide:screenshots`) reads each screenshot's name and tag from its release address (`https://github.com/solution84-com/agenthix/releases/download/<tag>/<name>.png`) in the guide's pages and the root `README.md`, in a Markdown image or an HTML attribute, and fails, naming the page, for a linked name that no recipe in `hq/e2e/guide/` saves, and for a page still using the old `screenshot:<name>` form. · fulfils AC-284a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`, `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/screenshotLinks.test.mjs`
- **SC-801** — The name check fails, naming the recipe, for a recipe that no guide page or the README links. · fulfils AC-284a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`
- **SC-802** — The name check fails for a screenshot name that is not lowercase words joined by hyphens, reporting it once whether a page or a recipe carries it, for two recipes that save the same name, for a name linked to two release tags (naming the pages), and for a link whose tag is not a release tag; it is quiet when pages and recipes match. · fulfils AC-284a, AC-284b · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`, `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/screenshotLinks.test.mjs`
- **SC-803** — A recipe's screenshot name is its file name without the `.recipe.ts` suffix and without a leading `fresh-` (a recipe that needs hq with no organisation yet); a file that is not a recipe has no name. · fulfils AC-284b · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`, `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/recipeName.test.ts`
- **SC-804** — `guide:compare` compares each captured screenshot with its picture in the release its link names, or, when that release does not hold it, in the release the last release's pages linked it to, and sorts it as **changed** (the images differ, or differ in size), **new** (no such release holds it) or unchanged; a name the last release's pages linked that this run did not capture is **removed**. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs`
- **SC-805** — The comparison ignores a difference of 50 pixels or fewer (colour distance under 0.1) and reports a larger one as changed. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs`
- **SC-806** — With no earlier release tag (`v*`) the comparison fetches nothing: every screenshot is new, and the summary says there is no earlier release to compare with; with a tag, the last release is the nearest `v*` tag before the current commit, and its pages' links are read at that tag. · fulfils AC-285b · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs`
- **SC-807** — The comparison writes a summary that names the last release, lists the changed (with how many pixels differ and the release compared with), new and removed names and counts the unchanged, and an HTML report showing the old and new image of each changed screenshot side by side. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs` (the summary; the HTML report has no automated test)
- **SC-808** — The guide databases are the developer's own servers with only the database name swapped (`agenthix_guide` for hq, `agenthix_site_guide` for the site); where there is no developer file (CI) the addresses given in `GUIDE_HQ_DATABASE_URL` and `GUIDE_SITE_DATABASE_URL` are used as given. · fulfils AC-284c · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/databaseUrls.test.ts`
