A refused action of the person's own shows the server's reason in one shared way. · fulfils AC-327d
- **SC-1019** — ~~A chat from before the conversations held in hq (a chat session with no task and no conversation; a task's session whose task was deleted is not one) is gone from the person's view: no list shows it — a place's conversations, the Runs panel and screen — and opening one by its session address (`/sessions/:id`) goes to Chat, showing nothing of it. Anything sent into one — a message, voice, Try again, Summarise now, Send now, Cancel, an answer to a tool request — is refused, saying it is closed and to start a new conversation, before anything reaches its site; no chat starts any more; for anyone but its owner it does not exist. Nothing of it is converted or carried into a new conversation. A task's session still opens and takes a message, and a conversation's standing worker still gets its briefs. · fulfils AC-316a~~ **Retired 2026-10-18** — nothing has been released, so no install holds a chat from before the conversations held in hq; the code that refused sends into one, and the listing and address cases around it, is removed. A task's session and a conversation's standing worker are unchanged.

## The desktop shell (added 2026-10-18)

The unit *the desktop shell*: the shell is drawn once above the pages; a place's sections are the **left panel**, which drills into the place, and the **canvas** (the page area beside it) is the section; and one conversation is **docked** on the right of every page — the **dock** — until the person changes it. The tools panel, the layouts and the small-screen place page are retired (**SC-501**, **SC-505**, **SC-506**); the scenarios this unit changed in place are **SC-011**, **SC-150**, **SC-152**, **SC-224**, **SC-225**, **SC-249**, **SC-278**, **SC-280**, **SC-495**, **SC-496**, **SC-499**, **SC-507** to **SC-509**, **SC-516**, **SC-492**, **SC-1001** and **SC-1012**. The conversation itself — its thread, message box, pickers and work bar — is unchanged.

### The shell, drawn once

- **SC-1020** — The shell — the navigation, the screen bar, the notices, the voice bar, the Notifications and Runs panels and the dock — is drawn once, above the pages: moving between pages keeps it mounted, so the navigation does not reset, a collapsed navigation stays collapsed, the drawer closes on navigating, the screen bar shows each page's breadcrumb and bar buttons, and the browser tab is named after the page; a page that shows no shell (signing in) has no frame. · fulfils AC-329e · tests `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/RootShell.test.tsx`

### A place's navigation

- **SC-1021** — A project, your space and an agent's page show their sections in the left panel, which drills into the place in place of the main navigation: ‹, the place's avatar and name, then every section with its count in its tone, the one shown marked, under small group labels and with nothing nested, in a fixed order the person cannot change — a project Overview, Conversations, Work (Tasks, Boards, Timeline), Agents at work (Runs, Automation), Files (Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces), then Members, Settings and Memory (admins only) under *Project*; your space Overview, Conversations, Tasks, Boards, Runs, Automation, Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces and Agents; an agent's page Overview, Conversations, then *At work* (Now, Projects, Runs, Usage) and *About it* (Your instructions, Memory, Your settings). A label is never repeated. The canvas has no header or row of its own: the chosen section fills it at full height and full width, a view inside it drawing no second shell. The place's own address opens Overview; any other section is `?tool=<section>` in the address, so a link opens it and Back returns to the section before; a tool the place does not have opens Overview, and a former address for a section (`?tool=people`, `?tool=agents`) opens its section. Leaving the place for a page with no sections puts the main navigation back. · fulfils AC-329a · tests `hq/src/places/__tests__/placeSections.test.ts`, `placeNav.test.tsx`, `PlaceScreen.test.tsx`, `PlaceScreenDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/AppSidebarDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/drilledShell.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/placeNavigation.spec.ts`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1022** — At icon width — a collapsed navigation, or a medium window (1024 to 1279 px) — the place's panel shows ‹, the place's avatar and one icon per section, its name on hover and its count as a badge, with Pinned as avatars; ‹ opens the main navigation at full panel width over the canvas (**SC-1043**). Below 1024 px the drawer is the full panel whatever the collapsed setting, and the phone header names the section with its place's name beside it: its title lists the place's sections, with their counts and the one shown marked, in a popup that a choice or Escape closes, and a choice from the drawer closes the drawer. · fulfils AC-329b · tests `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/AppSidebarDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/places/__tests__/PlaceScreenDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/drilledShell.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/placeNavigation.spec.ts`, `hq/e2e/mobile-responsive.spec.ts`
- **SC-1023** — A place's Overview shows its facts under a heading (a project's: **SC-1046**); for a project, Members (its people and agents) as a row after them with no heading; then its sections in groups, each with a short count or state — for a project Work (Tasks as "N needs you · M", Boards, Timeline), Agents at work (Runs as "N running", Automation On or Off, then Run all and Stop all, **SC-503**) and Files (Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces), with Settings alone at the foot. - **SC-1024** — The head of the place's panel is the place: ‹, the avatar (an agent's own), the name — which opens Overview — and the pin (an agent pins by its own kind; your space has no pin), once, whichever section is shown; the canvas has no header of its own. The screen bar's right end holds *Talk about <project>* (*Talk in your space*, *Talk to <agent>*): on a project and in your space it starts a new conversation about the place in the dock (**SC-1035**), the canvas staying on its section; on an agent's page it starts one in the dock with that agent picked; with no dock it opens the place's Conversations section. · fulfils AC-329c · tests `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/AppSidebarDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/places/__tests__/PlaceScreen.test.tsx`, `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockActions.test.tsx`, `dockDraft.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`

- **SC-1044** — In a place the breadcrumb reads *Home ▾ / Projects ▾ / <place> ▾ / <section> ▾* (an agent's: the agent's name opens no list), and each part opens a list: Home the main navigation, Projects the places, the place's name the person's projects with their space — pinned first, the current one marked, and switching keeps the section — and the section the place's sections with their counts, the one shown marked. A choice or Escape closes the list, and Escape returns focus to the part. · fulfils AC-329g · tests `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/CrumbLists.test.tsx`, `hq/src/places/__tests__/placeNav.test.tsx`, `PlaceScreenDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/placeNavigation.spec.ts`

### The docked conversation

- **SC-1026** — One conversation at a time is open in the dock, on the right of every page, and stays open wherever the person goes — another project, Tasks, Settings, Home — until they open another, start a new one or close it. It is named in the address as `?c=<id>` on whatever page the canvas shows, written back onto every page a link leads to without adding to the history, so a reload and Back restore both, and an address naming another conversation (Back included) opens that one; with none open there is no dock and the address is left as it is. · fulfils AC-330a · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockAddresses.test.tsx`, `dockAddress.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1027** — A conversation's own address (`/chat/t/<id>`, `/projects/<key>/t/<id>`) is kept for every list and link, and is replaced at once by the `?c=` form: reached from inside hq — a row in a list, a box that has just started one, Home's box, an agent's page — it opens the conversation in the dock and the canvas stays as it was, with its scroll and filters, never redrawn, at every width; reached on a fresh load (a pasted link, a bookmark) the canvas shows the conversation's place on its Conversations section — the Chat page for your space, the project's Conversations section — with the conversation in the dock, open whatever the width. Under the shell a conversation is never drawn in the canvas. · fulfils AC-330b · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockAddresses.test.tsx`, `dockAddress.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/pages/__tests__/threadPagesDocked.test.tsx`, `hq/src/chat/__tests__/placeConversationAddresses.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1028** — The dock's header shows where the conversation lives — your space, its project, or its task by title — as a chip that opens that place in the canvas, and its buttons 🕘, ＋, ⤢ and ›; it has no title of its own, since the thread's own header carries it. The open conversation's row is marked as current (`aria-current`) in any list on the canvas. A link in the conversation — a task, a board, a run — opens in the canvas and the conversation stays. · fulfils AC-330b, AC-330c · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockSurvival.test.tsx`, `dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/src/conversations/components/__tests__/NewConversationsList.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1029** — Because the dock is drawn once, the same thread stays mounted through every change of the canvas — its live subscription, a reply being written, its scroll and unsent text — and through expanding and collapsing; a reply keeps streaming while the person moves around. · fulfils AC-330c · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockSurvival.test.tsx`, `dockActions.test.tsx`
- **SC-1030** — Deleting or archiving the open conversation from the dock closes the dock, through the thread's own report that it was deleted or archived, and leaves the canvas where it was. · fulfils AC-330g · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockSurvival.test.tsx`, `hq/src/conversations/components/__tests__/ConversationThreadCallbacks.test.tsx`
- **SC-1031** — 🕘 lists up to eight of the person's conversations in the place the canvas is on — a project, for anything under its address or a task's, else your space — newest activity first, each with its place and time and the open one marked, or says there are none in that place yet; choosing one opens it in the dock, and *All conversations →* opens the Chat page. · fulfils AC-330d · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1032** — *Over the page*, the first entry of the conversation's ⋯ menu, puts the chat panel over the canvas, the navigation staying, and reads *Back beside the page* while it is, putting it back; the menu is shown for that entry alone when the person has no other, and the entry is not offered on a phone, where the panel is the whole screen. Going anywhere in the canvas drops it back beside the page, while opening another conversation keeps it over; a file open in the viewer keeps it beside, the entry greyed with why in its tooltip (**SC-1875**). *(Changed 2026-11-11: the ⋯ menu's entry in place of ⤢.)* · fulfils AC-330e, AC-458b · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1033** — One button at the top of the chat's bar minimises the panel, leaving only the bar, and opens it again where the person left it, beside or over the page (beside while a file is open, and never over on a phone); it has the selected fill while the panel is open and its label and tooltip say what it will do (*Minimise the chat (⌘J)*, *Open the chat (⌘J)*), and ⌘J (Ctrl+J) does the same. The thread stays mounted while minimised, with its unsent text; the panel stays minimised when the canvas moves, and opening a conversation from a list opens it. *(Changed 2026-11-11: one button in the bar, in place of › and the rail.)* · fulfils AC-330e, AC-458a · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/src/conversations/components/__tests__/ConversationThreadCallbacks.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1034** — The dock's width is dragged on its inner edge while it sits beside or over the canvas — not expanded, put away or on a phone — between 400 px (its default) and the smaller of 720 px and half the window. It is remembered in the browser when the drag ends and read on load; a window too small for it narrows what is shown without changing what is remembered; a remembered value that is not a number is ignored, and blocked storage only means it is not remembered. The edge is a focusable separator: the arrow keys change the width in steps of 24 px, Home and End go to the limits, and a double click resets it; dragging selects no text on the page and never redraws the conversation. · fulfils AC-330f · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockResize.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/dockResize.spec.ts`

### A new conversation

- **SC-1035** — ＋ in the dock, or *Talk about <place>* in the screen bar, puts a draft of a new conversation in the dock, the canvas staying: its header reads *New conversation* with Cancel in place of 🕘 ＋ ⤢ ›. The conversation that was open stays mounted, hidden, underneath; Cancel or Escape puts back exactly that conversation, in the position it had (docked, expanded or collapsed), with its unsent text, or closes the dock when nothing was open; text written in the draft is discarded. Back from a draft returns to the conversation that was open. · fulfils AC-331a, AC-331c · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockDraft.test.tsx`, `dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`

The place the canvas is on is selected and the message box has focus; only the selected place shows its explanation (a project's: *A project. Works in its files, with its agents. Choosing another place gives it a fresh message box whose agent picker lists that place's agents. ### A reply on screen

- **SC-1041** — A reply being written keeps streaming on screen when its turn writes an action line, a task card or a line saying work started before the reply ends; the streamed text is replaced only by the turn's own reply or its failure, and a later turn's or a person's message leaves it. · fulfils AC-330c, AC-316b · tests `hq/src/conversations/hooks/__tests__/threadState.test.ts`, `useConversationThread.test.tsx`

## Connecting HQ to Agenthix Cloud, and signing in with an Agenthix account

HQ connects outward to Cloud by the connection protocol in [`core.md`](core.md) (SC-1082); it never calls Cloud unless it is connecting or connected. The Fleet Console's side is in [`console.md`](console.md) and [`console-web.md`](console-web.md).

- **SC-1086** — Connecting asks Cloud for a connection with HQ's public key, its address and the organisation's name, and shows the user code to check and the page to approve on; an approval page off Cloud's own origin is refused and nothing waits. · fulfils AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`
- **SC-1087** — HQ's private key never leaves HQ — nothing sent to Cloud carries it — and is kept encrypted; HQ signs for short-lived access tokens and keeps each until shortly before it lapses; Cloud's own check refuses a replayed assertion, one signed by another key and a device code that lapsed; and HQ calls Cloud only while connecting or connected. · fulfils AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudStores.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1088** — HQ polls until the connection is approved, then keeps it and is connected as Cloud's organisation; it slows down when Cloud says so; a refusal ends the request and says so; a request not approved in time lapses and polling stops; Cloud briefly away keeps the request waiting and away until it lapses fails it; asking again while one waits answers the same request and once connected is refused. · fulfils AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`
- **SC-1089** — Disconnecting revokes the connection at Cloud and forgets the connection, the key and the token — also when Cloud is away, so the key can never be used again; a request still waiting is dropped and polled no more; when Cloud ends the connection from its side, the next token request is refused and HQ forgets it; connecting again uses a new key. · fulfils AC-337b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`
- **SC-1090** — The connection is one record while connected and none after; *(Changed 2026-10-23: the Fleet Console no longer connects: a fleet admin reads the state, and connecting and disconnecting are refused with where to do them; an organisation admin, a fleet admin or not, connects and drops the request from HQ (**SC-1179**); a member may do none of it and Cloud is asked nothing; Cloud busy is refused with 429, saying when to try again; and disconnecting is refused on an HQ that Cloud runs.)* · fulfils AC-337b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudStores.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1091** — Signing in with an Agenthix account is off while HQ is not connected, and Cloud is never asked; connected, HQ sends the browser to Cloud asking only for `openid`, with PKCE S256, a state, a nonce and HQ's own return address, then signs a linked person in with HQ's own session, the code exchanged with a signed request and the verifier. · fulfils AC-338b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts`
- **SC-1092** — A sign-in with Cloud is refused for an account not linked (saying to link it from Settings first, making no account) and for someone who has left; says so when the person said no on Cloud; and is bound to the tab that started it: a state HQ never gave, a return without this tab's binding, a state used twice, a return after ten minutes, another sign-in's code and a connection Cloud says it ended are refused. · fulfils AC-338b, AC-338c · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts`
- **SC-1093** — Back from Cloud, HQ's page hands HQ the code with the tab's binding, keeps the session HQ made, and goes Home, or back to Settings once an account is linked; it shows HQ's words with the way back to sign in when the account is not linked, and sends an empty binding from a tab that did not start the sign-in, so HQ refuses it. · fulfils AC-338b, AC-338c · tests `hq/src/auth/pages/__tests__/CloudReturnPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1094** — In Settings › Sign-in the Agenthix account says it is not available while HQ is not connected; linking asks for the password first and starts nothing without the right one, keeps the account's subject with the person so signing in with it then signs them in, finishes only for the person who started it, and refuses an account already linked to someone else; one account links to one person, linking again replaces the link, unlinking ends it, and a person who has left reads as not active. · fulfils AC-338a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts` (the describe *linking, in Settings › Sign-in*), `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudAccounts.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/CloudAccountRow.test.tsx`
- **SC-1095** — Disconnecting tells each linked person still here, before the key is revoked, that they sign in with their HQ password from now on, and sign-in with Cloud is off afterwards; a change to HQ itself names the linked accounts of everyone still here. · fulfils AC-338d · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts` (the describe *disconnecting*), `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudAccounts.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1096** — For a booked change HQ asks Cloud, with a token it signed, to email the linked members the change affects, sends a new time again under the same id, withdraws a change called off, and signs for a fresh token when Cloud does not take the one held; it asks nothing for other updates, nothing when no affected member has linked an account, and nothing while HQ is not connected. · fulfils AC-339a, AC-339b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/downtimeEmail.test.ts`
- **SC-1097** — `site.register` from Cloud takes the site's credential by its SHA-256 hash only, shows the site as Cloud's, and lets the site in by it; asked again for the same machine it answers the same site and changes nothing; it is a conflict when another site has the name or the credential is another site's, and refuses what is not a hash or an empty name; `site.unregister` retires the site and forgets its credential, is safe to repeat, lets the same machine register again with its site taking back its own id, and never touches a site that is not Cloud's; nobody sees a Cloud site's figures. · fulfils AC-334e · tests `hq/src/fleet/__tests__/cloudSites.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteFigures.test.ts`
- **SC-1098** — `account.firstLink` from Cloud makes a first-admin link while HQ has no account, each ending the one before, never logged, and kept with its reference on the account it makes; once an account exists it answers who was made first and never a link; `account.resetLink` makes a one-time link for an active organisation admin, announced to every admin as made from Cloud, and refuses a member, someone who left and no one at all in the same words; `account.adminInvite` makes an admin invitation only while no active organisation admin is left and announces it; no link is made for an address that is not https; the control-socket commands pass what they read and answer the result as data, never a link in the text, and refuse what is not well formed. · fulfils AC-334e · tests `hq/src/auth/machine/__tests__/cloudAccountLinks.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/auth/machine/__tests__/cloudMachineCommands.test.ts`
- **SC-1099** — HQ's answers to the Fleet Console about the connection name the fleet admin asking and pass on nothing else, and the record of what fleet admins did words connecting HQ to Cloud and disconnecting it with the organisation it was connected as. · fulfils AC-337a, AC-337b · tests `hq/src/fleet/__tests__/consoleMethods.test.ts`, `hq/src/fleet/__tests__/activityWords.test.ts`

## Phones and other clients

- **SC-1100** — A phone's session slides forward with use: its expiry moves to 30 days from now, written at most once an hour per session; a session that has lapsed is not brought back; a session that is not a phone is left alone. A phone paired with a code, or a session a phone registers after signing in with a password, is marked as a phone with the name it gave. · fulfils AC-340a · tests `hq/src/mobile/__tests__/deviceSessions.test.ts`, `hq/src/mobile/__tests__/deviceSessions.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1101** — Settings › Devices and connections › Phone lists the person's own phones — device name and when last used — and Sign out ends one at once, closing its sockets; no one else's phone is listed or can be signed out; with none it says so, and a refusal is shown in plain words. · fulfils AC-340b, AC-340c · tests `hq/src/mobile/__tests__/deviceSessions.test.ts`, `hq/src/mobile/__tests__/deviceSessions.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/mobile/pages/SignedInPhones.test.tsx`
- **SC-1102** — A client acknowledges a ping at once (a ping with no callback is ignored), and a signed-in client reads HQ's version over its connection — a socket with no person gets nothing; `/health` keeps answering without it (**SC-723**). On connecting, a socket joins its person's room and its session's and renews the session, and a socket with no person joins nothing. · fulfils AC-341a, AC-341b · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientEvents.test.ts`
- **SC-1103** — An event for one person goes to that person's room only; with no one named it goes to the people with a socket open, and each named person is told once. · fulfils AC-341c · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientEvents.test.ts`

## The guide and its screenshots, for a pre-release

- **SC-1104** — Each Fleet Console recipe (`hq/e2e/guide/console-<name>.recipe.ts`) opens its screen through `captureConsole`, which serves the Fleet Console's built screens and answers its API from a sample of the same organisation typed with `@agenthix/core`'s types, and names its test and picture after its file; the name check covers both apps' recipes. A change to the API that the sample no longer matches fails `typecheck:guide`. · fulfils AC-342a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/consoleRecipes.test.ts` (the typed sample is checked by `typecheck:guide`, which has no test of its own)
- **SC-1105** — The guide's administrator is a fleet admin, the sample's dates follow the sample's fixed date alone, and the server's clock text and free-space figures are masked in a screenshot of HQ, so a picture does not change from one run to the next. · fulfils AC-342b · no automated test (seed and capture settings; proved by running the capture)
- **SC-1106** — The guide commands run HQ with its files in a directory of their own: the caller's `HQ_STORAGE_ROOT`, else a directory under the temporary one. · fulfils AC-342b · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/databaseUrls.test.ts` (the describe *where hq keeps its files for the guide*)
- **SC-1107** — A pre-release's screenshots are taken like a release's, in the release build: *Guide screenshots* is called by *Release build* — on every push to `release/**` and every run by hand for a commit — and keeps the set when it passes, and no longer runs for a tag; a pre-release tag (`v<version>-alpha.N`, `-beta.N` or `-rc.N`) carries its new and changed screenshots like any release tag, from the release build run the tag promoted its images from (**SC-1268**, **SC-1926**). · fulfils AC-343a · no automated test (workflows)
- **SC-1108** — Every workflow is checked with `actionlint` and `shellcheck` on each pull request or push to `main` that changes one, and fails on a finding. · fulfils AC-343b · no automated test (a workflow; proved by running it)

## What the platform shows of Agenthix Cloud

- **SC-1109** — The sign-in page always carries *Sign in with Agenthix Cloud*: while HQ is not connected it is greyed out with its reason beneath and carries no link to Cloud; connected, it starts the sign-in at HQ and goes where HQ answers, keeping the binding for this tab (**SC-1091**). · fulfils AC-344a · tests `hq/src/auth/pages/__tests__/LoginPage.test.tsx` (the describe *Sign in with Agenthix Cloud*)
- **SC-1110** — Beside an invitation or setup the option is greyed out while HQ is not connected — in setup with a reason that points to after setup — and when connected it says to accept with a username and password first and starts nothing. · fulfils AC-344b · tests `hq/src/auth/pages/__tests__/CloudSignInOption.test.tsx`
- **SC-1111** — In Settings › Sign-in, while HQ is not connected, the Agenthix account says what it would give and offers *Ask a fleet admin to turn it on*; the ask is kept once and for good (a second changes nothing), then it reads *You asked* with no button; if the ask cannot be sent it says so and keeps the button; it is refused while HQ is connected and for someone not signed in; and it offers no Link until HQ is connected. · fulfils AC-344c · tests `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/CloudAskRow.test.tsx`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/CloudAccountRow.test.tsx` (the first test), `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudForMembers.integration.test.ts` (the describe *a member asking…*)
- **SC-1112** — The people who asked are counted without those who have since left or been erased, and nothing is counted when nobody asked; an offer is dismissed per person and per offer, for good, only a fleet admin who is still here can dismiss one, only the three known offers exist, and a stored offer it does not know is ignored. · fulfils AC-346a, AC-346c · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/platformFacts.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudForMembers.integration.test.ts` (the describe *offers to fleet admins*), `hq/src/fleet/__tests__/consoleMethods.test.ts` (the second test)
- **SC-1113** — The Fleet Console reads how many people asked, for the fleet admin signed in, reads the offers dismissed and dismisses one as the person signed in, refuses a name that is not an offer without asking HQ, passes on HQ's refusal, says an HQ older than the count is older, needs a session, and gives the screens Cloud's address for the links out. · fulfils AC-346a, AC-346c · tests `console/src/__tests__/cloudPlatform.test.ts`
- **SC-1114** — The menu's organisation switcher lists only this organisation; not connected it says other organisations need Agenthix Cloud and where to ask, connected but not linked it points to linking in Settings, linked it opens the person's switcher on Cloud carrying only the entry point and the page to return to; *Start your own organisation* is always there and dropped only when HQ does not know where Cloud is. · fulfils AC-345a, AC-345b · tests `hq/src/shared/components/sidebar/__tests__/OrgSwitcher.test.tsx`
- **SC-1115** — HQ's page in the Fleet Console has a Cloud section in each state: not connected says what Cloud gives, how many people asked (nothing when none did) and Connect with no command to run; connected shows the person's own Cloud account as not linked, and Disconnect; HQ on Agenthix Cloud shows the connection as Cloud's own, with no Disconnect. · fulfils AC-346a · tests `console-web/src/__tests__/cloudPlatform.test.tsx` (the describe *the Cloud section on HQ's page*)
- **SC-1116** — The Fleet Console's menu head carries the same switcher in its three states, in the Fleet Console's words. · fulfils AC-346b, AC-345a · tests `console-web/src/__tests__/cloudPlatform.test.tsx` (the describe *the organisation switcher in the menu head*)
- **SC-1117** — The Fleet Console shows a fleet admin one dismissible offer line where Cloud removes a limit, and dismissing it asks HQ to keep that for good; it shows nothing when HQ cannot say what was dismissed, and an address offer only for a bare address (not https, or a network address). · fulfils AC-346c · tests `console-web/src/__tests__/cloudPlatform.test.tsx` (the describe *the offers where Cloud removes a limit*)
- **SC-1118** — On *no site available* HQ shows a fleet admin one line offering to run a site on Cloud, with a link out for the task and nothing about the organisation or the person in it; a member sees nothing, nothing shows once it is dismissed, and nothing when HQ does not know where Cloud is. · fulfils AC-346c · tests `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/CloudOfferLine.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/NoExecutionSiteNotice.test.tsx`
- **SC-1119** — A link out to Cloud carries a path on Cloud, the entry point, the task and the page to return to, and nothing else; the return is a path on HQ — anything else is left out — and there is no link where the address of Cloud is not known. · fulfils AC-345c · tests `hq/src/shared/__tests__/cloudLinks.test.ts`, `console-web/src/__tests__/cloudPlatform.test.tsx` (the describe *links out to Cloud*)
- **SC-1120** — Organisation › Infrastructure opens its HQ group with *Where HQ runs*, a line that says self-hosted and not connected (naming the Fleet Console's HQ page), self-hosted and connected as whom, or that Cloud keeps it up to date and backed up; HQ's page says it and links to the guide's page on Agenthix Cloud. · fulfils AC-346d · tests `hq/src/org/pages/infrastructure/__tests__/whereHqRuns.test.ts`, `hq/src/org/pages/__tests__/InfrastructurePage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/org/pages/__tests__/HqPage.files.test.tsx`
- **SC-1121** — Setup › Review, under *Where agents run*, mentions Agenthix Cloud in one sentence with no link or button, not on an HQ that Cloud runs and not before that is known. · fulfils AC-344d · tests `hq/src/setup/pages/steps/__tests__/CloudSetupLine.test.tsx`

- **SC-1124** — On a machine Agenthix Cloud runs, Cloud connects HQ with no code: asked through the host service, HQ answers its connection key (made once, kept encrypted, without calling Cloud), and connects to the organisation Cloud names only once that key signs for a token, recording it as every connection is; asked again for the same organisation it answers the same connection, and another organisation, a key asked for while connected, or a connect before any key is refused. HQ refuses both where its host service has not said Cloud runs it, and asks nothing of Cloud; the host service checks every field both ways. The Fleet Console, while such an HQ is not yet connected, offers no Cloud pitch and keeps *Connect* as the way back. · fulfils AC-337d · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudStores.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/auth/machine/__tests__/cloudMachineCommands.test.ts`, `host/src/cloud/__tests__/hqOperations.test.ts`, `console-web/src/__tests__/cloudPlatform.test.tsx`

Not signed, the card takes them straight to signing on Cloud with what it asks beside it; withdrawn, the plain invitation; signed the current version, no card, dismissed or not; signed an earlier version, a card about the new one, once per new version, even after the invitation was dismissed, and dismissing it never goes back to an older version. Settings › Sign-in shows the person their own signature and version, with a link to their badge, and nothing without one. · fulfils AC-347c, AC-347d · tests `core/src/cloud/__tests__/pledge.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/pledgeStatus.test.ts`, `hq/src/home/__tests__/pledge.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/home/__tests__/PledgeCard.test.tsx`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/PledgeRow.test.tsx`

## The version baseline (added 2026-10-21)

Written from the finished code at the close of the version baseline unit. Every connection between parts installed or upgraded apart restarts at protocol 1.0 under one compatibility rule, in [`core.md`](core.md) (**SC-1127** to **SC-1136**); hq's side of each is here, the site's in [`site.md`](site.md), the host service's and the release's in [`host.md`](host.md). How a version is moved is [`docs/architecture/versioning.md`](../../architecture/versioning.md).

### The mobile app's connection

- **SC-1138** — Every route Wasp serves reads `X-Client-Protocol-Version`: a request that states none — a browser, hq's own pages, an app from before the baseline — is served, and so is an app at hq's major or one major ahead; an app two or more majors ahead, or one whose version cannot be read, is answered 426 with hq's version in the same header and the refusal (**SC-1130**) as its body, naming the side to update. The check runs after CORS, so a refusal carries CORS's headers, and a client in a browser may send the header across origins; `User-Agent` changes no answer. Sites, host services, the Fleet Console and Cloud never send the header, so their routes are untouched. · fulfils AC-348b · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocol.test.ts` (the describe *the client protocol over HTTP*), `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocolMiddleware.test.ts`
- **SC-1139** — A socket handshake states its version in `auth.clientProtocolVersion`, or in the header when its auth has none, and is decided by the same rule: one with no version, or one hq serves, connects, keeping what it stated; any other, an unreadable value included, never connects, and the client gets `connect_error` whose message is `client_protocol_refused` and whose data is the same refusal as the 426's. · fulfils AC-348b · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocol.test.ts` (the describe *the client protocol in the socket handshake*), `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocolSocket.test.ts`
- **SC-1140** — Before anyone signs in, `GET /api/org/public-identity`, which the mobile app reads at its address step, answers HQ's `release` — the version the host service gave it when it started (**SC-1255**), `dev` for a local build — and its `clientProtocolVersion` beside the organisation's public identity. · fulfils AC-348b · tests `hq/src/org/__tests__/publicIdentity.test.ts` (the describe *hq's versions before sign-in*), `hq/src/org/__tests__/publicIdentity.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1141** — hq still has everything core lists for the mobile app (**SC-1132**): each listed operation is declared in a `*.wasp.ts` as its listed kind, each listed path is served with its method, the sign-in, sign-out and session paths come from Wasp's username-and-password sign-in, and each listed event is handled or sent by hq's server code; an event hq does not have fails, so removing one is seen. · fulfils AC-349a · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/mobileAppProtocol.test.ts`
- **SC-1142** — The operations the mobile app uses answer core's declared shapes, built field by field and never a database row handed out: a task carries its declared fields and neither its application data nor anything its row gains, labels that are not a list of words read as none, and a comment's agent is a reference only; a board's columns keep their declared fields in their stored order; a task session's record reads a value the site left out as null and keeps the session key and dispatch metadata in hq; the run events hq sends a client are built from the run and their declared fields, dropping a stream delta with no text or no run and a pending approval whose tools or commands are not a list. The task operations refuse an argument of the wrong kind with 400 and pass on only the declared fields, so a member can set no other column of a task. · fulfils AC-349a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskWire.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskArgs.test.ts`, `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/taskSessionWire.test.ts`, `hq/src/sites/channel/__tests__/clientRunEvents.test.ts`

### Agenthix Cloud's connection

- **SC-1143** — hq states its Cloud protocol version and `User-Agent: hq/<release>` on every call to Cloud, whichever endpoint, the code exchange of a sign-in included (the browser's visit to Cloud's authorize page is not a call of hq's and carries neither). A Cloud two or more majors ahead — by a 426, or by any answer stating such a version — is refused: hq says HQ must upgrade and asks Cloud nothing more, and a connected hq stops checking in but keeps its connection and key; a 426 that does not show Cloud ahead stops calls until the next check-in is due (55 minutes), then hq asks again; an answer with no version is read as 1.0; a poll refused for its version ends the request as failed rather than polling on. While calls are refused, hq's state says why, and says nothing once the refusal has lapsed. The stand-in Cloud refuses a call with no version, or one two majors from its own, with 426 and its version, as Cloud's server does. · fulfils AC-348a, AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudProtocolVersion.test.ts`
- **SC-1144** — HQ refuses to ask Cloud for a connection, or to answer its connection key, while its address is not one Cloud can send a sign-in back to, and asks Cloud nothing; the address is registered with the connection once approved; at a check-in HQ tells Cloud its address when it is not the one registered, once, and again at the next check-in when Cloud was away; Cloud sends a sign-in back only to the address HQ registered, never to one a request names. A downtime notice names its kind from the closed list of scheduled changes, and nothing is sent for any other kind; it counts as taken only when Cloud answers that it was accepted. · fulfils AC-337a, AC-338b, AC-339a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudCheckIn.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/downtimeEmail.test.ts`

### A site a minor version ahead

- **SC-1145** — A `site.event` whose name hq does not know is dropped with one warning per site and name, however often it arrives, and hq stops remembering names past a cap, so a site a minor version ahead neither fills the log nor grows hq's memory. · fulfils AC-348c · tests `hq/src/sites/channel/__tests__/siteEventUnknown.test.ts`

### HQ at a network address over plain http

A browser gives some features — `crypto.randomUUID`, `crypto.subtle`, the clipboard, the microphone — only to a secure page: https, or `localhost`.

- **SC-1146** — hq's browser code makes its ids with `randomId`, a version 4 UUID made from random bytes where the page is not secure and `crypto.randomUUID` is missing, so a message is sent, and a conversation started, from HQ opened at a network address over plain http. · fulfils AC-350a · tests `hq/src/shared/__tests__/randomId.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/notSecure.spec.ts`
- **SC-1147** — A check in Checks reads every file hq's browser code reaches from its client and its pages (never its server code), the Fleet Console's screens and `@agenthix/ui`, parsing each so that comments, types and look-alikes do not count, and fails on a direct use of a feature only a secure page has, except in a helper listed with how it handles the absence; a listed helper that no longer uses it fails too. The microphone, opened through its helper, is refused on a page that is not secure in the words that voice and dictation need https or this computer at `localhost`, not by a missing object. · fulfils AC-350b · tests `scripts/__tests__/check-secure-page.test.mjs`, `hq/src/shared/__tests__/securePage.test.ts`
- **SC-1148** — hq's end-to-end tests run a `not-secure` project at a made-up network name over plain http, in the full regression before a unit merges: the page is not secure; Home's voice button is greyed with its reason; a conversation started from Home opens and a second message goes in through the thread's own box; Copy on the phone pairing page reads *Copied!* or says it could not copy; no page error is raised. · fulfils AC-350a, AC-350b · tests `hq/e2e/notSecure.spec.ts`
- **SC-1149** — The install test (`scripts/install-test/run.sh`, in each release build and the release rehearsal, and by hand on a machine with no install) installs the images the release ships — the start test's locally, the candidate's pulled by digest in CI, never built again for it (**SC-1253**) — starts a host service under a namespace of its own and installs HQ at the machine's network address over plain http, refusing, touching nothing, where an HQ already runs or a port it needs is held. In a browser the first admin is then made from the install's link with a stand-in model as the model, the organisation talks on it, a conversation opened from Home is answered by the stand-in, and the Fleet Console opens on the machine and lets the first admin in; afterwards it removes only what carries its namespace. The stand-in answers chats, streamed or whole, and its model list, and nothing else, logging each request, so no real model is called. · fulfils AC-350a, AC-351b · tests `hq/e2e/install/walkthrough.spec.ts`, `scripts/install-test/__tests__/stand-in-model.test.mjs`

### Tests that reach nothing outside

- **SC-1150** — No unit or integration test of hq, the site or the Fleet Console reaches a model or voice provider: a fetch or an https request to one throws, naming the test and the host, and fails the test even when the error is swallowed; a local stand-in, GitHub and hosts that only look like a provider's pass. · fulfils AC-352a · tests `hq/src/test/noProviderCalls.test.ts`, `site/src/test/noProviderCalls.test.ts`, `console/src/test/noProviderCalls.test.ts`

- **SC-1152** — The comparison and the pin step read a released screenshot from the GitHub Release its link names: through GitHub's API with `GH_TOKEN` (or `GITHUB_TOKEN`), each release looked up once, and from the picture's address when there is no token; a missing release or picture counts as none, and any other answer fails, naming the picture and the tag. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs` (the describe *a released screenshot, read from the release its link names*)
- **SC-1924** — The pin step (`npm run guide:pin -- --tag <tag> [--captures <folder>]` in `hq/`) compares a set of captures as `guide:compare` does and points each new or changed screenshot at the upcoming tag, and each unchanged one at the release that holds it — so run again, a screenshot pinned to the upcoming tag that is the same as its release again moves back; it refuses a tag that is not a release tag or is already tagged, links the name check refuses, and a set that lacks a linked screenshot, changing nothing. · fulfils AC-285d · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/pin-screenshots.test.mjs`, `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/screenshotLinks.test.mjs`
- **SC-1925** — The pin step rewrites every link to a moved screenshot, in the guide's pages and the root `README.md`, Markdown and HTML alike, and leaves everything else in them as it was; it never rewrites a README inside the guide, and commits nothing. · fulfils AC-285d · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/pin-screenshots.test.mjs`, `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/screenshotLinks.test.mjs`
- **SC-1926** — *Publish release* (`publish-images.yml`) takes, from the guide set of the release build run its tag promotes, exactly the screenshots whose links at the tagged commit name the tag, failing before anything is published when the set lacks one, and attaches them to the release page with the release record; a release none of whose links name its tag carries none. · fulfils AC-285c · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/release-screenshots.test.mjs` (the selection; the workflow has no automated test)

## Providers (added 2026-10-23)

The organisation reaches AI through providers: a model provider, a speech-to-text provider and a text-to-speech provider, each a vendor with at most one key for the whole organisation, kept by organisation admins on Manage organisation › Providers. Nobody else holds a key: the organisation, project and person key forms are gone, and a person's own Claude subscription is the one credential that belongs to a person (**SC-1197**).

- **SC-1170** — An organisation admin adds a provider by its vendor, from a pop-up that lists the vendors directly (**SC-1843**) — Anthropic, OpenAI, Google Gemini, OpenRouter or the organisation's own OpenAI-compatible endpoint for models; AssemblyAI for listening; Inworld for speech and for listening on the same key, and for models while that is offered (**SC-1851**) — with its key and an optional name. The key is checked with the vendor first, and a vendor that refuses it, or says the account is out of credit or cannot be reached, stores nothing and answers (422) in plain words and then the vendor's own (**SC-1848**). The organisation has at most one provider of a vendor: adding it again is refused and says to replace its key. A custom endpoint needs its address and takes a key only if it has one; every other vendor needs its key and takes no address. *(Changed 2026-11-10: the dialog no longer asks for a type first, and a short guide to the vendor's key sits above the key — **SC-1847**.)* · fulfils AC-355a · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `providers.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/providers/components/__tests__/addProviderForm.test.tsx`
- **SC-1171** — No operation, page, message or log ever carries a provider's key. It is stored encrypted under the secrets master key; what is kept in the clear is its last four characters; every answer gives at most those four; a key too short, or with spaces or control characters inside, is refused before it is checked; and a vendor's message kept on a provider has the key, and anything that looks like one, taken out and is trimmed to one line. · fulfils AC-355c · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/secrecy.test.ts`, `write.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1172** — A provider is written only by one of three actors, and anything else is refused: an organisation admin, whose role is checked again at the write; whoever can run commands on HQ's machine, through HQ's control socket (**SC-1180**); and Agenthix Cloud's controller, only on a machine Cloud runs and only for Agenthix Cloud as a provider. A member, someone who has left the organisation and someone signed out are refused every provider operation, reads included. · fulfils AC-355d · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `providers.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1173** — Replacing a key checks the new key with the vendor before the old one is dropped; a refused key changes nothing, and an accepted one makes the provider work again and changes nothing else. Agenthix Cloud has no key to replace. A provider is renamed to a name of one line and at most 80 characters, and the old and new names are recorded. · fulfils AC-355b · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1174** — Before a removal is confirmed, the page says what it touches: which defaults and which agents use the provider and what each falls to (the same model through another provider first, else the next that may run), and says so in the record. A removal that leaves the organisation with no model, no listening or no speech provider is not refused: the confirmation says that type is down until a provider is added. *(Changed 2026-10-28: it was refused (409) after setup; an organisation with no provider of a type is an ordinary state — ADR-0160.)* A revoke is the same removal at once, whatever it breaks, and is never refused: the defaults and agents that used it are left empty for the resolver to fill by its rules (**SC-1195**). An unknown provider is not found. · fulfils AC-355e · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `providers.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/providers/components/__tests__/providerWords.test.ts`
- **SC-1175** — Every write is recorded with who made it, as which of the three actors, what it was and when, shown on the provider; and every organisation admin is told of it, the admin who made it included, with a custom endpoint's address always in the words so that an endpoint pointed somewhere else is seen. · fulfils AC-355f · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `providers.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1176** — A provider is in one state: works; key refused (a refusal of the key confirmed by an immediate check); out of credit (confirmed by a check); unreachable (three failures in a row, calls or checks, at least a minute apart, failures closer than that counting as one); and, for Agenthix Cloud only, not available yet or disconnected, set from Cloud's answers and never by a check. A single server error changes nothing; a rate limit, a bad request, a cancel or a spending cap is not a state; a success makes the provider work again and clears the vendor's message. Every call to a provider reports its outcome, an authentication failure asks for a check at once, and a check that costs the vendor nothing runs hourly on a working provider and every five minutes on one that is not. · fulfils AC-355g · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/healthRules.test.ts`, `health.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/__tests__/checks.test.ts`
- **SC-1177** — A provider that is not working is one persistent notice to every organisation admin: what is down in the vendor's words and what depends on it, linking to the provider's line, not dismissible, and gone once the provider works again, or is replaced. Nobody else is told by notification; they see the provider greyed out and marked *not working*, with *an organisation admin has been told*, where they pick a model or start voice. · fulfils AC-355g · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/providers.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/models-settings/__tests__/offered.test.ts`
- **SC-1178** — Manage organisation › Providers lists each provider with its state in words, the vendor's message, what uses it, the end of its key and when it was last checked, a custom endpoint's address on its line, and a way to check it now; a provider a notification linked to is marked; with none it says so. Add provider offers only vendors not yet added; Replace key never shows the old key; Rename, Remove (with what falls to what) and Revoke (with its warning) are dialogs of their own. · fulfils AC-355h · tests `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/providers/components/__tests__/providerWords.test.ts`
- **SC-1179** — Agenthix Cloud is a provider of all three types that stores no key. An organisation admin connects it from setup or from Providers, with a short code approved at Agenthix Cloud (the panel shows the code to check and the page to approve on, says it is waiting, says why an attempt ended and offers to connect again); it is announced to every organisation admin and, while Cloud does not offer models and voice to this HQ yet, reads *not available yet* in its own words, is never chosen as a default and tells nobody. Disconnecting is only on Providers, after asking, and never on an HQ that Agenthix Cloud runs. On such a machine Cloud's controller supplies it as the provider. · fulfils AC-355i · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `machineCommands.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/components/__tests__/CloudConnectPanel.test.tsx`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1180** — Through HQ's control socket the machine admin adds a provider (with its key travelling inside the request, never as an argument, and neither the answer nor the list carrying it), lists the organisation's providers and removes one by its vendor, with the removal of the last provider of a type refused as a conflict; anything else is answered with its usage. The command line is **SC-1233**. · fulfils AC-356a, AC-356b · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/machineCommands.test.ts`
- **SC-1181** — A provider is checked with something that costs nothing at its vendor (a list of models, for a custom endpoint without a key header when it has none); the key is sent only in its header; a refusal of the key, of money, of load and of the server are told apart (an OpenRouter key whose limit is used up is out of credit); and a vendor that cannot be reached is unavailable. · fulfils AC-355g · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/checks.test.ts`

## Setup (added 2026-10-23)

- **SC-1182** — HQ's setup state is worked out from its data and never stored: *unclaimed* with no account, *setting up* with a first account and setup not finished, *set up* once setup is recorded. Setup opens at the organisation until its step is saved, then at the last look; the AI step is never where it resumes, whatever providers exist, since AI may be left for later (ADR-0160) and is reached again with Edit on the last look. *(Changed 2026-11-10: the resume step is the organisation or the last look.)* · fulfils AC-357a · tests `hq/src/setup/__tests__/setupState.test.ts`, `setup.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/SetupPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1183** — Before setup is finished HQ's address shows no organisation: with no account the sign-in page is a holding page, *This HQ isn't set up yet*, with no form, saying the installer sets it up from the link printed at the install and giving the command to get a new one on HQ's machine; with a first account it says *Almost ready* and that signing in as the first account carries on where it stopped; an expired or used link says so with the same command. Apps read the claim state from the public identity and say *not set up yet*. · fulfils AC-357b · tests `hq/src/auth/pages/__tests__/LoginPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/org/__tests__/publicIdentity.test.ts`, `hq/src/auth/__tests__/inviteService.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1184** — Setup is one flow in HQ for the first admin, in four steps, the same for everyone: You (the first account); Your organisation (name, picture, cover, time zone); AI (the organisation's providers and what agents use, **SC-1842** to **SC-1848**); and a last look. Agenthix Cloud is an entry in the AI step's Add a provider pop-up, not a path of its own. Each step is in the address, so a refresh and Back keep it, and an old step or path is ignored. Each step's buttons sit in one row under the form, pinned to the foot of the window while the form is longer than it. *(Changed 2026-11-10: four steps in place of up to six; the choice of how agents reach AI, the model provider and the voice steps are gone.)* · fulfils AC-357c · tests `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/setupSteps.test.ts`, `SetupPage.test.tsx`, `AiStep.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/setup.spec.ts`
- **SC-1185** — Finishing setup is never refused for providers: with none, with a model and no voice, or with voice and no model, it records setup done once. It writes those of the organisation's three defaults — the default model, the voice and the listening model — that the providers added can fill and that are not set, and keeps one that is; a provider added or given a new key later fills the defaults still empty, so the organisation never runs on the resolver's last-resort fallback. The last look says plainly what is not set up yet. *(Changed 2026-10-28: models and voice may be left for later, and the organisation comes before AI — ADR-0160.)* Anyone but the first admin changes nothing and is asked for nothing. · fulfils AC-357d · tests `hq/src/setup/__tests__/setupState.test.ts`, `setup.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1186** — The defaults setup proposes are the newest model of a named family on the first provider that serves it (**SC-1857**) — a Claude Sonnet on an Anthropic key, the same model by name behind OpenRouter, the newest Sonnet in Inworld's own list on an Inworld key (**SC-1856**), for any other vendor a model chosen from the catalogue by price band, none for a vendor that reaches no catalogued model — Inworld TTS 2 speaking as the default voice and the default listening model; they skip a provider that is not working, leave alone what is already set, and with Agenthix Cloud as the only provider put every default on it with the models left to Cloud's own catalogue. The AI step shows the three, each with Change (**SC-1844**), and the last look shows Kernel speaking as that voice. *(Changed 2026-11-10: the model is the newest of its family, never a fixed id; the defaults are shown on the AI step, not on the last look.)* · fulfils AC-357e · tests `hq/src/setup/__tests__/setupDefaults.test.ts`, `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/SetupPage.test.tsx`, `AiStep.test.tsx`
- **SC-1187** — The organisation step stores the organisation's time zone and makes the default billing profile, named after the organisation; saving it again makes no second default, and the profile follows a renamed organisation until setup is finished; a time zone that is not one, a member and someone signed out are refused. The last look shows the zone and where models and voice come from, with a way to change the choice. · fulfils AC-357f · tests `hq/src/setup/__tests__/organisation.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/SetupPage.test.tsx`

## Model routing (added 2026-10-23)

- **SC-1192** — One resolver decides every model, listening and speech call — a conversation's reply, its titles and summaries, handed-off work, a task's run, the voice sample phrase, listening and speech — and answers the model and its provider, the runtime, the basis its cost is written on and the billing profile, or one problem. A call takes, in order: the person's pick; the model a conversation already talks on; the agent's own default; the organisation's default. A conversation starts on the agent's default model, else the organisation's; the work it hands off takes the model the conversation talks on, falling to the next allowed when that one may not be used for it, unless the person's instruction names another; a task's or routine's run takes the model it names, else, for a task run a conversation started, the conversation's model, else the default; titles and summaries are on the conversation's own model; the voice sample phrase takes the organisation's default model, never an agent's. Memory search never asks: it uses HQ's built-in embedding model. · fulfils AC-359a, AC-359b · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `resolver.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1193** — The runtime follows the model and the provider, never a setting: a Claude model on an Anthropic key, on a person's own Claude subscription or through Agenthix Cloud runs on Claude Code; anything else on OpenCode. A task's run and a model change keep a session that is already on the runtime the run needs and replace one that is not. · fulfils AC-359c · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchTaskModel.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1194** — Which models may run is every model the organisation's providers reach, narrowed by every list that applies — the organisation's, the agent's, the project's, the person's — and no list widens another: a ban anywhere beats an allow anywhere. A list's entry is a model, a maker (`deepseek/*`) or a family (`*-pro`), matched on a model's maker and name and never its provider, ignoring case and reading a dotted and a hyphenated version as the same; characters of a regular expression mean themselves. *Only these* makes the allow entries the whole list — with none, nothing runs — and *Never* still carves exceptions out of it. · fulfils AC-359d · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/listMatch.test.ts`, `hq/src/models-settings/__tests__/coverage.test.ts`
- **SC-1195** — A default a list cuts falls to the next layer and is said once; a pick a list cuts is refused and never swapped, with the problem saying so; with every model cut, the problem says no model may run and who can widen a list. A problem always names what is missing and who adds it — an organisation admin for providers, defaults and lists; the person for their own pick — in words an organisation admin or a member can act on. · fulfils AC-359e · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`
- **SC-1196** — New work goes around a provider that is not working: a default on it resolves to the same model through another provider first, then the next layer, then any model that may run, with nothing stored, so the default comes back by itself once the provider works; voice resolves to the other provider of its type. A conversation fixed to it is not switched: it gets the problem naming the provider and one offer — the same model through another provider, else the conversation default, else the picker — and taking the offer switches the conversation, writes a notice and sends the failed message again; nothing reachable keeps the message. With no provider of a type working it says so. · fulfils AC-359f · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `resolver.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/access/__tests__/talkingModel.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1197** — A person's own Claude subscription serves work in their own place only — their space and their personal projects — on Claude Code, with its cost included in the subscription, and goes only to a run that runs as them; it is refused in a shared project and in someone else's place, with the problem saying so, and it never pays for HQ's own calls, such as a conversation's reply, which use an API provider. A person signs in with their own subscription in Settings (a warning first that it covers one person's own use, the link opened on Claude's own page, the code pasted back); it is stored encrypted as the person's own provider, never the organisation's. · fulfils AC-359g, AC-164a · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `resolver.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/model-access/__tests__/claudeLoginActions.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1198** — A model picker offers every model through a provider the call may use, each with its provider on one line (*Claude Sonnet 5 · via Anthropic*, a model two providers reach appearing twice), a model on a provider that is not working listed and marked, a model a list rules out left out even then, and a choice valued as the provider and the model together. A pick through a provider that is not working is refused. · fulfils AC-359h · tests `hq/src/models-settings/__tests__/offered.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/__tests__/modelChoice.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1199** — Whether a person can talk to an agent is asked of the resolver as a conversation in their own space would be, and nothing is kept: with no model provider working, Home says under its message box that agents cannot answer yet and links an organisation admin to Manage organisation › Providers, and tells anyone else an organisation admin can set one up. · fulfils AC-277a · tests `hq/src/model-access/__tests__/talkAccess.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/home/__tests__/HomePage.test.tsx`
