## Voice through providers (added 2026-10-23)

- **SC-1205** — Which speech services work is readable by any member, naming each by its provider as a value and nothing more. A listening token comes from the provider the resolver picks for the agent (Inworld by default; a client that does not say it can stream to Inworld, such as the mobile app, is given AssemblyAI's — **SC-1835**), costed to the conversation's place, the person speaking and the conversation (the organisation, for the person, without one): hq seals a ticket that comes back as the session's ledger row, refuses a conversation the person may not speak in, makes no token for it, and refuses a ticket opened by someone else. With no working listening provider it answers 503, saying voice is down and who has been told. · fulfils AC-361a · tests `hq/src/voice/__tests__/speechToken.integration.test.ts`, `speechTokenTicket.test.ts`, `speechAccess.test.ts`, `speechScope.test.ts`, `voiceOperations.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1206** — A voice sample and a voices list are asked of a working speech provider: with none, listing and preview answer 503 with the outage message; a provider that is not a speech provider is refused; the sample is a short phrase written by the organisation's default model, or a fixed phrase in the voice's language when no model can write one. · fulfils AC-361b · tests `hq/src/voice/__tests__/voiceOperations.integration.test.ts`, `phraseModel.test.ts`

## Personal projects (added 2026-10-23)

A person's own place is their space and every personal project they make. A personal project can never have members, be shared, be filed under a product or programme or be an organisation project; it has its own storage.

- **SC-1207** — Anyone in the organisation, an organisation admin or not, makes as many personal projects as they like with *New project*, choosing *Just you*, asking only a name, an optional description (at most 120 characters, a name required) and, if they type one, a key: each is private, owned by its person as its one admin, has a Project Tasks board and no slug, is not the space, and is put on the site that holds the person's own storage with its own files there; it says nothing in the organisation's activity. Someone signed out, or who has left, is refused; a project whose files cannot be provisioned is not left behind. · fulfils AC-362a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProjects.integration.test.ts`, `personalProjectKey.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/screen/__tests__/NewProjectDialog.test.tsx`
- **SC-1208** — A personal project is listed to its owner only, under *Your personal projects* and marked *Only you*; anyone else, an organisation admin included, is answered as if it did not exist, and sees neither its contents nor its name. · fulfils AC-362b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProjects.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1209** — Its owner deletes a personal project, typing its name, with its tasks and files, and the site that held it is told; the space is never deleted, not even by its owner who is an organisation admin; another person's personal project is deleted by nobody else; an organisation project is deleted by an organisation admin only. · fulfils AC-362c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProjects.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1210** — The space is the person's one default personal project, found by its mark whatever other personal projects they have, made with the account (concurrently too, once) and for every account that has none; an act on a personal project that only a shared project can have — members, leaving, access — is refused with 400 naming the act; a task cannot be moved onto another project's board or the other way round. The person's storage is their own for the space and the project's own for every other project, a personal one included. A task in the space runs as its person in their own storage, one in another personal project as them in the project's own, any other as its project, and a task that is gone as none; the cost of a personal project goes to the default billing profile and the person's list applies. · fulfils AC-362d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProject.integration.test.ts`, `personalProjectRules.test.ts`

## Billing and costing (added 2026-10-23)

Every model, listening and speech call is costed to a billing profile and written once to an append-only ledger.

- **SC-1211** — The ledger holds one row per call, written once however often it is reported, with its provider as it was named then, model, usage, price, basis and billing profile; a call with no profile yet is costed to the default one; money is whole millionths of a currency unit with its currency, a currency that is not a code is refused, and an amount that cannot be carried exactly is refused rather than rounded. Nothing changes or removes a row: Agenthix Cloud's actual figure is appended as a new row pointing at its call, never twice and never for a call hq did not write, and a correction is a new row, negative amounts allowed; a call's cost is its latest row with an amount. · fulfils AC-363a · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/writer.test.ts`, `ledger.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/__tests__/report.test.ts`
- **SC-1212** — A call is priced on a basis: *estimated* from HQ's price list for the organisation's own keys (a model priced by the vendor that serves it, converted once to whole millionths), *included* on a person's own Claude subscription, *unpriced* for a model with no known price or an answer with no usage, never priced by hand, and, through Agenthix Cloud, *awaiting actual* with its catalogue price as a guide while usage billing is on, and *estimated* from that catalogue while it is off. Listening is priced per hour of audio and speech per million characters from a hand-kept list of the vendors' public prices, which the usage report says is an estimate. Each row names its price: the price list's version or the provider's figure. · fulfils AC-363b · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/pricing.test.ts`
- **SC-1213** — HQ's own calls are written as they happen: each streamed step once, with its usage, a completed call once, each with a call id of its own, its place and its provider, a title costed as a title and to no turn, and an access that is not costed leaving no row. · fulfils AC-363c · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/hqCalls.test.ts`
- **SC-1214** — An agent's calls reach HQ as the site's usage reports (`POST /api/v1/usage/report`, with the site's machine credential and refused without it): HQ keeps each call once, answers every call id it holds, those it already had included, keeps a call whose answer carried no usage so the ledger writes it unpriced, leaves out a report it cannot read, refuses a batch over the limit, and feeds each new call's outcome to the provider's health once. Each is written to its run's place and billing profile with the relay's call id; a report for a run HQ has not kept yet waits a little and then goes to the default profile; a run on a person's own subscription is written once when it ends, included, with the tokens it added. · fulfils AC-363d · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/usageReports.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/__tests__/sources.test.ts`, `ledger.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1215** — A listening session is written when it ends, its duration capped at the time since its token was issued, from a ticket HQ sealed (a ticket someone else opened, one HQ did not seal and an old one are refused); the characters a speech call sent are counted. · fulfils AC-363e · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/sources.test.ts`, `hq/src/voice/__tests__/speechTokenTicket.test.ts`
- **SC-1216** — Billing profiles are the organisation admins': made (with the projects picked for it), renamed, made the default, and archived, never deleted and their ids never reused. There is always exactly one default, made in setup; the default and a profile projects still use cannot be archived until they move, and the page asks where they go; each move is recorded. The page lists the profiles, marks the default and gives this month per basis, with archived profiles apart and without actions; anyone else is refused. · fulfils AC-363f · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/ledger.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/pages/__tests__/BillingProfilesPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1217** — Every project has a billing profile: a new project gets the default and works at once, its creator, when an organisation admin, choosing another in the new-project form only when there are two or more (no field and none sent otherwise; anyone else's project bills to the default); later changes are the organisation admins' alone, from the next call and recorded, shown to anyone else as text, and shown to nobody when there is only one profile. · fulfils AC-363g · tests `hq/src/billing/components/__tests__/ProjectBillingSection.test.tsx`, `hq/src/projects/screen/__tests__/NewProjectDialog.test.tsx`
- **SC-1218** — Manage organisation › Usage is the usage report for organisation admins: it opens on the current month and cannot go past it, groups cost by billing profile, project, agent or person, keeps each currency and each basis (actual, estimated, included, unpriced) a separate total, explains each basis in words, names an erased person *Former member* and calls with none apart, shows the time zone the months are cut in and changes it, says when nothing was used, and exports the month as a CSV of one call cost per line for the chosen profile, writing millionths as an exact decimal, quoting what needs quoting and keeping a name from being read as a formula. · fulfils AC-363h · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/report.test.ts`, `months.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/pages/__tests__/UsagePage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/billing/components/__tests__/costWords.test.ts`
- **SC-1219** — A month is cut in the organisation's time zone, starting at local midnight on the first across a change of daylight saving, so a call belongs to the month of the organisation and not of UTC; only a real month is read. Deleting a person or a project never deletes cost: an erased person's rows stay, named *Former member*. · fulfils AC-363i · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/months.test.ts`, `ledger.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1220** — A task's run on an organisation's own provider is followed end to end with no outside call: an organisation admin adds the provider (HQ checks the key with the vendor, keeps it and answers no key), the run is dispatched to the site with that provider and costed to its project and the default billing profile, the call goes through the site's relay to the vendor with the provider's key and never the relay token and the stream comes back whole, and the site's report makes HQ write one call row with the vendor's counts, the run, its project and the profile. · fulfils AC-363d · tests `tests/cross-app/06-model-usage.test.ts`
- **SC-1221** — A site asks HQ for Agenthix Cloud's token over its channel (`POST /api/v1/cloud/ai-token`): it is refused as not connected while HQ has no Cloud connection and as not available while HQ's calls to Cloud's AI do not exist yet. · fulfils AC-355i · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/cloudAiTokenApi.test.ts`

## Workflows (added 2026-10-25)

A place — a project, or a person's own storage — keeps a library of workflows: a definition (`workflow.yaml`) and the files its steps run, frozen as versions identified by a SHA-256 hash. Its secrets are used only by an approved version, run by the site (`site.md`, *Workflow runs*). The definition's own rules are in `core.md`, *Workflows*.

- **SC-1276** — In a place, a project's admins administer its workflows, its members work with them (run, propose), its viewers read them and anyone else gets nothing; being an organisation admin gives nothing in a project by itself. A person administers their own storage's workflows and nobody else does. · fulfils AC-375a · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/library.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1277** — Proposing a version reads the definition from the workspace named, one of the place's own, and the files it names relative to its folder, and hashes them with the site's executor image digest (another digest, another hash); it refuses a link among the files without following it, a definition path that climbs out of the workspace, a definition core's parser refuses (saying where), YAML with aliases or duplicate keys, and a secret or variable the place doesn't have, naming them, while `GITHUB_TOKEN` is never looked for among the place's secrets. The next version is stored with its proposer, and the same content proposed again answers the same waiting version. · fulfils AC-375a · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/proposalContent.test.ts`, `library.integration.test.ts`, `agentToolsPropose.test.ts`
- **SC-1278** — An approval is recorded as (workflow, place, hash, who, when) and the first one turns the workflow on; only the place's administrators approve. A person approves the waiting version named by its own hash, and any other hash is refused, as are a version not waiting, one older than the approved one and one whose plain checks have not run. The same content in another project waits for that project's approval. A newer approval supersedes the approved version and older waiting ones, leaving newer ones waiting; a waiting version never stops the approved one running. Declining is by hash, of a waiting version only, and a declined version can't then be approved. · fulfils AC-375b · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/rules.test.ts`, `library.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1279** — In a person's own storage, the person approves a version whose GitHub permissions are contents and pull requests only; wider ones also need an organisation admin, who may approve them in their own storage; the refusal names what goes beyond the limit, and the limit applies to approving, never to declining. · fulfils AC-375d · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/rules.test.ts`, `library.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1280** — An agent is refused approving, declining, switching a workflow and administering a place; the agent's workflow tools reach neither approving nor switching; an approval of a workflow card passed on by an agent is refused before anything is read. · fulfils AC-375a · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/agentRefused.test.ts`, `agentToolsNoApprove.test.ts`, `hq/src/workflows/review/__tests__/card.test.ts`
- **SC-1281** — The plain checks, with no model, flag in a step that holds a secret: the secret next to `echo`, `base64`, `curl` or `cat` to output, in the step or in the script it runs, with its line (not a key used as a file, a `cat` piped on, or `echo` on another line) — rule 1; a secret the step never reads (`push-bundle` counts as reading `GITHUB_TOKEN`), and a step that both installs and holds a secret — rule 5; running workspace code (`npm run`, `node_modules/.bin`, `dist/`, `npx`, a relative path from the workspace), high — rule 9; running `git` in the working folder, but not in a new repository under `/tmp`, high — rule 10; and handing files on through `/run/out`, high when a later step reads them and the workflow has hosts — rule 12. The same in a step with no secret raises none of these. · fulfils AC-375c · tests `hq/src/workflows/checks/__tests__/secretRules.test.ts`
- **SC-1282** — The plain checks also flag: a host the code uses that is not in `hosts`, once each (not a listed host, one held in a variable, loopback or a comment) — rule 2; a text parameter pasted into a command, read into a variable or run, while a file argument, a redirect and `gh pr create --title "$(cat /run/params/title)"` pass — rule 3; an unpinned install (`npm ci` without its lock file among the version's files, a named package, `pip` without hashes, `curl … | sh`) — rule 4; `rm -rf`, `DROP`, `--force` and `git push -f`, not `rm -r`, `rm -f` or "drop" in words — rule 7; each secret, variable or host new since the approved version, nothing for a first version — rule 8; and a parameter whose pattern or values can start with `-` — rule 11. · fulfils AC-375c · tests `hq/src/workflows/checks/__tests__/otherRules.test.ts`
- **SC-1283** — Rule 6 flags GitHub permissions beyond what the steps appear to need (none when the need can't be told; `contents: read` counted as needed by `gh pr create` and no other `gh pr` command; an environment's secret counted as `environments`, a repository's as `secrets`), and any permission new since the approved version. The ceiling is the App's permissions on GitHub and what the place's accounts have all accepted (the project's default account when it has one), unknown while GitHub hasn't been read, when nothing is flagged for it; beyond it, the flag names the permission the App lacks and how to add it, or the account that hasn't accepted it. · fulfils AC-375c · tests `hq/src/workflows/checks/__tests__/otherRules.test.ts`, `ceiling.test.ts`
- **SC-1284** — The card may offer Approve only when no step newly gets a secret and no secret is newly given to a step, what no secret step runs changed (its script included), the hosts, GitHub permissions and parameter patterns are unchanged, and no flag was raised — never asking the reviewer; so a first version that gives a step a secret needs the full review though nothing is flagged, and a version whose secret step changes is never card-approvable however low the reviewer rates it. The card's one-line summary is written from the diff, naming steps by number and never by the name the agent gave them, and says when only the executor image changed. · fulfils AC-375c · tests `hq/src/workflows/checks/__tests__/card.test.ts`, `hq/src/workflows/review/__tests__/secretStepCard.test.ts`
- **SC-1285** — The reviewer is given the version's files, settings and flags as data, names only and no values, with a warning that the data may address it; its answer is read strictly as a level (*low*, *look closely*, *high*) and points naming a file and line, and anything else is stored as *look closely*. A failed call is recorded and tried once more; after a second failure the version has no level and is never held back. · fulfils AC-375c · tests `hq/src/workflows/review/__tests__/reviewer.test.ts`
- **SC-1286** — At proposal the plain checks' flags, summary and card rule are stored and the flags answered; the reviewer's level is stored and the card written in the proposing run's conversation, carrying the summary, flags, level and the hash it shows, with no card for a proposal no conversation's run made. Only an administrator of the place sees its buttons and approves from it, and only the version it showed; Approve is refused (409) when the stored rule asks for the full review, which the card then offers alone with the reason, and declining on the card withdraws the card and leaves the version waiting. · fulfils AC-375c · tests `hq/src/workflows/review/__tests__/card.integration.test.ts`, `card.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/components/__tests__/WorkflowRequestRow.test.tsx`
- **SC-1287** — Turning a workflow off asks the site to stop each run in progress and nobody can run it; turning it on needs an approved version and no new approval; a non-admin is refused either; every switch is kept with who and why. · fulfils AC-375e · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/library.integration.test.ts`, `rules.test.ts`, `runs.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1288** — Each site reports its executor image's digest, release and tools key, and a change of digest is a change of image. When the image changes, an approved version keeps its approval only in the same release line with an unchanged tools stage; otherwise its new version waits for approval while the old one stays approved, and so it does when either image doesn't say what it is. · fulfils AC-375f · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/imageUpgrade.test.ts`, `executorImage.test.ts`, `library.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1289** — A version is compared with the approved one: lines diffed with unchanged lines far from a change folded, files added, removed, changed or kept and a change of mode, a binary file as changed without lines, and a step newly given a secret. The full review shows the reviewer's points first, then the checks and the changes; it approves and declines exactly the version shown, by its hash, and offers no decision to someone who may not make it or on a version already decided. · fulfils AC-375b, AC-375c · tests `hq/src/workflows/__tests__/versionDiff.test.ts`, `hq/src/workflows/pages/__tests__/compareVersions.test.ts`, `VersionReviewView.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/workflows.spec.ts`
- **SC-1290** — The Workflows section is in a project's panel after Automation, and in a person's space: each workflow with its steps, the secrets it uses, its last run (from the list's own answer) and its switch; what waits for an administrator comes first, with Review, for the place's administrators, while others are told a version waits for an administrator and get no switch; Run is hidden from someone who may not start runs. A workflow opens with Steps, Versions and Runs, and Run asks for each parameter with its rule in words, refusing a value that doesn't match before anything is sent. ## GitHub through workflows (added 2026-10-25)

- **SC-1305** — The mint gate refuses: a workspace whose row the site answers belongs to another place, another workspace than the one asked for, a location with no connection, a connection without GitHub's installation, repository or account id, an installation that wants to connect, is ignored or is no longer known, a place the connect rule no longer includes (checked at this mint), a person's own storage under *every project*, an account matched by login but not by id, and a person who can't work in the place, whoever's run it is; GitHub's answer for another repository ends the token. · fulfils AC-382a · tests `hq/src/github/__tests__/mintGate.test.ts`
- **SC-1306** — The token the gate mints is for the workspace's repository by its id, with exactly the permissions asked for and always naming them, minted fresh each time and never cached; one module asks GitHub for installation tokens, reached only from the gate, and revoking reads 204 or 401 as ended. · fulfils AC-382a · tests `hq/src/github/__tests__/mintGate.test.ts`, `installationTokens.test.ts`
- **SC-1307** — Registering the App is for an organisation admin (anyone else 403, someone signed out refused): it answers GitHub's manifest page address with a one-time state, for a GitHub organisation that GitHub knows as one (trimmed; a blank one is never taken for a personal account; an unknown one, a person or a name that is not a GitHub login refused with 400 before leaving HQ; GitHub not answering leaves the check to its own page), refuses while an App is connected (409) and says so readably when HQ can't store secrets. ## Secrets and variables (added 2026-10-25)

- **SC-1326** — A secret's value is encrypted with AES-256-GCM, bound to its own id and place as associated data, so a ciphertext copied into another secret's row, or a row moved to another place, is refused; what is stored holds no plain bytes, a changed byte or an unknown format is refused, no read but the run's returns a value, a refusal names no value, and only the store and the run read values. · fulfils AC-376a · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/store.test.ts`, `secrets.integration.test.ts`, `onlyReader.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/crypto/__tests__/secretCryptoBound.test.ts`
- **SC-1327** — A typed value is kept exactly, newlines and spaces included, and a file round-trips byte for byte up to 48 KB; a file over 48 KB, an empty value and a broken upload are refused; every secret is asked to say what it is; a name is capitals, digits and `_`, not starting with a digit, and `GITHUB_TOKEN` can't be stored. · fulfils AC-376a, AC-376b · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/store.test.ts`, `hq/src/secrets/pages/__tests__/secretsWords.test.ts`
- **SC-1328** — Only the place's administrators set, replace and delete secrets, changing nothing for anyone else; the place's members are shown everything about a secret but its value, and someone outside the place is refused a read, an organisation admin or not. · fulfils AC-376f · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/store.test.ts`, `secrets.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1329** — Replacing a secret keeps its name, description and record, and the next run gets the new value. Deleting one is refused while a switched-on workflow's approved version names it — `set-github-secrets`' approved names included — naming the workflow, is not stopped by another place's workflow, and once that workflow is off goes, keeping its record without the row; deleting a project deletes its secrets, variables and their records. · fulfils AC-376d · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/store.test.ts`, `secrets.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1330** — Each run that used a secret is recorded, never its value; the values for a run skip an integration's name and refuse a name the place lacks. · fulfils AC-376e · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/store.test.ts`
- **SC-1331** — A secret with a rotate-by date is due a week before the day and overdue from its start: the place's administrators are told a week before, naming the secret and when and nothing of its value, the notice is cleared once it is replaced, and its address opens the section. · fulfils AC-376b · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/usedByAndRotation.test.ts`, `secrets.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/secrets/pages/__tests__/secretsWords.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/secrets.spec.ts`
- **SC-1332** — A variable keeps every change old → new with who; a name that changes how programs run (`PATH`, `HOME`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `GIT_DIR`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `NPM_CONFIG_REGISTRY` and the like) or is not capitals is refused, as are a second variable of the same name and anyone but an administrator; a value that looks like a secret is held back, saying why without repeating it, until the person says it is not one; deleting one a switched-on workflow uses is refused; a run is given the values it names, and one the place lacks is refused. · fulfils AC-376c · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/variables.test.ts`, `looksLikeSecret.test.ts`, `secrets.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1333** — *Used by* lists each workflow once per secret, by name, reading variables from the approved definition only and nothing from a shape it doesn't know. · fulfils AC-376d · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/usedByAndRotation.test.ts`, `hq/src/secrets/pages/__tests__/secretsWords.test.ts`
- **SC-1334** — *Turn off what uses it*, for the place's administrators only, switches off every switched-on workflow that names the secret or variable, stopping their runs in progress, and records it, doing nothing when nothing on uses it; the screen lists the workflows before turning them off, then says what to do next, and offers it when a delete is refused for use. · fulfils AC-382e · tests `hq/src/secrets/__tests__/turnOff.test.ts`, `hq/src/secrets/pages/__tests__/SecretsTool.test.tsx`
- **SC-1335** — *Secrets and variables* is in a place's panel and opens the section: the Secrets tab shows each value's kind (text, file, *From GitHub*), never a value, with rotation and *Used by*, Replace and the menu for administrators and no Replace for the GitHub token, and only reading controls for others; *Add a secret* asks what it is, where to get a new one and the value, refuses an empty form, says what is wrong with a name before sending it, and shows the GitHub box where there is a repository; the Variables tab, in the address with its own add button, shows each value in plain sight with Edit for administrators and asks when a value looks like a secret. ## Knowledge for agents (added 2026-10-26)

Written from the finished code at the close of the agent knowledge unit. Every agent — the lead in a conversation, or a worker in one place on a site — reaches every level of knowledge the same way: `search_knowledge` and `read_knowledge`, which say where each answer came from, and `write_knowledge`, `edit_knowledge` and `delete_knowledge`, which change a page only through the site's gate (`site.md`, *Knowledge on the site*). The levels are the platform guide (`platform:`), the organisation (`org:`), a project (by its name) and Your space (`personal:`). Nothing of knowledge is in the prompt beyond a few lines saying it exists.

- **SC-1397** — `search_knowledge` is one search over every level its caller holds: its places — a run's one place, or a conversation's own place and then those its person added — then the organisation, then the platform guide. Each hit is marked with its page and level: `level:page:line: text` (a context line `level:page-line- text`), `level:page` for pages and `level:page:N` for counts with a total; case is ignored and lines are numbered unless told otherwise; `scope`, or a `path` that carries its level (`website-rebuild:processes`), narrows the search to one level and asks no other; `head_limit` and `offset` count across the levels in that order; the answer always ends naming the levels searched in words (Your space, the project's name, the organisation, the platform guide); a place whose name would read as `org` or `platform`, or repeats an earlier one, is left out. · fulfils AC-324a · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgeSearch.test.ts`
- **SC-1398** — A search answer is held to 8,000 characters and says where to continue, and continuing gives the next hits; more hits than `head_limit` say the offset to continue from; an offset past the end says there is nothing there; nothing found says so, naming the levels searched; a pattern over 500 characters, a parameter of the wrong kind and a caller's 31st search in a minute are refused with the file tools' fixed refusals (**SC-1375**), and a pattern refused as slow or not valid is the answer when no level found anything. · fulfils AC-324a, AC-324b · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgeSearch.test.ts`
- **SC-1399** — An empty pattern answers each level's outline: its page count, its sections with their page counts and its tags with their counts, most used first (60 shown, then how many more); for the guide its page count and sections, no tags, and that `platform:tools.md` and `platform:glossary.md` also exist; the outline is held to the same 8,000 characters. · fulfils AC-324a · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgeSearch.test.ts`
- **SC-1400** — A level that cannot answer is said in the result while the others still answer: a site that cannot be reached, one that needs updating, one that does not answer within 5 seconds, a site or guide that fails and a guide missing from the installation each give one line saying that level was not searched and that the others answered — never a site's address, a path or an error's text. · fulfils AC-324a · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgeSearch.test.ts`, `guideSource.test.ts`
- **SC-1401** — A level its caller does not hold is refused with a fixed message listing only the levels it holds, and no site is asked; in a conversation the message says the place is not among the conversation's places, so it was not searched or read — out of reach, not empty — and that `suggest_place` offers it to the person, in the same words for a real place and a made-up one; a search that found nothing in a conversation adds that a place not in it was not searched, which is not the same as empty. · fulfils AC-324a, AC-388d · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgeSearch.test.ts`, `knowledgePages.test.ts`
- **SC-1402** — `read_knowledge` gives a page of a level its caller holds with numbered lines, up to 2,000 a call (a line over 2,000 characters cut, saying so), its front matter and its version, saying which lines it showed and the offset to read on from; it reads the organisation's and the platform guide's pages as it does a place's; a missing page, a site that cannot be reached, a name with no level, a name leaving its level (`..`, a leading `/`, a backslash) and a page that is not text each give a fixed message ending "Nothing was changed." · fulfils AC-324a · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgePages.test.ts`, `platformGuide.test.ts`
- **SC-1403** — `write_knowledge` makes a new page with no read first, but replaces a page only after its caller read it — otherwise it is refused, saying to read it first and giving none of its text; a write passes the version read as the version it started from, and if the page changed since, the gate's conflict comes back with the current text, from which the next write starts; what one run or conversation read is not known to another; a page over 64 KB is refused before it is sent, and the gate's problems are passed on. A version read is remembered for 24 hours, for at most 20,000 pages, oldest forgotten first. · fulfils AC-324c, AC-386c · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgePages.test.ts`, `readRecord.test.ts`
- **SC-1404** — `edit_knowledge` replaces one passage with no read first and passes on the gate's count when the passage is found other than once; `delete_knowledge` deletes a page and says it is kept among Recently deleted for 30 days and how it is restored; a change that lands answers `Done:` with the page, what was done, the first 12 characters of its version and whether the previous one is in History. · fulfils AC-324c · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgePages.test.ts`
- **SC-1405** — A change to the platform guide is refused (its pages are changed by the maintainers); a change to the organisation's knowledge is made only for an organisation admin, or an agent acting for one; a caller that may change nothing — a turn that answers no one, a report turn, or a run without a write tool — is refused before any site is asked and told to tell the person what should change. · fulfils AC-324c, AC-387a · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgePages.test.ts`
- **SC-1406** — Every change the gate accepts, an agent's or a person's, is recorded in hq with its place, page (and the page it came from, for a move), kind — created, edited, moved, deleted or restored — the version after it and who made it: the person it was for and, for an agent, the agent and its conversation or run; a person's own change names no agent; a delete records the version the page had; a refusal records nothing; the ids of an agent, conversation and run are kept plain so the record outlives them, and it goes with its project or person; if the record cannot be written the change stands, is said to be done, and the log says the record is missing. · fulfils AC-384c · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgePages.test.ts`, `changeRecord.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1407** — No knowledge tool throws or gives a detail away: a bad input gets a fixed message, a part that fails gets one saying the tool could not finish and nothing was changed, and hq's log never holds a search pattern, a page's name or a page's text, however a search, read, change or record fails. · fulfils AC-324b · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgePages.test.ts`, `knowledgeLog.test.ts`
- **SC-1408** — A worker reaches the five tools through hq's data endpoint under its run's capability alone: its reach is its one place (its project, or Your space for a chat or run in a person's own storage), the organisation and the platform guide, for the person the run is for, its agent and its run; a place or scope named in the call adds nothing, a project that is gone or a token naming no place reaches no place; a run may change knowledge only with a write tool (one that is never allowed stays off even when approval is bypassed); a call from a conversation's turn or a person's connection, one the site did not name its run for, a tool the agent may never use and a run for no one are refused before anything is asked. · fulfils AC-324d · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/knowledgeOps.test.ts`
- **SC-1409** — The five tools are described as Grep, Read, Write and Edit are, under Claude Code's own parameter names (`pattern`, `output_mode`, `-i`, `-n`, `-A`, `-B`, `-C`, `head_limit`, `offset`, with `scope`, and `page` for the file), name the product from its one constant, and state the front matter's rules and limits and that knowledge is changed only when a person asks; they are given to every run — write, edit and delete only to a run that may change files — with the run's id filled in by the site, and none of them is in a connection group; `suggest_place` is the lead's alone, offered in no tier, list or connection group. · fulfils AC-324d · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/knowledgeToolSchemas.test.ts`
- **SC-1410** — The lead has the same five tools as the conversation's own: a turn that answers a person is offered all five, a turn that answers no one only search and read, and a report turn or an expired one none; a tool the agent's place never allows is left out; a change asked by a turn answering no one is refused before anything is asked; a refusal of the page rules is said in its own words and any other failure plainly, without its detail; the lead reaches the conversation's own place, then the places its person added, oldest first, and a task's thread with no project reaches none. `recall` returns memory only, never knowledge, and `remember` is told not to keep what a knowledge page says. · fulfils AC-324a, AC-324c · tests `hq/src/conversations/memory/__tests__/leadKnowledgeTools.test.ts`, `leadMemoryTools.test.ts`

## The platform guide for agents (added 2026-10-26)

The platform guide is the user guide (`docs/guide/`) with its glossary and a reference of the agents' tools, built into hq's image and read through the knowledge tools as the `platform:` level.

- **SC-1411** — The platform guide is built from the user guide's pages, its glossary and the tools reference into one folder: every page but the README and the glossary is written with its images replaced by their alt text, beside an index of each page's name, title and description and the glossary's terms with what else people call them. The build fails naming a page without a description, a glossary that does not parse (no entries, an entry with no text, a term twice, an entry with no term), front matter that does not parse or is not keys and values, and anything in the folder that is not part of the guide; a build replaces what an earlier one left, takes only the guide's Markdown, and passes without a glossary. hq's image builds the guide in a stage of its own and carries it into the server. · fulfils AC-383b · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/guideBuild.test.ts` (the image stage has no test of its own)
- **SC-1412** — The tools reference is written from the tool registry and equals it: every tool of every access tier once and no other (the lead's `suggest_place` left out), search and read given to every run and write, edit and delete only to runs that may change files, a line saying which runs get each group, and something to read for every tool; it opens with the front matter the guide's build accepts. · fulfils AC-383b · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/toolsReference.test.ts`
- **SC-1413** — Every answer from the guide starts with what the guide is about and that what the agent may do here is what its tools allow, naming the product from its constant; a search of the guide leaves out its index; a guide page is read by its own name (`platform:your-tools/providers.md`, `platform:tools.md`, `platform:glossary.md`, with or without the level) with offset and limit, a name that leaves the guide is refused with a fixed message holding no path, and a guide that was not built answers a fixed message to a search and a read. · fulfils AC-383a, AC-383c · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/platformGuide.test.ts`, `guideSource.test.ts`

## Knowledge in the conversation (added 2026-10-26)

- **SC-1414** — A knowledge call is told to the conversation as a step of its own: a search names the places it covered (Your space, the project's name, Organisation, Platform guide, a level it cannot name kept as it is; a search refused before reaching any level covered nothing); a read of a guide page names the page and its title, and a read of a place's page tells nothing more; a change tells the page, its title (read back after an edit), the version after it, the version Undo restores and one line of what changed — *New page*, *The whole page rewritten*, *Now says "…"*, *Removed "…"* or that it was deleted and can be restored from Recently deleted for 30 days; a change the gate refused tells nothing, and nor does a worker's reach. · fulfils AC-384a, AC-384b · tests `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgeSteps.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/memory/__tests__/leadKnowledgeTools.test.ts`
- **SC-1415** — Among a reply's thinking steps each knowledge call is a line of its own, in a person's words and never a tool's name — *Searched Knowledge · Website rebuild and Organisation*, *Read page · …*, *Looked up the guide · …*, *Wrote page · …*, *Updated page · …*, *Deleted page · …*; a call not yet done says what it is doing (*Searching Knowledge*, *Updating a page · …*), one the gate refused says it couldn't (*Couldn't update a page · …*), and what the call did is kept on the step; the reply's other calls in one step share a line where the first of them was asked, so no call hides another. · fulfils AC-384a · tests `hq/src/conversations/coordinator/__tests__/replySteps.test.ts`
- **SC-1416** — A change's step shows *Wrote*, *Updated*, *Moved*, *Deleted* or *Restored page · title* with its one line, *See change* — the page's History at that version, in the organisation's Knowledge for an `org:` page and in the conversation's place otherwise, and none for a deleted page — and *Undo*, which restores the version before it in the place the step names, once, then reads *Undone* (a refusal is shown and Undo kept; none for a page just created); a search step names its places and a guide step reads *Looked up* the product *· title*; a step that carries no knowledge is drawn as its label. · fulfils AC-384b · tests `hq/src/conversations/components/knowledge/__tests__/knowledgeSteps.test.tsx`

## Knowledge for people (added 2026-10-26)

A person reads and changes a place's knowledge in its Knowledge tool — in a project, in Your space, and in the organisation's own area — through the same gate the agents use.

- **SC-1417** — A project's knowledge is read by whoever may read the project — checked before any site is asked, so a non-member is refused 403 and the site hears nothing — and changed by its Members and Admins; a Viewer reads, and every change is refused 403 saying it needs the Member or Admin role and to ask one of its admins. Your space's knowledge is its person's alone (anyone else 403) and that person changes it; the organisation's is read by every member and changed only by an organisation admin (anyone else 403). Each answer says whether the reader may change; a caller not signed in is refused 401, and an argument no screen sends (another kind of owner, a page name over 600 characters, a version id over 100) 400. · fulfils AC-385d, AC-386a, AC-387a · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleOperations.integration.test.ts`, `peopleReads.test.ts`, `peopleChanges.test.ts`
- **SC-1418** — The listing gives a place's sections and pages as the site lists them (**SC-1391**), each page with who changed it last from hq's record, cut short when the site cut it or 2,000 pages were reached, and waits up to 30 seconds. A page is read with its text, version, who changed it last, whether the reader may change it and *Linked from* — the place's pages whose links lead to it, found by searching the site for its file name as fixed text and reading at most 20 candidates, resolved by the itemiser's rule; any failure, or 5 seconds without an answer, is an empty list, never a failure. One kept version is read by its id (anything else 400) with no *Linked from*; a name that leaves the folder is refused 400 before the site is asked, a missing page 404, and a file holding a NUL byte 415, saying to download it from Files instead; the site's silence is put in words, never its error. · fulfils AC-385a, AC-385b · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleReads.test.ts`, `peopleOperations.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1419** — A page's last change, each kept version and each deletion name who made them from hq's record: a person by name (*Former member* once erased or gone), an agent as *agent, asked by person* linking to its conversation or run (*A removed agent* once it no longer exists); a version takes the newest change recorded at or before it was saved, falling back to the earliest that made its text when the site's clock is behind. · fulfils AC-384c, AC-386f · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleOperations.integration.test.ts`, `peopleReads.test.ts`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/history/__tests__/lineDiff.test.ts`
- **SC-1420** — Saving a page goes through the site's gate from the version the person opened (none for a new page) and is recorded with the person and no agent; a page over 64 KB is refused with the problem on its text before the site is asked; the gate's problems come back for the editor to show on their fields and nothing is recorded; a site that cannot be reached is said in words; and a change the site made stands when hq could not record it, the log saying so without the error's detail. · fulfils AC-386a, AC-386b · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleChanges.test.ts`, `peopleOperations.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1421** — A save that meets a change made since the page was opened, or a new page whose name is taken, keeps nothing and answers with the page's current text, version and who made it; the editor keeps the person's text, shows the other change on request (*See their change*) and *Save mine* saves again from the new version. · fulfils AC-386c · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleChanges.test.ts`, `peopleOperations.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/__tests__/editor.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1422** — Moving a page writes it under its new name, as a move from the old, then deletes the old one, each through the gate, and records two moves, one under each name; the page must still be the version the person opened (else a conflict with its current text), a name already taken leaves both pages as they were, a move to the name it has is refused 400, and when the old page cannot be deleted the new one is removed again, so a page is never under both names. On the page, *Move* offers a section — suggested from the place's sections and checked as a name — keeps the file's name, and stays disabled while the section is unchanged. · fulfils AC-386d · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleChanges.test.ts`, `peopleOperations.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/__tests__/page.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1423** — Deleting a page keeps it among Recently deleted, recorded with the version it had; after the confirming step the person is taken to the page's section and told the page is kept for 30 days in Recently deleted, with a link there, while a refused delete is shown in the gate's words and the page stays. Recently deleted lists each deleted page — not one moved away — with who deleted it, when, and in how many days it is removed, with *Restore* (back at its path, or beside it when another page has taken the path) and *Delete permanently*, only for those who may change the place. · fulfils AC-386e · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleChanges.test.ts`, `peopleReads.test.ts`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/history/__tests__/history.test.tsx`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/__tests__/page.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1424** — *Delete permanently* removes a page and every version at once, or with a version only that kept version (a single version is deleted only permanently, otherwise 400), is recorded as a deletion, and on both screens asks once first — *Delete this page permanently?* or *Delete this version permanently?*, there being no way back — and Cancel does nothing. · fulfils AC-386e · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleChanges.test.ts`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/history/__tests__/history.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1425** — History lists a page's kept versions newest first, the page as it is now at the top, each with when, *created* for the first, and who; choosing one shows what it changed against the one before — the front matter's fields first (title, description, tags and properties, with what each was, *(new)* or *(removed)*), then the text line by line with two lines of context and long unchanged runs folded; past 250,000 line pairs the whole text shows as replaced, and front matter that cannot be read is compared as plain lines. *Restore this version* saves it as a new version, recorded as restored, and is offered neither on the current version nor to those who may not change; the page says versions are kept 30 days and the last 20 always. History opens at a version named in the address. · fulfils AC-386f · tests `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/history/__tests__/history.test.tsx`, `lineDiff.test.ts`, `authorHref.test.tsx`, `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleChanges.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1426** — A person's search finds the words typed (trimmed, at most 200 characters, else 400; nothing typed asks no site) as fixed text, never as a pattern, ignoring case, in this place, the organisation's or both — the switch *This project · Organisation · Both*, Both by default, *Your space* in place of This project in a person's own space, and no switch in the organisation's own Knowledge; each level is asked at once with a 5-second limit and at most 100 lines; one result per page in the site's order with its title, description and its first two matching lines of text (never a front-matter line), titled from its file name when the page cannot be read, each marked with its level; a level that does not answer is named in the answer and a level with nothing is no error. The screen searches 300 ms after typing stops, the results taking the page list's place while there is text, and an organisation result opens in the organisation's Knowledge. · fulfils AC-385c · tests `hq/src/knowledge/people/__tests__/peopleReads.test.ts`, `peopleOperations.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/search/__tests__/search.test.tsx`, `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/__tests__/browse.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/orgKnowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1427** — A person makes at most 120 searches in any minute; the 121st is refused 429, saying to wait a moment and search again, and asks no site. · fulfils AC-385c
- **SC-1428** — The Knowledge tool shows a place's sections as a tree — each with its page count, a folder holding only folders still shown, pages at the top level beside them, each collapsible — then *All pages* (the default), *Needs fixing* (pages with problems, the problems in place of the description, or *Nothing needs fixing.*) and *Recently deleted*; a section's pages newest first, each with its title, description, tags, properties, when and who; filters by one tag and by each property the listed pages use, and a text box; an empty place says agents add pages as they learn. *New page* and *New section* (named, then the editor opens in it) are only for those who may change. The introduction says what the place knows, that it is looked up when needed and not read on every run, and for Your space and the organisation who sees it; a site that does not answer is said. On a phone it shows one level at a time with a way back. · fulfils AC-385a, AC-385d · tests `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/__tests__/browse.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1429** — A page reads with its title, description, tags, properties, who changed it and when (linked to the conversation or run for an agent), its text without front matter, *On this page* (its headings, not those inside code) and its problems above the text. A link to a page of the place opens in the tool, an `org:` link opens in the organisation's Knowledge, and a link to another place's page, to no page or outside the place's knowledge is not opened and says so; *Linked from* lists and opens the pages linking here. *Edit*, *Mention* (puts the page's name into the message being written, else copies it), *Move* and *Delete* are for those who may change; others get *Mention*, *History* and *Download*. A read is not tried again for a page that is gone and is tried at most 3 times for a site that is slow. · fulfils AC-385b, AC-385d · tests `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/__tests__/page.test.tsx`, `readRetry.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1430** — The editor is fields — Title, Section for a new page, Description, Tags, Properties and the text — and the person never writes front matter; a new page's name comes from its title (lower-case words joined by hyphens, at most 80 characters, then `.md`) in the chosen section, and it is offered the properties its section's other pages use; a problem shows on its field as the person types (not for what is not yet written), a save with one is not sent, and what the gate refuses shows on its field; saving opens the page, a failed save keeps the text, Cancel goes back, and a page whose front matter cannot be read opens with its text and empty fields. · fulfils AC-386b · tests `hq/src/storage/pages/knowledge/__tests__/editor.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/knowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1431** — The Knowledge tool is in a project's and Your space's Files group, its count reading *N pages* or *1 page*; the organisation's own Knowledge is at `/org/knowledge` (*Organisation › Knowledge*, for those signed in), with a Knowledge card on the organisation's Workplace giving its page count. The old list of topics, titles guessed from `INDEX.md` and the placeholder Edit are gone, and hq no longer lists or opens a place's `knowledge/` folder through the Files queries. · fulfils AC-387b · tests `hq/src/org/pages/__tests__/OrgKnowledgePage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/storage/pages/__tests__/filesToolCounts.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/orgKnowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1432** — An organisation page found by a project's search or reached by an `org:` link opens in the organisation's Knowledge; a member reads it with nothing to change it with, and reads the new text once an admin has changed it. · fulfils AC-387a · tests `hq/e2e/orgKnowledge.spec.ts`
- **SC-1433** — The organisation's knowledge is placed on the default site the first time it is used, recorded once, and never moved when another site becomes the default; two first uses at once land on one record. · fulfils AC-387c · tests `hq/src/storage/__tests__/storageSite.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/knowledge/__tests__/changeRecord.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1434** — A project whose name would read as `org`, `platform` or `personal` is refused 400, saying those name the organisation's knowledge, the platform guide and each person's own space, with nothing created; the key `ORG` is refused whether typed, in any case, or made from the name (a made key skips it, so *Organic* does not become ORG). · fulfils AC-387d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/reservedNames.test.ts`, `projectCreate.integration.test.ts`

## A conversation's places (added 2026-10-26)

A conversation reaches its own place — a project's conversation its project, a person's own conversation Your space — the organisation's knowledge, the platform guide, and the places its person added to it; no other (ADR-0152).

- **SC-1435** — A conversation's places are its own place first — Your space for a person's own conversation, its project for a project's (a project that is the person's Your space reads as Your space), its task's project for a task's thread — then the places its person added, oldest first, each with who added it and when. The places the person could add are their own space, when not held, and the projects they work in; never another person's Your space or a project they are not in; and none for anyone but the conversation's person, or in a task's thread, who read the list only. A project that is gone is said to be. · fulfils AC-388a · tests `hq/src/conversations/__tests__/places.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1436** — Only the conversation's person adds or removes a place, and only one they can open — their own space, or a project they are a member of at any role; a project they are not in, one that does not exist and someone else's Your space are refused in the same words, storing nothing; another person's conversation is refused, a task's thread takes no added place (409), adding what the conversation holds or adding twice changes nothing and keeps one record, and the conversation's own place cannot be removed (409); a removal counts from the next call that reads the places. · fulfils AC-388b · tests `hq/src/conversations/__tests__/places.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1437** — A place the person can no longer open drops out: at the start of the next turn, and on every read of the places, it is removed, so the knowledge the conversation reaches and the places the lead is told of are the rest only. · fulfils AC-388c · tests `hq/src/conversations/__tests__/places.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/lead/__tests__/placesBlock.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1438** — The lead is told its places every turn, after the cached part of its prompt: each by the name the person sees (Your space, the project's name), the name its pages start with (`personal:`, the project's) and whether it is the conversation's own or added by the person; a place the person lost is not named and a conversation whose project is gone says so; it is told that the organisation's knowledge and the platform guide are always in reach, that any other place is out of reach until the person adds it, that finding nothing there does not mean it is empty, and to offer it with `suggest_place`. · fulfils AC-388d · tests `hq/src/conversations/lead/__tests__/placesBlock.test.ts`, `placesBlock.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1439** — `suggest_place` is how the lead asks for a place, and never adds one: it is offered only to a turn that answers a person — not a report turn, a task's thread, an expired turn, or where the agent's tool is at Never — and leaves a card in the thread naming the place and the lead's reason; it finds the place only among those the person can open, by name, key or the name its pages start with (case and a trailing colon ignored) or as Your space, and refuses one they cannot open, or that does not exist, in the same fixed words, repeating nothing asked; it refuses a place already in the conversation, saying so, and suggests nothing for a turn acting for someone other than the conversation's person or for arguments that do not fit. · fulfils AC-388b, AC-388d · tests `hq/src/conversations/__tests__/placesSuggest.test.ts`, `places.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1440** — The lead's card reads *Add a place to this conversation:* the place, with the reason and what adding lets the agent do; the conversation's person alone sees *Not now* and *Add* the place, and anyone else sees that it waits for the person; *Add* adds the place as the header does, *Not now* hides the card on that screen only (it is back after a reload while the place is not added), a refused add keeps the card and says why, and once the place is in the conversation the card reads *Added to this conversation* with no buttons. · fulfils AC-388b · tests `hq/src/conversations/components/__tests__/ConversationPlaces.test.tsx`, `MessageItem.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/conversationPlaces.spec.ts`
- **SC-1441** — Under the conversation's header, *Places:* lists its own place first, with no cross, then each added place with a cross for the person only, titled with who added it; *Add place…* lists the places they can add, each described (Your space, Personal project, Project and its name), or says there is no other place they can add; choosing one says once what adding does — the agent will be able to read the place's knowledge and start work there, and what it finds can reach the conversation's other places — and adds only on *Add* (Cancel leaves it as it was); a refusal is shown in words; anyone else, and a closed conversation, read the list with no changes; on a phone it reads *2 places* (*1 place*) and a tap opens the list as a sheet with Done. · fulfils AC-388a, AC-388b · tests `hq/src/conversations/components/__tests__/ConversationPlaces.test.tsx`, `ConversationThread.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/conversationPlaces.spec.ts`
- **SC-1442** — `start_work` starts work only in a place the conversation holds: a project the person works in and the agent is assigned to, but that the conversation does not hold, is refused saying the person can add it with *Add place…* in the conversation's header or be asked with `suggest_place`, starting nothing, and is left out of what the tool lists; it is admitted once added and refused again once removed; the person's own space is refused from a project's conversation that does not hold it; only held places are listed as not assigned; and the person's membership is checked first, the conversation's places second and the agent's assignment last. · fulfils AC-388e, AC-318d · tests `hq/src/conversations/work/workers/__tests__/startWork.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/__tests__/places.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1443** — The lead's instructions say: a place not in the conversation is out of reach, not unknown and not empty, and is offered with `suggest_place`; a project it is not assigned to is not unknown either, and a project admin can assign it; *files* are a place's Files and *knowledge* its Knowledge, and *my space* is Your space, never the conversation's project; a correction to what a knowledge page says is made to the page itself, not remembered and not sent to work; a question about the product is searched in the platform guide first and answered naming its screens as the guide does. · fulfils AC-388d, AC-383a · tests `hq/src/conversations/lead/__tests__/placesInstructions.test.ts`
- **SC-1444** — With hq and its site running together, a conversation's search reaches its own project, the organisation and the platform guide, each hit marked with its level, the place's first, then the organisation's, then the guide's, with a line naming what was searched, and nothing of a project the person is in but has not added; a search scoped to that project is answered as not searched — not as empty — pointing to `suggest_place` and giving nothing of it, and reaches it once the person has added it. · fulfils AC-388a, AC-324a · tests `tests/cross-app/07-knowledge-levels.test.ts`

## The lead checked against a real model (added 2026-10-26)

- **SC-1445** — The lead's model checks send a real model what a turn sends — the lead's own instructions, memory rules, places block and tools with their descriptions, built by the code a turn uses — in a fixed world (one person, Your space, one project with a *Release process* page, the platform guide's pages), the tools answering from a script and reaching no database, site or card. They run on demand (`npm run test:models` in `hq/`) and before a release, never in `npm test`, CI's unit suite or the integration suite; without a vendor, a list of models and a key they call nothing and skip with one line saying what to set. Each check runs 3 times per model unless set otherwise (up to 5 model calls a run) and passes for a model only when every run does; each run prints a line, the whole a table and a report under `hq/.model-checks/`; the key is never shown; and the command fails when any check fails on any model. · fulfils AC-389a, AC-389b, AC-389c · tests `hq/src/conversations/__modelchecks__/__tests__/modelChecks.test.ts`
- **SC-1446** — The checks grade only the calls a run made and its words: a question about the product (a model key, voice) passes when `search_knowledge` looks in the guide, or unscoped, before the answer, and fails when answered or asked back without searching; *Look through the files in my space.* asked in a project passes with `suggest_place` for Your space and fails on `start_work` or a knowledge call in the project; a question about the project asked in Your space passes with `suggest_place` for the project; a correction to a page the lead answered from passes with a change to that page and fails if it also remembers or starts work; a question about the project's own process asked in the project passes with a search in reach before the answer. · fulfils AC-389a · tests `hq/src/conversations/__modelchecks__/__tests__/modelChecks.test.ts`

## Seeing the agents' work: opening a file (added 2026-10-27)

hq decides who may open what and signs a ticket; it never carries a file's bytes. Opening asks the site about the file (never for its content), chooses the kind, signs a preview ticket and a download ticket for the site's relay, and records who opened what. A ticket link is the relay's files address and the ticket's path (**SC-1450**).

- **SC-1509** — Opening a file answers its facts (name, size, version, type, kind, and the language of code), a preview link and a download link on the site's relay, each ticket made at the file's version and recorded before it is handed back, and signs and records nothing when nothing is opened. · fulfils AC-390a
- **SC-1510** — Someone who may not read the place, or reads another person's own files, gets not found — whether the file is there or not, and without hq asking the site, signing or recording anything; a path the site refuses as outside the place, or not there, is not found too; a request with no session, or naming no file of a place, an artefact or a knowledge page, is refused outright. · fulfils AC-390e
- **SC-1511** — When a file cannot be reached, opening says which of the states it is, at once and without signing a ticket: the site's relay is not connected (the site is not asked), the relay has no address a browser can reach, the site has no relay or only a removed one, the site is offline, timed out, disconnected or too old, or hq no longer knows the site; any other failure is let through as an error. · fulfils AC-390d
- **SC-1512** — On an installation with no files address a PDF is answered as needing one, with a download only, and text is previewed through the bare door. · fulfils AC-390b
- **SC-1513** — A project's artefact opens for its members and for no one else, and the organisation's knowledge pages open for anyone signed in, named without an id, while a person's own pages open only for them. · fulfils AC-390a
- **SC-1514** — Several files open in one request, in order, through the one open, at most 50 at a time and a few in flight at once; one that throws is answered as not found or unavailable without failing the others, a request that is not a list of files in a place, an artefact or a knowledge page (too many, a path that steps up, an upload) is refused, and so is a request with no session. · fulfils AC-392a
- **SC-1515** — A chip's facts are asked without opening: hq checks access as opening does, asks each site once about its files (and stops asking a site that does not answer), answers each file's facts with the kind core chooses, and signs and records nothing — even with no relay; a file the site says is not there is gone, a folder of a place a folder, an artefact that is not a file gone, a refusal from the site not found and any other failure unavailable; someone outside the project, or another person's own files, get not found without the site being asked. · fulfils AC-393b
- **SC-1516** — A task's attachment opens through hq's own storage: for a member of its project it answers the facts and a preview and a download on hq's relay, both upload tickets for this person, at the version on disk and recorded; the kind is chosen from the name and first bytes; no such attachment, a file gone from disk and someone not allowed are not found (access checked before the relay is asked); and while hq's relay is not connected it says so before reading or signing anything. · fulfils AC-394a
- **SC-1517** — A chip for a task is answered with its key, title and status, by its key or its id, each task found once; an attachment is answered by its name with its id for the click and gone when the task has none by that name; a working file is asked in the task's own folder, all in one request (one of Your space has no folder to open by task); someone who may not see the task gets not found for it and its files, never the title; nothing is signed or recorded; and what is not a task reference, or too many at once, is refused. · fulfils AC-393b
- **SC-1518** — A link to an upload hq keeps (a task attachment or a picture) is a ticket on hq's relay, for this person and this file at its version on disk, signed and recorded, as a preview or a download, under the bare door on a bare address; it is none for someone not allowed (whether the file is there or not), a path that does not decode, no session — except a picture the sign-in page shows, on a ticket naming no person — and none while hq's relay is not connected. · fulfils AC-391a
- **SC-1519** — hq-server answers no upload path with its bytes, signed in or not: every file hq keeps reaches a browser through hq's relay. · fulfils AC-397b
- **SC-1520** — An upload opens by the rule for what it is: a task attachment to a member of the task's project (a person outside is refused the same way whether the file is there or not, and with no session or for a task or file that is not there); an agent's, a person's and a team's picture to anyone signed in and to no one signed out; the organisation's to anyone signed in and, signed out, only the current ones while the sign-in page shows the cover; and a name hq never writes, a team that is not a team id, a link (out of the storage folder or inside it), a folder, a link on the way and a path that steps out are refused. · fulfils AC-397d
- **SC-1521** — A picture's ticket is signed once for the same picture asked twice (also at the same moment) and given again until a minute before it expires, then signed anew; it is signed anew when the file changed, for another person, purpose or relay, is not kept when signing fails, and at most a set number of pictures are kept, dropping the one used longest ago; a task attachment's and a site's file's ticket is signed on every ask. · fulfils AC-397f
- **SC-1522** — hq signs a file ticket that core accepts, with every claim and a five-minute life, refused by another site, on another relay's line, once expired, for a purpose not accepted or a ticket missing what it must name; it signs only the fields of the file's own space, a knowledge page's ticket the site accepts, an upload's ticket for hq's relay (its audience the relay, a site refuses it), and a ticket with no person only for a picture shown before sign-in; a relay pass is signed for this site on this relay with an hour's life, refused by another relay or presented by another site, and cannot be passed off as a ticket nor a ticket as a pass. · fulfils AC-397c
- **SC-1523** — hq records every ticket it issues — who, which place and path, which version, when, and whether to preview or download — before handing it back, hands back no ticket when the record could not be written, and records each space by its own fields. · fulfils AC-397g
- **SC-1524** — hq's relay exists when `HQ_RELAY_SECRET` is set to 32 characters or more (trimmed), is absent when it is unset, blank or shorter and says then that files cannot be opened and what to set; it is one row, its secret hashed, on the files address, any later outcome reported, and without the secret an existing row is taken out of service so the old secret is refused, and nothing is written when there is no row. · fulfils AC-397a
- **SC-1525** — An optional `HQ_RELAY_LINE_URL` gives hq's relay's line an absolute web address (http, https, ws or wss) for a site that cannot reach it through hq's own address; unset or blank leaves the path unchanged, another scheme or a non-address is refused with a warning that says what is wrong and what to do, and it overrides for hq's relay only. · fulfils AC-397a
- **SC-1526** — A relay's link to hq refuses a missing or wrong secret with 401 whatever version it states, and a relay two majors apart or stating none with 426 and hq's own version; an accepted relay is welcomed with its id and recorded as seen, each report is recorded and a frame that is not one dropped, dialling again replaces the old link, other paths are left alone, and the link authenticates only the secret it holds and never revives a removed relay. · fulfils AC-397a
- **SC-1527** — hq places a site on a relay: a site with none goes to hq's relay with its line path, a site keeps the relay it holds while that relay is usable, one on a removed relay moves to hq's, every unplaced site is placed at boot, removing a relay leaves its sites to be placed again, and a removed or unknown site, or no relay available, gives no placement. · fulfils AC-397a
- **SC-1528** — When a site connects, hq sends it its relay's line path and a fresh pass for this site and this relay (nothing when none is available, to a site that is not connected, that went while its pass was made, or that speaks a protocol before relays, and never throwing when the site cannot be asked); before the pass ends, while the same connection lasts, it sends a new one, stops for a connection that was replaced or closed, and tries again soon when the site did not take the assignment. · fulfils AC-397a
- **SC-1529** — hq's relay is named after its machine and tagged as its site is — "hq-box · HQ's relay" — wherever a file cannot be opened, so a stopped relay is not read as the machine being down; every other relay keeps its own name. · fulfils AC-390d
- **SC-1530** — hq tells the Fleet Console about its own relay — address, the sites it serves and its traffic from its last report — and says it is not answering after a minute of silence, when quiet or when never heard; a report that is not its shape is read for nothing. · fulfils AC-396a
- **SC-1531** — A task attachment up to 10 MB uploads: the upload gets the same body limit as a picture's and every other operation keeps the default; a larger file is refused in the browser, saying the limit, before anything is sent. · fulfils AC-391b

## Seeing the agents' work: the Files tool, links and chips (added 2026-10-27)

People meet a file in the Files tool, in a workspace, in an artefact, in a task, in a link and in a chip beside a conversation; all draw it in ui's one frame (**SC-1578**), opened by hq's one open action.

- **SC-1532** — A screen opens its selected file once, however often it draws or mounts it (twice in development, or after a layout change moves it) and again for a new selection, opens nothing when nothing is selected, opens afresh on *Try again* or *Show the new version*, says why when hq refuses or cannot be reached, opens again shortly before its tickets end keeping the old answer on screen meanwhile (waiting for a hidden page to be looked at), and does not open again for an answer that carries no tickets. · fulfils AC-390d
- **SC-1533** — A file opened in the Files tool is drawn by the frame: Download is a link on the relay, *Full screen* is a button in the header and *Copy link*, *Copy path*, *Show in Files* and *Mention* are under ⋯ (**SC-1871**); full screen shows the same frame without opening the file again and puts it back (**SC-1872**); a state is worded from the open answer and *Try again* opens afresh; when hq refuses it says what hq said; and a Markdown file draws its relative image from its own folder in the place. · fulfils AC-390a
- **SC-1534** — In a running hq a file opened in Files is drawn by kind: Markdown as a page with a relative image and a sized image tag drawn through the relay, code highlighted with its language in the facts line, a CSV as a table with its header and rows (quoted commas kept), an image with its pixel size, and a PDF by the viewer on the files address with its page count. · fulfils AC-390b
- **SC-1535** — A file is named as the agent sees its place — `files/…`, `workspaces/<id>/…`, `artefacts/…` — and read back in the owner's folder, naming nothing outside those folders or a folder alone, and a chip is labelled by the path after its folder; the paths written in a message are found without a sentence's full stop, leaving a path inside another path or a web address and a folder alone. · fulfils AC-393b
- **SC-1536** — A stable link is the place's address with the tool and the file, never a conversation, a site, a relay or a ticket; each kind of file has its parameters, a link is read back into its place and reference from this hq only, and an agent's link to a file opens in its tool while every other link is left alone; a link of this hq pasted into a message is found, and a link of another is not. · fulfils AC-393a
- **SC-1537** — *Copy link* gives the place's address with the file, without the conversation open on the page, and starts from the place it is given where the file opened beside a conversation; the link opens the file in place, and *Mention* names a workspace's file as the agent sees it, under its workspace. · fulfils AC-393a
- **SC-1538** — A link to a file that is gone says "This file isn't here any more" with the folder it was in, and opens that folder; a link into a place someone cannot see gets the place's usual "not found", never the file's name. *(Changed 2026-11-10: a file asked for where it was mentioned says what was looked for, **SC-1874**.)* · fulfils AC-390e
- **SC-1539** — A path an agent writes in a reply becomes a chip with icon, name and kind that opens the file in the file viewer over the main area, the chat staying beside it (**SC-1869**), in the conversation's place; a link to an artefact or a workspace file written by an old message is a chip too; a chip asks after its file's facts only, opening none, so no ticket is signed for it; a file that is gone is struck through with its name kept (**SC-1880**); text that names no folder of the place stays text; and where words are not read in a conversation's place they stay plain text. · fulfils AC-393b
- **SC-1540** — A stable link pasted into a message shows as the same chip, opening in the file viewer when it names this place and going to the other place's address, asking that place for nothing, when it names another; a file opened from a chip shows in the frame and its *Copy link* gives the place's stable link, not the conversation's. · fulfils AC-393c
- **SC-1541** — A task named in a reply is a chip with its key, title and status that goes to the task; a task the person may not see stays plain text and never shows a title; a task's attachment opens in the file viewer (**SC-1869**) through the task's opening and a working file in the task's folder; and a person's message shows the same chip. · fulfils AC-393b
- **SC-1542** — A folder named in a conversation is a folder chip — a path written with a trailing slash at once, one written without once the site says it is a folder, never a gone file — that opens the place's Files tool at that folder, asking and opening nothing; on its own line it falls back from the card to the chip, and in a person's message it is the same chip. · fulfils AC-393b
