- **SC-1543** — *Mention* puts a file in the message box as a chip at the caret (at the end if the box was never focused), between the words typed either side, text typed after it is kept, one Backspace or Delete deletes the chip whole and only it, and what is sent is exactly the text with the paths written out; with no message box beside it, *Mention* copies the reference instead; dictation writes beside a chip keeping the chip and the same field. · fulfils AC-393d
- **SC-1544** — A path typed into the message box becomes a chip once something follows it (not while it is being typed), one pasted at once (only plain text goes in) and one when the box loses the focus, and stays text when it names no file; the box breaks a line with Shift+Enter, sends with Enter (not while a word is being composed), copies the paths and not the chips' labels, and keeps its role, label and placeholder. · fulfils AC-393d
- **SC-1545** — Typing `@` at a word start in the message box opens a picker of the conversation's places' files, the most recent under a heading, the field keeping the focus; it narrows as the person types, asking once the typing rests; arrow keys and Enter, Tab or a click choose a file and the `@…` becomes its chip with nothing sent; Escape closes it leaving the text until another `@` is typed, a space closes it, `@` inside a word does not open it, it says when it is finding, when nothing matches and which place is not answering, Enter sends when there is nothing to choose, and a box that takes no mentions or has no conversation has no picker. · fulfils AC-393e
- **SC-1546** — The picker's files are found by name on the site holding the conversation's place — a project conversation's place, or the person's own files in their own conversation and not a place they added — answering each file's reference and folder; a place whose site does not answer is left out with a note, someone who cannot read the conversation is refused without a site being asked, a project the person has left is left out, and a request with no session or a fragment over the cap is refused. · fulfils AC-393e
- **SC-1547** — The lead's instructions name every reference form from the place's root, say that a relative path is not clickable and how to put the root in front of a listing, and say never to write an address or make up hq's. · fulfils AC-393f
- **SC-1548** — `get_task_details` lists a task's attachments with name, size, label and reference, oldest first, with the task key, an empty list for a task with none, and refuses a caller not entitled to the task's project naming no attachment; `attach_file` answers the attachment id, file name and reference, and no URL or path; a task reference is read by its key or id, a working file at any depth and an attachment by its one file name, and written so that it reads back whole. · fulfils AC-393f
- **SC-1549** — Above a workspace's files and the Files tool's `files/` a git status line reads *clean*, *changed* with a count that is a floor when cut off, *no commits yet*, a merge or rebase stopped halfway and a detached commit, with the last commit's age in words; it reads the folder it sits above and again when the window has focus, and there is no line for a folder that is not a repository, while it is read, or when the site does not answer; hq asks the site holding the workspace only after checking the person may read it, and turns a site refusal into an error carrying only its detail. · fulfils AC-395a

## Seeing the agents' work: embedded files and a task's files (added 2026-10-27)

Markdown is drawn in one place for every surface — a file, a knowledge page, a task's description and comments, an agent's reply — and an embedded file is drawn by the same boundary as an opened one. Only an image is drawn inside the text; a file of another kind is a card or a chip.

- **SC-1550** — Markdown given no place draws as before: an image tag stays text and an image loads as written. · fulfils AC-392d
- **SC-1551** — An image in a place is embedded through the relay and resolved against the page's folder; a path out of the place is a broken image with its name asking for nothing, and a file that is not there or not the person's is a broken image; every embed on screen is asked for in one request, each file once, and later ones as they come into view; a failed request answers every embed as unavailable, and a very long page is split into calls the action accepts. · fulfils AC-392a
- **SC-1552** — A sized image tag draws like a Markdown image, alone or inside a sentence, keeping only `src`, `alt`, `width` and `height` (a width or height that is not a number dropped, the first of a repeated attribute kept) and dropping an `onerror`, a `style`, a `srcset` or anything else; every other tag — a script, an iframe, a link, an element with an event handler — stays text, and an image tag whose `src` is `javascript:` is treated as nothing a file can be. · fulfils AC-392d
- **SC-1553** — An image on another website, written as Markdown or as an image tag, waits for a click on *Load* before that website is asked for anything, in a page and in an agent's reply. · fulfils AC-392b
- **SC-1554** — A link to a file alone on its line is a card with the file's facts that opens it, and inside a sentence a chip with its kind; an image that turns out not to be one is drawn as a chip with the facts already opened, asking nothing more; a link to a website or an anchor stays a link. · fulfils AC-392c
- **SC-1555** — Where an embed points is decided one way: a relative path is read against the page's folder, a path out of the place names the file it points at, `javascript:`, `data:`, `file:`, `mailto:` and anchors name nothing, a place's folders (`files/`, `artefacts/`, `workspaces/`) are siblings of one another, the organisation's knowledge has no siblings, the same file is keyed the same way however written, and `attachments/<key>/<file>` in a task's text is that task's own attachment by key or id and elsewhere is left to the page's folder. · fulfils AC-392a
- **SC-1556** — A task's own attachments in its text are drawn inline, opened through hq, whether written as a Markdown image or an image tag (`attachments/<its key>/<image>`); one hq does not find or the person may not open is a broken image, another task's attachment is never asked for and is shown as outside, a link to a file of another kind alone on its line is a card, and a click on an embedded attachment opens the image in the file viewer over the task, or draws it with nothing to click where the screen opens nothing beside. · fulfils AC-392a
- **SC-1557** — A click on an embedded file in a task's text opens it in the file viewer over the task (**SC-1869**) — the task's own working file or attachment, another task's, a file of the place's `files/`, an artefact, a file of Your space and a workspace's file itself, not the workspace's README; a task's files resolve against its project, and against its person in Your space. · fulfils AC-394d
- **SC-1558** — A knowledge page embeds an image its text names from its own section of the same knowledge, and the editor's preview draws embedded files as the saved page does, the place's Files beside the page's own section. · fulfils AC-392a
- **SC-1559** — A task's attachment opens in the file viewer through hq's attachment action and draws in the frame with Download on the relay, with one ‹ back named for the task and no ✕ over ⋯, and a working file opens through the open action in the task's own folder; a file that is not there says what was looked for (**SC-1874**); with no file open the task is alone. *(Changed 2026-11-10: it opened beside the task.)* · fulfils AC-394a
- **SC-1560** — A task's files are listed under its attachments: its own folder on the site (nothing said when empty) with folders to step into and a file opened by its path in the folder; attachments open by name and still download. · fulfils AC-394b
- **SC-1561** — A task's file has a link form that names the project, the task and the file, never a ticket; *Copy link* copies it, it opens the task and its file in the Tasks tool on a project's address (in Your space, in Chat's Tasks tool), someone who cannot see the project gets its usual "not found" and never the file's name, a task's file in the address names a working file by its path in the task's folder or an attachment by its id, and an address that names a tool or no task is left as it is. · fulfils AC-394c

## Seeing the agents' work: what it stops (added 2026-10-27)

The thirteen threats the design names and the defence that holds against each. They are properties of hq, the relay and the site together, so each is cited by the tests of every part that keeps it, and by the cross-app tests that run hq and a site as they run in an installation. **No file byte passes through hq-server**: the cross-app tests check that nothing hq-server answered carries any file's or upload's content.

- **SC-1562** — A forged or altered ticket opens nothing: a ticket not signed by hq's key, one with any field changed, one for another site, signed for another relay or expired, is refused by the relay or the site and no byte is sent; a site takes a relay's line only with hq's pass for that site and relay. · T1 · fulfils AC-397c
- **SC-1563** — A leaked ticket is worth little: it is minutes long, names one file and one purpose, is dead once the file changes (409, no bytes), is sent with `no-referrer`, never logged by a relay or a site, and loaded in frames and fetches, not the address bar. · T2 · fulfils AC-397c
- **SC-1564** — A relay carries bytes only for the sites assigned to it, holds nothing at rest and asks a site for nothing but what a ticket names; whoever runs a fleet machine is trusted with what it holds or carries, as a site is. · T3 · fulfils AC-397d
- **SC-1565** — A rogue machine posing as a relay gets nothing: a site dials only the relay address hq names (and refuses any other it is told to), and a relay takes a line only with a pass hq signed for that site and that relay. · T4 · fulfils AC-397c
- **SC-1566** — A compromised relay cannot ask a site for other files: the site's line serves `files.serve` alone, refuses every method hq's channel serves, and checks a ticket for every read, so a relay can only replay a ticket it saw, for that file, until it expires. · T5 · fulfils AC-397d
- **SC-1567** — An agent's symbolic link out of the place gives nothing: the site resolves the real path and refuses anything outside the place's storage — a link, a directory planted as a link, a hard link, a path with `..` — when it states a file, serves it or finds files, and the relay never follows a link in the uploads folder. · T6 · fulfils AC-397d
- **SC-1568** — A hostile file runs no script as the viewer: it is served from the files address, another origin with no hq session, with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` and `nosniff`; text-like kinds are drawn as text and never as HTML; Markdown's only HTML is an `<img>` with its four attributes; a PDF with JavaScript runs none. · T7 · fulfils AC-397e
- **SC-1569** — No other page can read a file from the relay: CORS is for the app's address only, never with credentials, and the ticket is the only authority. · T8 · fulfils AC-397e
- **SC-1570** — Someone who loses access to a file keeps the ticket they hold only until it expires within minutes; every new open checks access again, and the page opens again before its tickets end. · T9 · fulfils AC-397c
- **SC-1571** — Flooding is held back: text and Markdown are shown to 1 MB, JSON to 5 MB, a table to 10,000 rows and 5 MB, an image to 20 MB and a PDF to 50 MB (a download has no cap and is streamed), a person has 16 streams at a relay, a site reads 32 files at once, a slow reader holds only its own stream, and a slow site affects only its own relay streams, never hq. · T10 · fulfils AC-397f
- **SC-1572** — Who opened what can be told: hq records every ticket it issues, with the person, the place, the path, the version, the time and whether it was a preview or a download. · T11 · fulfils AC-397g
- **SC-1573** — A repository's own configuration runs no code while git is read: hooks, `fsmonitor`, clean filters and an external diff are off for the site's status read, and the read never blocks an agent's commit. · T12 · fulfils AC-395b
- **SC-1574** — Opening a page tells no other website who is reading it: an image on another website waits for a click on *Load*. · T13 · fulfils AC-392b
- **SC-1575** — With hq and a site running together, opening, downloading and embedding a file of every kind sends no file content through hq-server: hq answers the facts and two links on the files address, and everything hq-server answered is searched for the file's bytes; someone outside the place gets not found, whether the file exists or not, and nothing is signed for them. · fulfils AC-397b
- **SC-1576** — A ticket hq signed opens the file through hq's relay: the preview link serves the bytes inline with the sandbox, `nosniff`, no-referrer and CORS for hq only, the download link as an attachment, a range as 206 with its bytes, `HEAD` the size with no body, and an HTML file and a PDF are served on the files address only, under the sandbox. · fulfils AC-390a
- **SC-1577** — hq's uploads open through hq's relay with nothing to configure: a task's attachment opens for a member of its project from hq's storage and the link is recorded, an agent's picture opens inline on the files address, someone outside the project gets no link and no session gets none to a picture, hq-server answers no upload path with the file, and on hq's own address (the bare door) a download and text are served as attachments, never inline, an HTML preview as plain text to save and a PDF preview refused. · fulfils AC-397a

## The Library and its assets (added 2026-10-28)

- **SC-1608** — An asset is a record keyed on its kind and `publisher/name`, with a trusted publisher's name no other signer can take, a place's skill keyed on its place and path, one draft per asset or place skill, and content that no record names removed only when nothing names it: content any asset version, draft or run record still names is kept. · fulfils AC-398d
- **SC-1609** — Add skill offers Agenthix's shelf: it lists what ships with this HQ, marks what the organisation already has, offers Install for what is new and narrows by what a skill is suited to (the 17 trade skills carry a hint); installing from the shelf installs `agenthix/<name>` saying *Signed by Agenthix*, installing the same version again reuses it, and the same version with other content is refused. A Library maintainer sees the shelf and installs from it; upload and a link are the administrator's, unless the organisation lets maintainers install from outside. · fulfils AC-398b
- **SC-1610** — An upload, a ZIP or a folder sent file by file, is read under one set of rules: every file kept under the root, two entries with one name, names that differ only by case or Unicode encoding, hidden and system entries and a path that climbs out are refused rather than skipped; a plain folder with only a `SKILL.md` is read as unsigned; a format 1 package or a manifest that fails core's checks is refused; and a refused file is counted, not silently dropped. · fulfils AC-398b
- **SC-1611** — A link is fetched safely: only https; the address is resolved once and the connection pinned to it; a private, loopback, link-local or unique-local address, an odd spelling of one (`127.1`, `0x7f.0.0.1`, a number) or a name any of whose addresses is private is refused, and again on every redirect, at most three; the body is streamed under the package size cap (a declared length too large is refused unread) and a timeout; no cookies or credentials are sent; the signature is fetched beside it and a signature that is there but unreadable refuses the whole fetch; and whatever went wrong, the person is told the same thing. · fulfils AC-398e
- **SC-1612** — Before anything is installed the person is told one of four things: *Signed by* a trusted publisher (Install); signed by someone new, naming the signer and saying a signature tells who published it, not that it is safe (trust this publisher and install, install this once, or cancel); not signed, installable only after ticking *I've read it* and only if the organisation allows unsigned assets; or *changed after it was signed*, refused with no way to install. A template says what comes with it. · fulfils AC-399a
- **SC-1613** — The publisher of an installed asset is the verified signer, never the manifest's claim: a trusted package installs under the name the organisation gave its signer, an unsigned one as `unsigned/<name>` apart from any signed asset of the same name, and a package claiming `agenthix`, or an embedded skill claiming another publisher, is refused unless Agenthix signed it; Agenthix's image is trusted by where it is; a name another signer already holds, or a built-in's identity, is refused; a new keyless signer is offered a name that is never a reserved one; and another signer's package is never offered as an update of an installed one. · fulfils AC-399b
- **SC-1614** — A signature is checked offline against what hq ships: a keyless bundle must carry its transparency-log inclusion proof, verified against the trust root that ships in hq's image (a Rekor v2 entry with a timestamp authority's timestamp included); a bundle with no proof or no log entry, a trust root that does not know its log or that has expired, or a key nobody added is *unverifiable*, never trusted; a statement changed by one byte, or a digest changed to the new statement without the signature, is *tampered*; a key-pair bundle is trusted only by a key the organisation holds now, and a key removed is refused; and a release cannot ship with a trust root older than the limit. · fulfils AC-399a
- **SC-1615** — An administrator trusts a publisher exactly: a keyless publisher is bound to its issuer, its exact certificate identity less the ref it ran from (no pattern, an address that could never match refused), and its source repository and ref, tags or the default branch only, so a feature branch that runs the release workflow cannot sign as the publisher; a person is trusted by issuer and email alone; a key pair by the sha256 of its public key, and what is not a public key is refused; a reserved name is refused; Agenthix is always listed first and cannot be removed; a publisher can be renamed (the name shown only) or removed, and one remembered from *install this once* can be trusted later; the list says what each proves and how many of its assets are installed; and every refusal ends "Nothing was changed." · fulfils AC-399c
- **SC-1616** — An administrator sets the organisation's install policy in Settings › Library and skills (in the Settings group of the admin menu): trusted publishers, whether unsigned assets are allowed after the warning (the default) or never, and who may install from outside (administrators by default); a member is told the page is for administrators; choosing *On by default* for skills found in files asks first and leaves the setting alone if it is not confirmed (and a stricter choice changes at once); and a value that is none of the choices is refused. · fulfils AC-399d, AC-402c
- **SC-1617** — An administrator names Library maintainers, who create, edit and publish every Library asset, accept updates, roll back and review proposals, and install from the shelf, but install from outside only when the organisation allows it and never remove an asset, choose which agents have it, change trust settings or name maintainers; the page shows the reach (*publish skills that run for every agent, in every project, including ones they can't access*) first and names nobody until confirmed, refuses naming an administrator, someone who left, or anyone when a maintainer asks, and removing the role leaves the history that names them; a member or an account with no role has nothing; and every refusal says who holds the action and ends "Nothing was changed." · fulfils AC-402a
- **SC-1618** — Installing matches identities: an asset not here is installed, the same version reused, a newer version from the same signer offered as an update, an older one leaves the installed asset alone, the same version with other content refused, a removed asset brought back, and one identity is installed once for every agent that has it; installing says what comes with it (for a template, its role skills apart from the shared ones it brings, which it already has, which are newer, update offered). · fulfils AC-398c, AC-398d
- **SC-1619** — An asset's versions are listed in SemVer order with the update offered; an update goes only to a newer version, and only for the same signer; *Update available* shows what changed file by file; an administrator or a Library maintainer rolls back to an earlier version with a reason, the dialog saying every agent that has it uses it from its next turn, and may install an older version from an upload on purpose, the history recording who, when and why; a refusal is shown and nothing on screen changes. · fulfils AC-400a, AC-400b, AC-402a
- **SC-1620** — Going to a version Agenthix withdrew (its list ships in hq's image and a row can say it too) is refused until the person accepts a warning naming why; another publisher's skill of the same name is not withdrawn by it; and the list is read strictly. · fulfils AC-400b
- **SC-1621** — *Skip this version* hides an offered update and every older one until a later version is held, and dismissing news on the organisation's own copy hides it until a later original is out; *Update available* and *news* show only for what was not skipped. · fulfils AC-400c
- **SC-1622** — An administrator gives an installed skill to chosen agents (*Add to agents…*, *Remove from Ada*) or every agent; *Remove* is for administrators only and names the agents that have it, asking first even when none does, and Cancel leaves it in the Library; their pages then show it as missing, with *Install*. · fulfils AC-400d, AC-403b
- **SC-1623** — A skill is checked against the Agent Skills rules on every save, upload and install: a name of 1 to 64 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, unique by identity; a description of 1 to 1024 characters; a header key a run drops flagged as ignored when run; every file `SKILL.md` names present; a name the file calls itself that differs warned; files a disk would store differently refused; and a body over 500 lines a warning, not a refusal. · fulfils AC-401b
- **SC-1624** — *New skill* asks for a name (checked as it is typed) and a description and starts a plain skill, one with `scripts/` and `references/`, or asks an agent to write it, opening the editor on a draft that no agent sees: an asset with a draft and no version, one draft shared by everyone who may edit it, saved a moment after the person stops typing (*Saving…* until then), never over a draft someone else changed until the person chooses, a refused save tried again only after the next change; `package.json` is not editable; and discarding the draft of a skill never published removes it. · fulfils AC-401a
- **SC-1625** — *Publish* asks for a note and makes the next version, the next minor unless the person chooses patch or major (1.0.0 the first), writing that version into `SKILL.md` before the fingerprint is taken, shows every changed file, publishes only the draft revision the publisher saw (refused if the draft changed since, or there is none), says why it cannot be used while a change is saving, when nothing changed, or when the person may not publish and who may, and after a first publish offers *Add to agents…*. · fulfils AC-401c
- **SC-1626** — The editor shows an asset's files in a tree with a marker for each change (added, modified, deleted and struck through, renamed with its old name; named for assistive technology), a rename recognised from a file removed and another added with the same bytes, a binary file listed as changed without lines, and three modes: view, edit (text over 1 MB read-only) and a per-file diff against the version in use; the editor, a place's file dialogs and Files fill the space they are given and scroll inside. · fulfils AC-401b
- **SC-1627** — *Ask an agent to edit* opens a dialog over the page that names, before anything is sent, the agent that will answer (the person's main agent in their own space, unless they pick another) and where the conversation will be. · fulfils AC-401f
- **SC-1628** — Editing an installed asset that another publisher signed makes the organisation's own copy (`fernhill/code-review`, made here) with its provenance, in the original's place for the agents that had it; the original stays installed but set aside, one click back (*Use the original again*), and removing the copy in use gives its agents the original back; in the Library the two are one entry (the copy's, with the original under it, counted once in the type chips and found under *Set aside*), each page shows the other, and a newer original shows as news with what changed; an asset the organisation made is edited in place. · fulfils AC-401d
- **SC-1629** — A copy carries a dated *based on* list written once when it is made: it inherits the original's list and adds the original as one line recorded by hq, every version carries it, and a person may add only a stated credit (a source, optionally a version and the day it was copied; an empty source is refused, and a list over the limit is refused rather than losing a line); a line shows as *verified* only when hq wrote it here, holds that version and the fingerprint matches, and otherwise as stated by whoever stated it or by the package's signer. · fulfils AC-401e
- **SC-1630** — The Library comes after Agents in the sidebar and shows every installed asset, skills, agent, board and team templates, in one searched view (the old Skills address opens it narrowed to skills): built-in skills first, each row naming the asset and saying who has it, who signed it or *made here*, and its state (*Update available*, *Draft*, *Waiting for review*, *Set aside*, *Unsigned*); the whole row opens the asset, states sit on a line of their own and every action is in its ⋯ menu; a Type chip, a Show chip (a second click clears it) and the search narrow the list, keep it in the address, count over what the other filters leave and say when nothing matches; an administrator sees *Add skill*, named for what it adds today; and the header leads to the review queue when proposals wait. · fulfils AC-398a
- **SC-1631** — The Marketplace is a button on the Library that opens its own page only while its flag is on; the flag is at preview (**SC-1761**), so the button appears in a development build and not in production or in a test run as shipped, and while it is off the Marketplace's server route refuses (404, *not available*, nothing changed). · fulfils AC-398a
- **SC-1632** — An asset's page has its own address and holds what it is, its content, the agents with it (the list to choose from only for someone who may choose), its versions (any can be viewed, with what changed since), the organisation's own copy and the original, credit and licence, who vouches for it in words (*Signed by Agenthix* for the image), when and by which agent it was last used, and *Remove*; a member reads the page and is offered no change, someone with no role in the organisation is refused, and an address that is not an asset says so in words. · fulfils AC-398a, AC-406d

## Built-in skills, templates and Kernel's own skills (added 2026-10-28)

- **SC-1633** — Two built-in skills, `working-with-git` and `skill-creator`, are installed at boot from hq's image as `agenthix/<name>` at version 1.0.0: a second boot with the same image changes nothing, a new image's change is the next major version, an older image brings back the version already held, and a built-in the image no longer ships is removed; `working-with-git` carries the workflow guide its `SKILL.md` names and never tells an agent to push straight to a default branch, and a folder that fails the rules is reported and left out. · fulfils AC-409a
- **SC-1634** — A built-in reaches a run only while it is on: an administrator switches one off and on again (the list says which are on, and that git is for repositories), the switch is kept across boots and new versions, a member reads the list but cannot switch one; the run is given those that are on, `working-with-git` only when the run works in a workspace with a repository (asked of the site holding the location, and no when it cannot say), staged as `platform-<name>`; a built-in switched off is left out of the dispatch and out of staging. · fulfils AC-409a
- **SC-1635** — The templates in hq's image are every agent template but Kernel, the team templates and the task templates (none of them a board template), each team template's projects carrying task types and fields rather than boards, each installed as `agenthix/<name>` without what it carries, and none keeps a copy of a shared skill: the build copies each skill its agents name from the shelf (the 23 shared skills the team templates used to keep by hand), fails a template that keeps a copy, gives each the version that already holds its content or the next major for new content, and keeps a role skill under its template's name; a template whose content changed in a new image is offered as an update and taken when accepted, one that names a skill no source has or fails its checks is reported, and a team template's shared skills are built from the shelf. · fulfils AC-408a
- **SC-1636** — An uploaded template installs through the same check: it says what comes with it, reuses a shared skill already here at its version, refuses a task template that carries skills and a template claiming Agenthix without its signature, and refuses a team template that names people or an agent that names a skill the package does not hold, a handle that names a path, and a template with a missing state. · fulfils AC-408a
- **SC-1637** — Removing a template is for administrators, names what was made from it, asks first; an image template an administrator removed is not installed again at boot until the administrator puts it back from the shelf, which lists the removed templates for administrators only. · fulfils AC-400d, AC-408a
- **SC-1638** — A template's page says what it carries and what was made from it; an agent template opens into *Create an agent* and a team template into its review from the row's menu (*Use this team*), which is left off for someone who is not an administrator. · fulfils AC-408b
- **SC-1639** — An agent's export is an agent template package carrying every skill the agent has but the built-ins, installs in the Library through the same pipeline, is a version that grows with each export, and is refused by the agent page's import. · fulfils AC-408c
- **SC-1640** — Kernel's own skills, `assembly`, `orchestrate` and `planning`, are installed from hq's image as Kernel's role skills at boot: a second boot changes nothing, a skill an administrator took from Kernel is left out, a Kernel that had none gets them on a later boot, a changed skill is offered as an update, a folder that fails the rules is reported while the rest install, and a missing folder is an error. · fulfils AC-408d

## Skills found in a place's files (added 2026-10-28)

- **SC-1641** — What a scan of a place found is recorded as one row per folder, under a path that names the root it was found under and keeps two workspaces with the same folder apart; a finding with an unsafe path or workspace, a path that climbs or hides, or a fingerprint that is not a digest records no row; at most the per-place cap is kept (everything under it is kept), a scan cut short by a bound marks nothing gone, a skill is marked gone only under the roots the scan covered, the front matter's name and description are kept, and a switch stays on its row while a new fingerprint does not move it. · fulfils AC-404a
- **SC-1642** — A place's Skills page (after Workflows under *Agents at work* and in Your space) lists what was found, grouped by where (the place, one agent, each workspace), and a skill runs only when switched on at the fingerprint hq last saw: under *Off by default* everything found is listed and off until switched on, and a changed skill goes off again, under *On by default* everything found runs, under *Never* nothing is read from files; *All on* names what it would switch before it does and switches only the skills still as they were shown; a switch for another fingerprint, or when the site's files are no longer what the place was last seen holding, is refused; the default is a labelled setting showing the organisation's beside it, a place only ever stricter than the organisation and a looser choice unavailable; and its words are only words for someone who may not change it. · fulfils AC-404a, AC-404b
- **SC-1643** — Writing is open and publishing is the control: a project's members edit drafts and add skills, its administrators publish, switch, roll back, review and set the default, and viewers read; an organisation administrator who is not in the project acts nothing there; in Your space the person does everything; and a viewer sees a skill and no control. · fulfils AC-402b, AC-404e
- **SC-1644** — Publishing from files gives a place's skill a version: the editor keeps the draft out of the place's live folder, *Publish* asks for a note (switching on asks for none) and writes the skill's folder through the site with the number in its `metadata`, hq records version, fingerprint and content, and nothing is recorded when the site cannot write the files; rolling back asks why, records no made-up reason when none is written, and publishes the older files as the next version; publishing is refused for files that changed again since they were shown. · fulfils AC-404c
- **SC-1645** — A change made outside the editor shows the skill as *changed in files, not published*, keeping its version, with each changed file's text then and now and two actions, *Publish as the next version* or *Put back the newest version* (the newest version's files put back over the change); a skill never published says so and is off, and a switch that stopped holding says why: switched off because its files changed since a named person switched it on. · fulfils AC-404b, AC-404c
- **SC-1646** — A workspace repository's skill is read-only in HQ: it can be switched on and off and nothing else, its versions being its commits. · fulfils AC-404d
- **SC-1647** — *Add to this place* copies the pinned version of an installed skill into the place's skill folder with its identity and version in its `metadata`, never over a folder HQ has not looked at, and *Remove* deletes a place's skill folder; a skill added from the Library says what it is based on and, to whoever publishes there, offers *Update…* when the Library pins a newer version, showing the change first and refused if the files changed since they were shown, a roll back to the version added keeping that version as the origin, and an origin is never taken from the files. · fulfils AC-404d
- **SC-1648** — In Your space the person does everything: the page adds the skills that follow them, and a skill found in files, switched on and then changed, goes off and says so. · fulfils AC-404e, AC-405a

## Delivery to a run (added 2026-10-28)

- **SC-1649** — A person's own published skills follow them into their own conversations everywhere, projects included, and into the work their own conversation hands to an agent on their behalf, delivered by fingerprint and only at the version they published (never an unpublished change, whatever the place's default), and not into runs someone else started, a task's session, a scheduled or automated run, a conversation that cannot be found, Your space itself (where they already are), or when the organisation has turned *Personal skills outside Your space* off; a project's own skill of the same name wins and is named. · fulfils AC-405a, AC-405b
- **SC-1650** — A run is given the installed skills that every agent or this agent has at their pinned versions, each version named once and delivered as content by fingerprint, built-ins that are on, and none for a run without skills; not a removed one, one set aside for the organisation's own copy, one not yet published, or a built-in as an installed skill; and only the sites that hold the run are sent them. · fulfils AC-403a
- **SC-1651** — A run is told of the place's skills in files by place, path and fingerprint, only the switched-on ones: none new under *Off by default*, each under *On by default*, not once its files changed under *Off by default*, not under a place's stricter default, not one switched off and nothing under *Never*; with the site, a switched-on skill is staged, one whose file changed is not, and the switch comes off when hq looks at the place again. · fulfils AC-404b
- **SC-1652** — When the same skill comes from two places, the organisation's installed one wins over the project's and the project's over the person's: only the same identity clashes and different skills all apply, the one set aside is named with the reason it lost in words a person reads, two of one level with the same identity are both kept, staging naming them apart, and an installed identity is never taken from a bare name. · fulfils AC-403d
- **SC-1653** — Every staged name is unique within a run as hq works it out: an installed skill is named by its level, a workspace's by its own path, a skill that cannot be staged at all is left out with the reason, and of two that would stage under one name the earlier installed is kept and the later set aside. · fulfils AC-403d

## An agent's skills (added 2026-10-28)

- **SC-1654** — An agent's page lists everything the agent will see in the clash order, grouped by why the agent has it (built in, every agent has these, this agent has these, each place it works in with counts, set aside and why), each skill a row with name and description; an administrator adds and removes skills for this agent only, on its manage page and its own page, and a member sees the same rows read only; someone with no role is refused. · fulfils AC-403a, AC-403b
- **SC-1655** — The agent's page shows what its listing costs per request, estimated from each skill's name and description plus a fixed entry per skill, and warns above about 20 skills or 4,000 tokens, saying to an administrator the way out. · fulfils AC-403c

## The conversation: picking, seeing and proposing (added 2026-10-28)

- **SC-1656** — The `/` picker lists the skills the conversation's agent has by staged name, grouped by where each comes from: its installed skills, the project's switched-on skills in files from the last scan, the person's own published ones, the project's copy of an installed skill set aside, and in the box that starts a conversation the chosen agent's in the project it will start in; a skill whose header sets `user-invocable` to false is left out; each is shown by its title and the first line of its description, bounded; the built-in given only in a repository is offered once the conversation's work has run in a workspace; and it is refused to anyone but the conversation's own person. · fulfils AC-406a
- **SC-1657** — Typing `/` at the very start of the message box opens the picker, closed for a `/` anywhere else, narrowing on name and description as the person types; Enter, Tab or a click chooses and `/name` stays in the text as highlighted text; Escape closes it and leaves the text; changing or deleting that text un-picks it; the pick is sent beside the text as a field and none when none was chosen; and a refused send keeps the pick. · fulfils AC-406a
- **SC-1658** — The work step reads *Used the skill* with the person-facing name (the staged name when hq names nothing), found from the run's record, which keeps each staged skill by its staged name, never counts a refused skill or trusts two entries of one name; the step opens the skill beside the thread, saying where it came from and the version the run used, listing its files and opening another when chosen; a step without one stays text. · fulfils AC-406c
- **SC-1659** — Opening a used skill checks access at view time: an installed skill opens for anyone in the organisation who reads the conversation and for nobody outside it, a place's skill only for whoever sees that place, a person's own skill for that person only and only from their own records; it opens nothing for one who cannot read the conversation or for a place or path hq has no skill at, and says a version is not kept when hq holds none with what the run used or the files changed and were never published; erasing a person takes their skills' kept content, leaving identity and fingerprint on the runs. · fulfils AC-406c
- **SC-1660** — An asset's page says when and by which agent a skill was last used: each skill is stamped once from the runs' records, a place skill by its place and path, the newest use kept and a refused entry never counted. · fulfils AC-406d
- **SC-1661** — `propose_skill` lands by who asked: from the person's own interactive conversation (their own chat, or work their own conversation handed on), where they may edit at that level, it is their own draft, and the same content again answers with the open draft; from any other run (a task, a scheduled or automated run, a conversation someone else opened, a run that is not the capability's own) it is a proposal, never a draft, and so is a call from a person who may not edit the organisation's level, aimed at a level they can change on the card; a person who only reads the project is refused; the person comes from the run's record, never an argument; at most the per-run number of proposals is allowed; a new Library skill whose name the organisation already has is refused; and the lead is told the outcome on its next turn. · fulfils AC-407a
- **SC-1662** — A proposed skill is checked as a workflow is: a name within the Agent Skills rules, a `SKILL.md` that names files all sent, header keys a run ignores flagged, the manifest refused, an aim among the three and a reason, relative links and scripts read, and every refusal ending "Nothing was changed."; and a model on the baseline given the `skill-creator` loads it and proposes rather than writing the skill into files. · fulfils AC-407a
- **SC-1663** — Proposals are reviewed at their level: the organisation's administrators and Library maintainers see the organisation's, a project's administrators their project's (a member and an organisation administrator outside it refused), the person their own and no one else, listed per level and counted together on the Overview and on a project's Skills page, and the Library leads to the queue; review accepts what was shown (refused if it holds other content), edits then accepts (publishing the edited files), or rejects with a note; accepting publishes version 1 into the place, or version 1.0.0 of the organisation's own skill, saying what it will do (an edit to another publisher's skill makes the organisation's copy), a decided proposal shows its outcome with no buttons, the conversation it came from opens beside the page only for someone who may read it, and rejected proposals are removed 90 days after review. · fulfils AC-407b
- **SC-1664** — An agent's request to publish, roll back, switch a skill on or install becomes an approval card for the person it acts for, and runs only when that person approves it and still holds the right (the organisation's setting read as it is now): the same card when asked again while it waits, answered by no one else, nothing when declined, refused on approval when the draft moved after the request or the right is gone, recorded with the person as publisher and *requested by* the agent, never taken from what a client confirms; a run no conversation started or a kind with no request (trust) is refused before any card. · fulfils AC-407c
- **SC-1665** — The approval card for an agent's skill request says who asks and who is recorded as having done it, shows the person it acts for the files a publish would change (and no one else), and says so when the request has gone stale. · fulfils AC-407c
- **SC-1666** — A proposed skill's card in the conversation says what it is and shows every file; a reviewer can accept from it and sees the outcome once reviewed; a skill aimed at the person offers *Add to my skills*; a draft's card offers *Open draft* and *Publish*, no Publish when the draft moved on since the card, and once published or discarded says which (*Published as 1.0.0*) and offers *Add to agents…*. · fulfils AC-407a
- **SC-1667** — An organisation that left models or voice for later is told so as *not set up*, not as an outage: voice for a type with no provider ever added says it isn't set up and that an organisation admin can add one, while a provider that was added and stopped working still says voice is down and who has been told; an admin's Needs attention says no model provider has been added (rather than none works) and lists *Voice isn't set up* while no speech-to-text or text-to-speech provider has been added, each linking to Providers and clearing itself once one is; a member's last look in onboarding says when the organisation has not set up models yet. · fulfils AC-357d · tests `hq/src/voice/__tests__/speechAccess.test.ts`, `hq/src/org/components/__tests__/NeedsAttentionSection.test.tsx`, `hq/src/model-access/__tests__/talkAccess.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/onboarding/pages/__tests__/OnboardingPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/conversations/voice/__tests__/voice.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1668** — Setup and onboarding keep each step's buttons in one row under the form, pinned to the foot of the window while the form is longer than it and clear of a focused field, and setup's panel beside the form centres the product's mark and name beside You, then shows the organisation's sign-in page from the organisation step on. · fulfils AC-271a · tests `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/SetupPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/onboarding/pages/__tests__/OnboardingPage.test.tsx`

## Code work on branches (added 2026-11-02)

Written at the close of the unit *live preview*, from the finished code. In a workspace that is a Git repository (a *code workspace*), every task and conversation works on a branch of the workspace's repository, in a working copy of its own (a clone that borrows the workspace's objects read-only), and only a merge or a restore moves `main`. The site's side — the commit helper, the copies and the runs on them — is in [`site.md`](site.md).

- **SC-1669** — Work is code work when its workspace is a Git repository, decided when a run starts and never by an agent: the first run of a task given a code workspace, or of a conversation's work started in one (start_work naming the workspace, from any conversation), makes a branch, one per conversation and workspace, never for a project's Viewer, and its working copy through the site; a later run of the same work uses the same copy, and a copy that was removed is made fresh in the same folder (in a folder of its own while the last one is still being removed); two runs of the work starting at once make one branch; a workspace without Git is not code work and leaves no row, and nothing is left behind when the site makes nothing. · fulfils AC-410a
- **SC-1670** — HQ names a branch from the work — the task's title or what the person first asked — in lowercase words joined by hyphens without filler words, cut at a word to 50 characters, taking `-2`, `-3` when the repository has the name already. · fulfils AC-410a
- **SC-1671** — The agent's `commit` tool commits in the calling run's own copy, as the agent on behalf of the person (a name and an address Git can write, from HQ's own host name), and refuses a run that is not the capability's; in a run on its live preview's site it commits in that copy when the workspace's site holds none, and refuses a run on no branch. It takes a message and nothing the agent could aim elsewhere, and is in no access tier. · fulfils AC-410b
- **SC-1672** — The agent's `update_from_main` tool merges `main` into the calling run's copy as the agent for the person and says what happened and the one thing to do next; on a clash it names the clashing files (at most twenty, counting the rest) and tells the agent to resolve them and commit. It takes nothing but the run. · fulfils AC-410b
- **SC-1673** — A site's refusal of branch work reaches a person and an agent's tool call in the same words a person can act on, each ending "Nothing was changed."; the repository refusals and an unreachable site keep the words every repository call has. · fulfils AC-410a
- **SC-1674** — A run on a branch is told, in a chat and in a task: its branch, that `main` is read only, how far behind `main` it is, the other branches with whose they are and who works on them (up to a cap, counting the rest), `update_from_main` for bringing `main` in and `commit` to finish a merge that clashed, to raise the merge confirmation at once when the person asks and never unasked, that the person confirms the merge, and that it has the app tools and how they reach the live preview, whatever it said before. · fulfils AC-410d *(The live-preview part is behind the `livePreview` flag, at experimental, so not in the release.)*
- **SC-1675** — The lead is told each workspace of the place with its id, the code workspaces marked, and nothing when the places have none; `create_task` asks for a workspace on every call, a code workspace or none, and a call without it reads as none. · fulfils AC-410e
- **SC-1676** — A Member or Admin deletes an unmerged branch whose work is over, told how many commits are lost, and its copy goes; deleting is refused while a run is starting on the branch, which stays open, and the branch opens again when the site deletes nothing; a rename takes only a name HQ would give a branch. · fulfils AC-410f
- **SC-1677** — Removing a workspace that has branches confirms first, naming each branch, whose it is, how far ahead it is and any agent working on it that stops, counting past five; with no branches the confirmation is as before. · fulfils AC-410f
- **SC-1678** — The branch select reads `main` and the open branches of the workspace, each with whose it is, for a Viewer too. · fulfils AC-412b, AC-415a

## Merging into main (added 2026-11-02)

- **SC-1679** — *Merge into main* takes the branch's changes since it left `main`, or since its last merge, in as one version, and closes the branch (its copy removed) when no agent works on it; while an agent works it is a checkpoint: the branch stays open and the next merge counts from the commit merged; a clash merges nothing and asks the task to run again as the person; a dialog made against an older `main` says `main` moved and changes nothing; a Viewer is refused with 403 before the site is asked. · fulfils AC-413a, AC-413b, AC-413c, AC-415a
- **SC-1680** — After a clash, the branch's agent is asked at once, on behalf of the person who pressed *Merge*, naming the files, the version of `main` that changed each (counting past twenty) and what to do; a task waiting for an approval runs again at once with the request replaced, a task with no run pending runs, a run already queued or going reads the ask at its next start, and a task held because its person lost access is left as *Run* leaves it. · fulfils AC-413d
- **SC-1681** — The agent's `merge_into_main` never merges on the agent's call: it puts one card in the branch's thread (*Kernel will merge … into main as one version* · *Merge* · *Not now*); the card is answered only by a Member's or Admin's own press, never by an approval the agent passes on, and *Merge* merges as the person who pressed onto the `main` the card was made against; a second press does nothing, a Viewer's or a stranger's press merges nothing, a card made against an older `main` merges nothing and offers the dialog, nothing is merged once the branch is closed, a clash says the agent was asked to bring the branch up to date, and *Not now* merges nothing. The tool takes only an optional summary and tells the agent to ask at once when the person asked, directly or through a brief, and never otherwise. · fulfils AC-413e
- **SC-1682** — With the workspace connected to GitHub, versions pushed there that `main` lacks arrive as new versions before a merge or a restore, and `main` is pushed after one, with a token minted for that one call; pushing a workspace not connected to GitHub is refused, and a token is never echoed. · fulfils AC-413f

## The workspace view (added 2026-11-02)

- **SC-1683** — Opening a workspace fills the page beside the app's rail, without the project's menu: *‹ project* goes back, the workspace switcher lists the project's workspaces most recently opened first, and *Live preview* is first in the panel with its state. · fulfils AC-412a *(The live-preview part is behind the `livePreview` flag, at experimental, so not in the release.)*
- **SC-1684** — The branch select lists `main` (protected) first, then each branch on one line: whose it is (*You* for the signed-in person), what works on it (the agent named while idle), how many commits ahead and behind, and whether it has a live preview; choosing a branch sets the address, its Git and its conversation. · fulfils AC-412b *(The live-preview part is behind the `livePreview` flag, at experimental, so not in the release.)*
- **SC-1685** — The conversation beside the view follows the branch, whatever conversation the address's `c=` names: a task's branch shows the task's thread, and a task with no thread yet offers the box whose first message starts it, saying why to a person who may not talk there; `main` offers *New conversation*, which starts one in this workspace and shows it. · fulfils AC-412d
- **SC-1686** — Files lists the branch's tree with each file marked added, changed or deleted, a deleted file kept where it was, and opens a file read-only in the file viewer's frame: an unchanged file is read at the commit in view through the relay, as any file is, with the same previews and Download (**SC-1882**), and a changed file opens whole with its changes in place; nothing in the view edits a file. *(Changed 2026-11-10: a file read from Git was a link of the page's own, shown as plain text.)* · fulfils AC-412c
- **SC-1687** — Git for a branch lists *Changes (N)*, and a change opens its diff in the middle, base against tip; a workspace event reads the chosen branch's changes again, and with `main` chosen asks for none. · fulfils AC-412c
- **SC-1688** — The view's address carries the branch, the section and the file (`main` left out), shows a file before a change when both are named, reads back what it wrote and ignores a section it does not know; the browser remembers the place per person and per workspace, checked as the address is. · fulfils AC-412e
- **SC-1689** — The view folds at about 1280 and 900 pixels and a phone's width: on a laptop the panel folds to its strip and Files or Git opens over the middle (Escape closes it); on a tablet the conversation is a drawer opened from the top bar, and opening the drawer closes the panel and the other way round; on a phone the conversation is the main view, with *Conversation*, *Live preview*, *Files* and *Git* in a switch at the bottom and the branch select as the title. · fulfils AC-412f *(The live-preview part is behind the `livePreview` flag, at experimental, so not in the release.)*
- **SC-1690** — The *Merge into main* dialog shows the changed files, a summary started from the work (editable), the version it goes in as and that the branch's commits become that one version; while an agent works it says the merge takes what is committed so far and the agent keeps working; a clean merge goes in against the `main` it showed and says so; a clash names the files grouped by the version of `main` that changed them, newest first, says the agent will bring the branch up to date, and ends "Nothing was changed." · fulfils AC-413a, AC-413b, AC-413c
- **SC-1691** — With `main` chosen, Git lists its versions newest first — number, summary (wrapped, never cut to a few words), who merged it, how many files, when — with *Restore* for a Member or Admin, which says what it did, and a quiet *Deploy…* that lists the place's approved workflows that are on and runs the chosen one on this workspace, lists them without running for a person who may not start runs, and says how to add one when there are none. · fulfils AC-414a, AC-414b, AC-414c
- **SC-1692** — A Viewer sees everything in the view — files, Git, changes, versions, live previews — and acts on nothing: *Merge into main…* and *New conversation* are greyed with the reason, `main` offers no *Restore*, and the server refuses a Viewer's merge, restore, branch delete and conversation start. · fulfils AC-415a *(The live-preview part is behind the `livePreview` flag, at experimental, so not in the release.)*
- **SC-1693** — A workspace without Git shows Files only, with *Turn Git on* where Live preview and Git would be; its confirmation says what happens, and turning Git on records the files as the first version by the person who pressed it, with a standard `.gitignore` when there is none and files over 50 MB left out and named. · fulfils AC-411a, AC-411b *(The live-preview part is behind the `livePreview` flag, at experimental, so not in the release.)*

## Live preview (added 2026-11-02)

> **Behind the `livePreview` flag, at experimental, so not in the release:** SC-1694 to SC-1709, SC-1712 to SC-1715, SC-1718 and SC-1719. The product does not keep these promises while the flag is off. The QA model has no mark for a promise outside the release, so this note stands in for one. Not behind it, and kept with the flag off: SC-1710 (the package card outside a live preview), SC-1711 (trusted package scopes), SC-1716 (the old preview path is gone) and SC-1717 (the disk notice).

A live preview runs the app from one branch's copy, or from `main`'s own copy, in a container of its own on one of the organisation's sites. The site's side is in [`site.md`](site.md), the relay's in [`relay.md`](relay.md), and the Fleet Console's settings in [`console.md`](console.md) and [`console-web.md`](console-web.md).

- **SC-1694** — A Member or Admin opens a live preview on a branch, binding the branch's own copy, on the workspace's site; a Viewer is refused and nothing starts; a second person gets the same live preview, and two opens at once make one; a full site is refused, naming its limit; a site that goes silent mid-start leaves nothing running; a start the site answered as stopped fails in HQ's words and keeps why for the start panel; a stop that lands while the site is still opening it stops it on the site too. · fulfils AC-416a, AC-420b
- **SC-1695** — A done task's branch with no copy gets one when its live preview starts, made as a run makes one, and keeps it while the live preview runs; once that live preview stops the copy goes, while a task not done keeps its copy; a done task's run ending leaves the copy a live preview runs from; a copy its site no longer holds lets HQ's row of it go, so starting again makes a fresh one. · fulfils AC-416a
- **SC-1696** — Before one runs, *Live preview* says where HQ would start it and how full that site is, and starts it there or on another allowed site with room the person picks; it turns Git on first when the workspace has none; it says why the branch's last live preview stopped, though this screen did not see it stop, and after starting follows HQ (one that stopped meanwhile shows why, never *Starting*); on a full site it lists the open ones, whose and which branch, with *Stop* on those the person may stop; it shows why none can start and a refusal as HQ worded it; a Viewer sees it but cannot start one. · fulfils AC-416a, AC-420a, AC-420b, AC-415a
- **SC-1697** — A running live preview narrows the panel to its strip and keeps its own bar at the top — its state, *Reload*, the app's address, *New tab*, *Stop*, and the site with how full it is; the app is entered in the frame (or a new tab) by a form post of HQ's ticket to an http or https address, never a link, entered once per reload even while a refusal renders the view again, its cookie renewed a little before it runs out; on a tablet the conversation's drawer opens below the bar, and on a phone *Live preview* is in the switch with the bar and the app and its footer says while the agent works that the app reloads when it saves. · fulfils AC-416b, AC-416d
- **SC-1698** — The app reloads for everyone when the agent asks, and with *Auto* on when the branch gets commits; *Stop*, *Auto* and *Reload* ask HQ; a Viewer sees the app but cannot reload it or switch *Auto*; a live preview is stopped by the person who started it or a project Admin, and nobody else. · fulfils AC-416c, AC-415a
- **SC-1699** — The view tells HQ a live preview is in use once when it is shown and again each minute, and nothing while it is not shown; HQ counts it once per person and live preview in 30 seconds, reading nothing for the repeats, and refuses again a person it refused; a live preview unused for its site's idle time is stopped. · fulfils AC-420c
- **SC-1700** — The live preview's reads come from HQ's own rows: a branch's reads ask the site nothing however many come at once, `main`'s ask the site nothing once HQ holds a branch of the workspace and otherwise share one question, and whether Git is on is asked once for many reads, a yes kept and a no or a failure asked again.
- **SC-1701** — The agent's app tools — `start_app`, `stop_app`, `list_apps`, `reload_view` and `set_auto_reload` — are offered to every code-work run and act only on its own branch's live preview on the run's site, found at each call: the one running now, even when the run's capability was signed before it started or names one that has since stopped; with none open on the branch the call is refused telling the agent to have the person open it, with one on another site it says so, and a run that is not the calling one is refused before any live preview is read. They are core's declarations, in no access tier with no *Allow*, and take nothing the agent could aim at another workspace or branch. · fulfils AC-417a
- **SC-1702** — `start_app` on a branch with no live preview open opens one first, as the run's person, on the branch the run works on and the run's site, then starts the app in it: a run for no person or on no branch is refused, a Viewer's run is refused, a full site or live previews off on the run's site is refused in HQ's words saying nothing was started, and nothing opens on the run's site while the branch has one on another site; it is the only app tool that opens one. · fulfils AC-417a
- **SC-1703** — The strip offers *Start* for each app known on the workspace, as it was last started: `main` offers the apps last started on any branch, each once with its latest command, a branch prefers its own record, and an app already going is left out; *Start* runs it in this live preview as the person, with no run, and shows it once started or why it did not; a Viewer is offered no *Start* and is refused, a paused live preview or an app nobody started on the workspace is refused, and the site's refusal is said in HQ's words; on `main`'s live preview with no app, the strip says how an app comes to start there. · fulfils AC-417b, AC-417e
- **SC-1704** — When a site is heard again, HQ marks an app the site no longer runs as stopped, saying where, takes the state the site gives for each app it runs and changes nothing that already agrees, and changes nothing and tells no screen when the site refuses. · fulfils AC-420e
- **SC-1705** — Opening a live app, HQ asks the site for its own ticket, signs an app ticket with it for one person, one app and the site's relay, to be entered within a minute, and answers the form for the app's address; it renews the cookie a renewal names and refuses one that names none; it refuses before asking the site anything that is not an app, not running, with no address or no relay, and signs nothing when the site refuses or gives no ticket. · fulfils AC-416d
- **SC-1706** — HQ's word on an app ticket stands only for a ticket it signed, for that person, app and live preview, while it runs and is open to them; it stops standing for a person cut off (every relay told) and for a live preview, an app or a ticket revoked; HQ answers each relay's ask, and no for an ask it cannot read, pushes a revocation to every relay connected, and from a relay's reports cuts off an app another page hammers, not one whose page is merely stale. · fulfils AC-416d, AC-421c
- **SC-1707** — An app's address is built from the preview pattern: `p-<id>.<preview domain>` on a server with a domain, `p-<id>.preview.localhost:<HQ's port>` for HQ at `http://localhost`, and none on a bare network address; the id is 26 lowercase base32 characters (130 random bits) given when the app first starts on that branch, never a name, and is read back only from a host the pattern makes. · fulfils AC-416d, AC-423b
- **SC-1708** — The lead's `offer_live_preview` leaves a card for a task's branch, refuses a workspace without Git and a task with no branch, and is not offered without a code workspace or without an address for live apps; the card starts the live preview and opens the workspace view on that branch's Live preview, or shows HQ's refusal and stays. · fulfils AC-417c
- **SC-1709** — The strip asks for a yes to a waiting service by its name and image, and to a blocked install script, worded by HQ; a blocked package is asked about once and everything is forgotten when the live preview stops; a package named with characters a person should not be shown is refused. · fulfils AC-418a, AC-418b
- **SC-1710** — A package blocked in a branch's copy with no live preview open puts one card in the task's thread for a Member or Admin, its question worded by HQ and never with the site's sentence (plainly when the verdict cannot be read); HQ drops a report from another site, for a copy that is not that branch's, from a run not going on it, or naming anything but one npm version; *Allow* is answered only by a person's own press and tells the workspace's site that one version, as the person who pressed; a second press does nothing, a Viewer's or a stranger's press is refused, *Not now* keeps the script off and asks the site nothing, a closed branch is refused, and a site with nothing waiting is said in HQ's words. · fulfils AC-418b
- **SC-1711** — An organisation admin sets the trusted package scopes on Organisation › Settings: shown one per line, saved from what was typed split on lines, commas and spaces, each scope kept once and trimmed, and anything that is not a short list of npm scopes refused saying what to fix, HQ's refusal shown as it came; a run's capability carries them. · fulfils AC-418c
- **SC-1712** — *Start live preview* and *Reload live preview* workflows go through the workflows' run path to the site the live preview runs on, aimed at it; one is refused with nothing run for a live preview that is not running, or a workflow not approved or switched off; the screen offers only those the place approved and switched on, and shows HQ's refusal. · fulfils AC-419a
- **SC-1713** — HQ picks the workspace's own site when it allows live previews and has room, else the allowed site with the most room, and none when no site has room; a limit or idle time that does not read is the default; every refusal ends with what was changed, a stop keeps HQ's own sentence for why it stopped, never the site's, and a time that does not read keeps HQ's clock. · fulfils AC-420a, AC-420d
- **SC-1714** — A fleet admin turning live previews off on a site stops every live preview there; removing a workspace tells every other site that ran one of its live previews, not its own; a paused live preview says why and, after a guard pause, offers *Resume*. · fulfils AC-420d, AC-422a
- **SC-1715** — A live preview on another site gets the branch as a Git bundle carried by the relay: HQ signs a bundle ticket each end accepts as itself, with a fresh id and the bundle's hash, never from a site to itself; it orders the relay with the ticket between the two sites, says so when it holds no link to the relay, reads only a report it can, and the carrying ends when the destination says it landed and fails at once when the relay reports it could not carry it, or when nothing lands in time. · fulfils AC-420b
- **SC-1716** — The old preview path is gone: no `start_preview` tool is offered to a run or a connection, and live apps are started with `start_app` in a live preview. · fulfils AC-417a
- **SC-1717** — Fleet admins are told when a machine's disk is 80% used, naming what branch copies take, until it is below 75% so the notice does not flap; it is not raised below 80%, for a site that is away, or with no disk figure. · fulfils AC-422d
- **SC-1718** — When `main` moves, `main`'s live preview's copy is brought up to date; before a branch whose live preview runs on another site is merged, its commits still to come back are brought back first. · fulfils AC-417e, AC-420b
- **SC-1719** — On an installation reached by a bare network address, branches and merging work and *Live preview* says a domain is needed. · fulfils AC-423b

## Feature flags, the Features screen and the live preview behind its flag (added 2026-11-03)

Written at the close of the unit *live preview*, from the finished code. A feature that is not ready is behind a flag with a stage ([ADR-0172](../../adr/adr-0172-a-flag-has-a-stage.md)); core's rule is **SC-1761**, the switches' wire shape **SC-1762**. hq stores the organisation's switches on its record, asks the rule at every entry point of a flagged feature, and sends the switches to its sites with each instruction; the site's side is in [`site.md`](site.md) and the Fleet Console's in [`console.md`](console.md) and [`console-web.md`](console-web.md). The live preview is the first flag in the list, at experimental, so these scenarios keep a release that has it off.

- **SC-1765** — *Features*, in the organisation's settings, is for organisation admins (a member who opens it is told who to ask, **SC-1188**), and in production it lists only the flags in beta and those released in this minor release, each with its title, a *Beta* or *New in this release* label, one line saying what it is, a switch and who last switched it and when (or *On/Off by default*), with *Beta* explained above the list; with none listed it says there is nothing to try; a refusal of a switch is shown as it came. · fulfils AC-424a, AC-424c, AC-424d, AC-424e · tests `hq/src/features/pages/__tests__/FeaturesPage.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/features.spec.ts`
- **SC-1766** — In a development build *Features* lists every flag grouped in stage order (experimental, preview, beta, released), explains all four stages above the list and lets a developer switch any of them, except a flag this build offers no switch for. · fulfils AC-425a · tests `hq/src/features/pages/__tests__/FeaturesPage.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/features.spec.ts`
- **SC-1767** — `setFeature` is for an organisation admin and refuses a member, a name that is not a listed flag, a request that does not say on or off, a flag this build does not let be switched (in production anything but a beta or a flag released in this minor release, saying what stage it is at) and an hq with no organisation yet, each saying who holds the fix and ending "Nothing was changed."; an admin's switch is stored on the organisation's record with who switched it and when, applies to the whole organisation at once, and two admins switching at once both keep their switch. `getFeatures` answers anyone signed in with each flag's stage and whether it is on, and tells only an admin who switched each. · fulfils AC-424b, AC-424d, AC-424e, AC-425b · tests `hq/src/features/__tests__/features.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/features/pages/__tests__/FeaturesPage.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/features.spec.ts`
- **SC-1768** — hq's server checks a flag with `requireFeature` first in each entry point of a flagged feature, and while it is off the entry point answers 404, *not available in this organisation*, ending "Nothing was changed.", and does nothing. The organisation's switches are read from its record at most once every few seconds, a read under way is shared by everyone who asks, a switch forgets the copy at once, a read that fails is not kept and its error reaches the caller (a check never decides from switches it could not read), and the stored column is read defensively, ignoring a flag no longer listed, a value of another shape and inherited properties. The Marketplace and usage billing are decided by the same switches. · fulfils AC-424b, AC-426a, AC-426b · tests `hq/src/features/__tests__/switches.test.ts`
- **SC-1769** — When hq signs a run's capability it asks the rule once with the organisation's switches and adds the tools of every flagged feature that is off to the run's never-allowed list, whatever the agent's own permissions say; a flag at preview cannot be turned on in production by a switch; and a feature's instructions are written only while it is on. · fulfils AC-426c · tests `hq/src/features/__tests__/runFeatures.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/capabilityClaims.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1770** — hq sends the organisation's switches with every request to a site: at once when its copy is fresh, reading them first when not, nothing to a site speaking a protocol before 1.12, and failing the request as offline when the site went away while the switches were read. · fulfils AC-426d · tests `hq/src/sites/channel/__tests__/siteChannelRegistry.test.ts`
- **SC-1771** — While the live preview's flag is off, every operation the workspace view calls for it answers 404, *not available*, and reads nothing; the Fleet Console's live-preview methods are refused as not found and read nothing; hq's background work for live previews does nothing (its site events are registered and each dropped unread, a run starting or ending keeps no live preview in use, a site's connect sends no setting and reconciles nothing, the idle stop and bringing commits back do nothing, no branch is carried between sites); and the relays' app doors hq answers refuse: no app ticket stands, even one hq signed, and no live app's name is given a certificate. · fulfils AC-426b, AC-426e · tests `hq/src/livePreviews/__tests__/flagOff.test.ts`
- **SC-1772** — While the live preview's flag is off agents are given nothing of it: a run is given none of the app tools (`start_app`, `stop_app`, `list_apps`, `reload_view`, `set_auto_reload`) or the lead's `offer_live_preview`, and its capability claims no live preview; a code-work run's prompt says nothing of live previews or app tools and keeps its rules for branches, `main`, commits and merging; the lead's instructions and `run_task` say nothing of live previews and keep the rules for code work; the lead's offer leaves no card; no run is sent into a live preview's container, its secrets with it. With the flag on all of these are as described under *Live preview*. · fulfils AC-426c, AC-426e · tests `hq/src/livePreviews/__tests__/flagOff.test.ts`, `hq/src/livePreviews/__tests__/offer.test.ts`, `hq/src/livePreviews/__tests__/runPrompt.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/lead/__tests__/livePreviewFlag.test.ts`, `hq/src/features/__tests__/runFeatures.test.ts`, `core/src/codeWork/__tests__/codeWorkPrompt.test.ts`
- **SC-1773** — While the live preview's flag is off the workspace view draws none of it: no *Live preview* in the panel, no icon or dot on the strip and none in a phone's switch, an address that names *Live preview* opens Git, the branch select says nothing of a branch's live preview, a workspace without Git is told only that Git needs a repository, and the lead's offer of one is not drawn; branches, Files, Git, merging, versions and *Restore* are as without it. · fulfils AC-426b, AC-426e · tests `hq/src/workspaceView/__tests__/workspaceView.test.tsx`, `hq/src/workspaceView/__tests__/workspaceViewWalk.test.tsx`, `hq/src/workspaceView/__tests__/livePreviewOffer.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/workspaceLivePreview.spec.ts`
- **SC-1774** — Every test runs as shipped, with each flag at its production value: a feature's own tests switch its flag on for their group with `withFeature`, and the tests that a feature is hidden while off sit outside it, or inside `withFeatureOff` in the suites the full regression also runs with every flag on. In hq's integration suite the helper switches the flag on in memory and on every organisation record as the screen does, in hq's end-to-end suite it switches it through the *Features* screen's own operation as the e2e administrator and restores the value it found, and in the cross-app suite it does the same through hq while the site follows hq; the seed writes production's values for every flag unless the run asks for every flag on, and a spec that uses the helper runs its group in one worker, in order. · fulfils AC-427a, AC-427b, AC-427d · tests `hq/src/features/__tests__/features.integration.test.ts`, `tests/cross-app/13-feature-flags.test.ts`
- **SC-1780** — Across hq, its site and the Fleet Console, with the live preview's flag as shipped (off) the Fleet Console is not told it is on and a fleet admin's live-preview methods are refused with nothing changed; with the organisation's switch on hq says it is on, answers the methods through to the site and sends the site its setting with the switches, which a development site takes. · fulfils AC-426d, AC-426e, AC-429a · tests `tests/cross-app/14-live-preview-flag.test.ts`

## Tasks, runs, boards and routines (added 2026-11-08)

Written at the close of the unit *tasks, runs, boards and routines* (adr-0174 to adr-0177), from the finished code. A task has a type and a column; its column sits in one task state (Backlog, To do, In progress, Done); what an agent is doing with it is a separate execution state set by the system; a board is a saved view; work starts only by an explicit act; a routine is a repeatable run on a cron schedule. A test named under a scenario asserts it today without carrying its `@SC` tag yet.

### Task types, columns and states

- **SC-1781** — Every project has the task types Task and Epic, each with four columns, one per task state (Backlog, To do, In progress, Done); neither can be deleted or archived. A project made without them, or missing one, gets it when first asked, the same ones every time, and two callers at once make one of each. · fulfils AC-430a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskTypes.integration.test.ts`, `core/src/tasks/__tests__/taskModel.test.ts`
- **SC-1782** — A person who works in the project adds a task type (it starts with one column per state and goes last in the list; a name taken, in any case, is refused), renames and reorders types, archives a type only while no open task uses it and never Task or Epic, and deletes only a type no task has ever used; a Viewer is greyed out with why. · fulfils AC-433a, AC-433c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskTypeServices.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskTypeUse.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/pages/settings/types/__tests__/TaskTypesTab.test.tsx`
