- **SC-1783** — A type's columns each sit in one task state: a column is added after its state's others (never as the default), renamed, recoloured and reordered within the states' order, made its state's default (a task set to that state lands there), moved to another state with its tasks (the refusal or the result names which tasks changed), and removed only while empty; a state's last column cannot be moved or removed, and a set of columns that leaves a state without one, gives it two defaults or repeats a name in any case is refused. · fulfils AC-433a, AC-433b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskTypeServices.integration.test.ts`, `core/src/tasks/__tests__/taskModel.test.ts`
- **SC-1784** — A new task starts in Backlog, on its type's Backlog column and last in the project's order; a task added from a board column starts in that column, still at the bottom of the order; two made at once get two different ranks, and a column of another project is refused. · fulfils AC-430c, AC-040c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlace.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1785** — A task's task state is the state of its column: moving it to a column writes that state with it, setting the state lands it on the type's default column for that state, and changing its type keeps its state on the new type's default column for it; a column of another type is refused, two moves at once leave the column and the state agreeing, and a task moved to Done leaves its agent's line. · fulfils AC-430b, AC-430d, AC-431b, AC-041a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlace.integration.test.ts`, `core/src/tasks/__tests__/taskModel.test.ts`
- **SC-1786** — Creating a task, editing it and moving it between columns or states start no work and change no execution state, whatever the column is called; a run ending leaves the task in its column and state, and a person (or the agent with its tool) moves it. · fulfils AC-430e, AC-438a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskQueueing.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlace.integration.test.ts`, `core/src/tasks/__tests__/executionState.test.ts`
- **SC-1787** — The project has one order: a task dropped after another goes right after it and before another right before it, two dropped into the same gap at once both land in the gap, a neighbour of another project or the task itself is refused, and the order is never shown as a number. Every board and the Tasks list sort by it by default, and it is the order an agent's Start follows. · fulfils AC-431a, AC-431c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlace.integration.test.ts`, `core/src/tasks/__tests__/rank.test.ts`
- **SC-1788** — An epic is a built-in type that is never run: Run refuses it saying where it is planned, an agent's Start and Start all agents never put it in line, a Run at… time is refused on it and one left on a task made an epic does not start it, and it is never counted as needing a person. It may have an agent that manages it, a task with an agent can become one (leaving its agent's line, and refused while running), and an epic belongs to no epic. · fulfils AC-432a, AC-432b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskContract.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/epicRules.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/scheduledStart.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/scheduleOps.integration.test.ts`

### Custom fields

- **SC-1789** — A person who works in the project makes a single-select custom field with its options in order (last in the list; the name unique in any case), renames and reorders fields, archives a field (hidden, its values kept, setting one refused until it is back) and deletes one (its values and its boards go, no task does); options are added, renamed, recoloured, reordered and removed, and a task whose option is removed has no value. · fulfils AC-434a, AC-434b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/customFields.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/pages/settings/fields/__tests__/CustomFieldsTab.test.tsx`
- **SC-1790** — A task has at most one option per field, changed or cleared on its page, on a card or by dropping it on a field board; setting one never touches its task state, is recorded in its activity, and refuses another project's field or an option of another field. · fulfils AC-434a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/customFields.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardDrops.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/components/__tests__/taskSprintAndFields.test.tsx`

### Boards as saved views

- **SC-1791** — A board shows the project's tasks in columns and owns none: a type's board has that type's columns and only its tasks, the Task state board has the four states across every type (and says it mixes types), the Execution board has the six execution states, read-only and without epics, and a field's board has No value first and then its options; each column has a count, cards are in rank order, and a card needs the person when its run asks or a failed run belongs to open work, never an epic. A type or field of another project, and a type board with no type, are refused. · fulfils AC-435a, AC-435e · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardSavedViews.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardViewRules.test.ts`, `core/src/tasks/__tests__/taskModel.test.ts`
- **SC-1792** — A board's filter only hides cards: to the current sprint (which follows the project's current one and holds nothing when there is none), to one sprint, to no sprint, to an epic, an agent or a type, all of them at once, and with nothing in it every task shows. · fulfils AC-435b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardViewRules.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardSavedViews.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1793** — Dropping a card sets what the board sets and the rank: on a type's board its column (so its state), on the Task state board its state (landing on the type's default column; a reorder within the state keeps its column), on a field's board that value only (No value clears it) and never its state; the Execution board is never dragged; on a filtered board a card goes right after the visible card above it and a drop next to a hidden card, or into a column the board does not draw, is refused. · fulfils AC-435c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardDrops.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardViewRules.test.ts`
- **SC-1794** — A person who works in the project saves a view as a board with a name and a filter, reads it back, renames it, changes its filter, copies it and deletes it; deleting a board deletes only the view, never a task. The Boards list puts the two built-in boards first and then the saved ones, each with its spread across its columns and how many cards need the person; Boards in the navigation lists the boards of the projects the person is in. A Viewer reads and saves nothing. · fulfils AC-435d, AC-435e · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardSavedViews.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardsAcrossProjects.integration.test.ts`

### Sprints

- **SC-1795** — A project has one series of sprints, started once from Sprints with a length of 1 to 366 days and a first day; the next two can be planned ahead, each starting the day after the last one's last day; a new length re-lays the upcoming sprints and the current one keeps its dates; a rename keeps the number ("Sprint 12 · Pricing push") and a goal can be set. · fulfils AC-436a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectSprints.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/sprintSeries.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/sprints/__tests__/SprintsTool.test.tsx`
- **SC-1796** — Planning a task into a sprint puts it in the project's order and moves a Backlog task to To do, on its type's default To do column; taking it out keeps its state and column; an epic, another project's task and a finished sprint are refused; what can be planned is the Backlog and To do tasks in no sprint, in rank order, leaving out epics, done work, other sprints and archived tasks; a task page offers None, the current sprint and those planned ahead. · fulfils AC-436b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectSprints.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/sprintPlanning.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/sprints/__tests__/planRules.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/components/__tests__/taskSprintAndFields.test.tsx`, `core/src/tasks/__tests__/taskModel.test.ts`
- **SC-1797** — A sprint finishes only when a person (or an agent with `finish_sprint`) finishes it, never by its date: it asks where the unfinished tasks go, the next sprint or none, and refuses without an answer; the done tasks stay recorded in it, an unfinished task sent on keeps its state and column, the next sprint becomes current, two people finishing at once finish it once, only the current sprint can finish, and a sprint past its last day stays current and is shown as overdue. A Viewer can finish and rename nothing. · fulfils AC-436c, AC-436d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectSprints.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/sprintSeries.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/sprints/__tests__/SprintsTool.test.tsx`

### Execution state and what needs the person

- **SC-1798** — A task's execution state is Not started, Waiting, Running, Needs you, Failed or Finished, set only by the system from its latest run and never from, or onto, its task state: a pending ask is Needs you, a run going is Running, a task in its agent's line or due at its Run at… time is Waiting, then the latest run's outcome (Failed, Finished) and otherwise Not started; a person's Stop leaves it Not started. · fulfils AC-437a, AC-175a · tests `core/src/tasks/__tests__/executionState.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/executionState.test.ts`
- **SC-1799** — Needs you is one rule everywhere: a task asking for an okay, an answer, a decision past its time or turn limit or someone to stand in for a person who lost access; a failed run of an open task (not one already Done, nor one taken back into line); and a routine run asking or failed. A reply in a conversation asks in its conversation and is not counted, and an epic never is. · fulfils AC-437b · tests `core/src/tasks/__tests__/executionState.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/needsYou.test.ts`
- **SC-1800** — The agent's avatar shows the run wherever a task has an assigned agent: Waiting a thin dotted ring, Running a turning ring, Needs you a full ring with a dot, Failed a full ring with a !, Not started and Finished none; when several apply a turning ring comes before Needs you, before Failed, before Waiting. · fulfils AC-437c · tests `hq/src/projects/workroom/__tests__/workroomRules.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/workroom/__tests__/WorkroomTool.test.tsx`

### How work starts

- **SC-1803** — Run at… gives a task a start time on the minute (a time with seconds rounds up) and the task is Waiting until then; at that time it starts once, as if its person had pressed Run, and nothing earlier starts it; setting one is for the person the task is done for or a project admin, an agent sets one only in a run a person started and only where the project lets agents set start times, and an epic and a time in the past are refused; clearing it takes the task out of Waiting. · fulfils AC-438c, AC-191a, AC-191b, AC-191c · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/taskSchedule.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/scheduledStart.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/taskScheduleOperations.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/scheduleOps.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1804** — An agent's Start lists the agent's To do tasks that were never run, in rank order, leaving out epics and tasks with a Run at… time, and the person confirms with ticks (1 to 500 of them); it puts the ticked tasks in the agent's line on the person's behalf at depth 0 and starts the first, and one run goes at a time, the next in rank order when the one before ends. - **SC-1805** — Start all agents starts each agent on its own ticked tasks side by side, one run each, lists a task with no agent among what cannot run, and never starts another project's task given with the ticks. · fulfils AC-439b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/agentStart.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1806** — A failed run does not hold its agent's line: the failure waits under Needs you and the agent takes its next task. · fulfils AC-439c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/agentStart.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1807** — An agent's Stop takes every task waiting in its line out of it, back to Not started (Run at… times included), stops the task it is running after settling the line and records the stop as the person's; a run ending while Stop runs starts nothing after it, and a Stop landing while a dispatch is on its way cancels the run that dispatch starts. · fulfils AC-439d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/agentStop.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1808** — Stop every run in a project, or in the person's own space, stops its running task runs and the caller's own conversations there, takes waiting tasks out of line back to Not started and clears every Run at… still to come, so nothing more starts until someone runs it; another person's conversation keeps running, a running task whose site cannot be reached is put back rather than shown as stopped, and only a project admin whose role may start work can do it (a Viewer never, even as the organisation's admin); in the personal space a person stops their own and nobody's else. · fulfils AC-439e, AC-176c, AC-201d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/placeRunStop.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1809** — A project's Viewer reads what a member reads and starts, stops or changes no work by any route, directly or indirectly (Run, Start, Start all agents, Stop, a start time, a routine, a conversation message, approving a tool), and is told the act needs the Member or Admin role in the project and to ask one of its admins; the controls are greyed with why, and no organisation role changes it. · fulfils AC-169a, AC-169b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectViewerWork.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/placeRunStop.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineActions.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/workroom/__tests__/WorkroomTool.test.tsx`
- **SC-1810** — An agent's `run_task` puts a task in its own agent's line as the agent's, at the run's depth and recording who started it, only in a run a person started and only for a task in the run's own project; it refuses an epic, a task whose Run at… time is still to come, an archived task, a task with no agent (saying what to do), the run's own task and a task something else claimed first, says when the task goes after its agent's running task, and cannot be used by a run an agent, a schedule or a routine started, nor by the run it started itself, so one press of Run never becomes an unbounded chain. · fulfils AC-198a, AC-444b · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/automationRunTask.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runTaskOwnTask.test.ts`

### Routines

- **SC-1816** — A routine is a schedule (a cron rule and its timezone), an agent, instructions and an optional model, in a project or a person's own space, on or off; making one stores the rule, switches it on, works out its first time in its timezone and makes it for its maker, and a firing starts one run of the agent with the instructions and no task. - **SC-1819** — Anyone who may work in the project (not a Viewer) makes or changes a routine, and it then runs on behalf of the last person who changed it; switching it on or off is a change, saving asks first when it would move whose behalf it runs on (and changes nothing when cancelled), and a Viewer and someone outside the project change nothing, Run now and delete included. · fulfils AC-442a, AC-442b · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineActions.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routines.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/routines/__tests__/RoutinesTool.test.tsx`
- **SC-1820** — Run now starts one run of the routine's agent on behalf of the person who pressed it, listed as started by them; it does not change whose routine it is or when it next fires, it is refused while the routine is off or while its last run is going, and two presses, or a press and a firing at the same moment, start one run between them. · fulfils AC-441c, AC-442a · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineFiring.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineActions.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1821** — hq's one scheduler ticks each minute and fires the routines whose stored next time has come: it claims each once (two ticks, or two hq processes, fire it once), starts one run of its agent on behalf of its person, numbered, and moves its time on to the next; times missed while hq was down fire once, not once per time; a routine that is off or not yet due does not fire; a claim read before a schedule change does not fire on the old time. · fulfils AC-441d · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineFiring.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/scheduler.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/dueTime.test.ts`
- **SC-1822** — A firing that comes while the routine's last run is still going is skipped and listed as skipped, and so is one that comes while a run is still being dispatched; a run whose end was never heard of stops counting as going after its time limit plus a margin, so a routine does not stay skipped for ever. · fulfils AC-442c · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineFiring.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1823** — A firing that cannot start is listed as failed with why and the routine keeps its schedule: no agent, or an agent taken off the project, or a site that refuses; a routine whose person can no longer work in its project is switched off with that reason and starts nothing, and someone who works there switches it back on for themselves, which makes it theirs. · fulfils AC-442d · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineFiring.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routines.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/lossOfAccess.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1824** — A routine's run is a conversation of its own for the run's person, in the project's storage, naming the routine and asking the agent to do what the instructions say; its start, end and failure frames settle its record once (a late, repeated or foreign frame changes nothing, and only the site it was sent to is heard); three failures in a row tell the routine's person once, with a way to the routine. · fulfils AC-441d, AC-442e · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineDispatch.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineRunStatus.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1825** — A place's Routines shows the counts, Up next and a card per routine (its agent's ring, on whose behalf it runs, the switch, Run now, its last runs, and a run waiting for the person as a Needs-you bar); a routine's page is its edit screen with its runs (a skipped firing and a Run now among them), New routine is its own page, Routines in the navigation lists them across the projects the person is in, each firing's cost and the runs of today are read from the sites, and a Viewer reads with the switch and Run now greyed; deleting a routine takes its record of runs with it. · fulfils AC-441d, AC-442b · tests `hq/src/projects/routines/__tests__/RoutinesTool.test.tsx`, `hq/src/projects/routines/__tests__/routineListAndFacts.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/routines/__tests__/routineRunFacts.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineActions.integration.test.ts`

## Voice that stays in the conversation (added 2026-11-09)

- **SC-1830** — While the `voiceBridge` flag is on, the lead decides in its own call whether a spoken message is finished: told the person spoke it, it judges from the whole conversation whether they would expect an answer now (a message that trails off, stops mid-sentence or ends on "and", "so" or "um" is not finished; a short answer such as "Yes." is). Not finished: it calls `keep_listening` with a short reason and writes nothing; nothing is said, no message is written, the reason is logged and the person is told the turn was held. Their next words join the same message and the lead is told they went on. Only a spoken message whose reply has started no work can be held; the call on a typed message, or after work began, is ignored and the reply goes on. With the flag off no turn is held. · fulfils AC-325h · tests `hq/src/conversations/coordinator/__tests__/keepListening.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/lead/__tests__/voiceTurnBlock.test.ts`, `voiceLogic.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voiceClient/__tests__/heldTurn.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voice/__tests__/continue.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1831** — When a spoken message needs real work (background work, several steps or a long answer), the lead begins its reply with one short sentence saying it will look into it, naming it in a few words, and in the same step starts the work; it does not state a result in that sentence, and a reply it can give at once has no opening sentence. The sentence is spoken like any reply, and the agent's own sentence heard back is not taken as the person's words. With the person's Adaptive voice off the lead is not asked for the sentence. · fulfils AC-325i, AC-446b · tests `hq/src/conversations/lead/__tests__/voiceTurnBlock.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voiceClient/__tests__/heldTurn.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voice/playground/__tests__/playground.test.ts`
- **SC-1832** — The old phrases said at 3, 7 and 15 seconds are gone. One stored standard phrase, in the agent's voice, plays once when a message was sent and about four seconds later no reply has started and hq has not said the turn was held, and nothing more however long the wait; it is not played when the reply starts first, for a message sent as the person trailed off, for a held turn, while the person holds the floor, or with Adaptive voice off, it stops at once when the person speaks, and a reply that begins while it plays waits for it. It is not part of the reply or the record. · fulfils AC-325i, AC-446b · tests `hq/src/conversations/voiceClient/adapters/__tests__/standardAcknowledgement.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voiceClient/__tests__/heldTurn.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voice/__tests__/phrases.test.ts`
- **SC-1833** — The listening family replaces active listening and encouragement: stored clips in three tiers, made once per voice and spoken plain by the speech service with no steering — ten hums, five acknowledging words and thirteen invitations to go on. While the person talks, one sound plays in any brief pause (0.3 seconds of silence) once they have spoken 8 seconds, counted across the messages hq held, where the text heard so far ends in punctuation (late punctuation makes a late sound, dropped if they speak again first; once 30 seconds have passed since the last sound, any pause will do). It does not wait for any model's ruling on the pause. Sounds are a random 10 to 25 seconds apart, none of the last 6 again; a hum can fall at any such pause, an acknowledging word only where the last words fit one (not after a question or a request, and not inside a clause, where only a hum plays and only after the 30 seconds). Nothing plays while the agent speaks or is about to; a hum or word that began is left to finish if the person goes on, and an invitation is cut at once. An invitation plays 2 seconds after a stop word (the reply stopped and the person silent) or 4 seconds after a held turn, never closer than 10 seconds to the last sound, unless they went on or stopped the reply another way. A tier with no clips yet is silent. With Adaptive voice off nothing plays. · fulfils AC-325j, AC-446b · tests `hq/src/conversations/voiceClient/orchestration/__tests__/listening.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voiceClient/__tests__/listeningFamily.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/voice/__tests__/phrases.test.ts`
- **SC-1834** — Each person's Adaptive voice is a switch in their own Settings, on by default; its line says what the agent does now and an information note says what it covers. Turning it off or on changes only the caller's setting, a value that is not true or false is refused (400), changing another field leaves it alone, and the voice client reads it each time voice starts. Off, the listening family, the opening sentence and the stored standard phrase are all left out; holding an unfinished message, noticing the end of a message, the echo check and stop words stay. · fulfils AC-446a, AC-446b, AC-446c · tests `hq/src/settings/__tests__/userSettings.integration.test.ts`, `userSettingsView.test.ts`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/AdaptiveVoiceSection.test.tsx`, `hq/src/conversations/voiceClient/__tests__/adaptiveVoice.test.ts`
- **SC-1835** — Inworld is one provider for hearing and for speaking on one key, and the default way to hear: it is listed first among listening vendors, the Listening row offers every working provider's models in one list (choosing a model chooses its provider) and says in plain words that Inworld may write spoken times as digits and that AssemblyAI is recommended for quality, and the Add a provider pop-up says one Inworld key covers them. A listening token is single-use and never carries the key, at most sixty a minute for a provider; when the first way to hear cannot mint one, the next provider that works is used, and only when none can does the request fail (502). AssemblyAI remains and is the fall-over. A client that names no service the way the mobile app does is given AssemblyAI only, never an Inworld token, and with only Inworld set up it is told listening there needs AssemblyAI. · fulfils AC-361c, AC-361d, AC-326c · tests `hq/src/voice/__tests__/hearingFallover.test.ts`, `speechMint.test.ts`, `speechAccess.test.ts`, `hq/src/models-settings/components/__tests__/ListeningRow.test.tsx`, `hq/src/providers/components/__tests__/addProviderForm.test.tsx`

## One AI step, the key guide and Inworld's models (added 2026-11-10)

Written at the close of the unit *one key, and a simpler AI step*, from the finished code. Setup's four steps, its resume and its finish are **SC-1182**, **SC-1184** and **SC-1185**, corrected above. SC-1851 to SC-1856 were behind the `inworldLanguageModels` flag until 11 Nov 2026, when Inworld became the default provider of models as well as speech and the flag went; they are ordinary scenarios now.

- **SC-1842** — Setup's AI step lists the organisation's providers as cards, each with the vendor's name, a chip for each thing it covers (Models, Hearing, Speaking), its state in words and Remove, which asks as Manage organisation › Providers does and then reads the list again; with none it reads *None yet*. The list, the one **＋ Add a provider** button and what agents use are the same whether nothing, one or several providers are added. · fulfils AC-447a · tests `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/AiStep.test.tsx`
- **SC-1843** — **Add a provider** opens a pop-up that lists the vendors directly, each with what it covers and one line, without asking what the provider does first, and then Agenthix Cloud (*Not available yet* until Cloud offers models and voice to this HQ, otherwise its short-code panel inside the pop-up). It is one pop-up for setup and for Manage organisation › Providers, offers only vendors not yet added, is titled *Add a provider* and then *Add* and the vendor, and moves focus to its first control on each view. · fulfils AC-447a, AC-355a · tests `hq/src/providers/components/__tests__/addProviderForm.test.tsx`, `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/AiStep.test.tsx`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1844** — Under *What agents use*, the AI step shows the model, hearing and speaking, each with Change; each is chosen for the person the moment a provider that can serve it works, and is never overwritten once changed; one that no provider covers reads *Not set. Add a provider that offers models / hears people / speaks.*, which is an ordinary state and not an outage; the rows read the defaults again when a provider is added. · fulfils AC-447b, AC-357e · tests `hq/src/setup/pages/__tests__/AiStep.test.tsx`
- **SC-1845** — The AI step never blocks: with no provider that works the primary button reads **Skip for now**, and once one works the same button reads **Continue**; it stays Skip for now while the only provider does not work and says why in words, and with nothing added the screen says what stays off until one is. - **SC-1847** — At the top of a vendor's key form a short guide, *Where do I get a key?*, gives three steps (sign in or create an account; add credit if asked; create a key, copy it and paste it below) and a button to the vendor's key page that opens in a new tab; Anthropic's adds that a Claude chat plan is not an API key, Inworld's says to copy the value labelled Basic (Base64); a person's own endpoint has no guide and keeps its Address field; what was typed is kept. ## File previews people can check their work with (added 2026-11-10)

Written at the close of the unit *file previews and the VPS*, from the finished code. A file opens in one file viewer wherever it is named (**SC-1869**), drawn by ui's one frame (**SC-1578**), and the states and renderers it shows are in [`ui.md`](ui.md) (**SC-1884** to **SC-1893**). A workspace with Git reads its files at a commit through the relay (**SC-1882**, **SC-1883**), which the site and core promise in [`site.md`](site.md) and [`core.md`](core.md) (**SC-1894** to **SC-1898**).

- **SC-1869** — A file clicked where it is named — a chip in a conversation, a task's attachment, its working files and the files named in its text, a README or a message in a workspace, a run's output — opens in the file viewer over the main area, between the navigation and the chat, and the page underneath stays as it was, with its scroll and filters; the viewer is drawn once by the shell, takes focus when it opens, and an attachment or a working file is opened through hq's attachment or open action. A workspace's changes keep their diff view, and an item an agent shows beside a voice conversation stays in the chat panel. On a phone (under 768 px) the viewer is the whole screen, above the chat. · fulfils AC-450a, AC-393c, AC-394a, AC-394d
- **SC-1870** — The viewer's address is `?open=` on whatever page is under it, holding the file and where the person came from as base64url JSON, read as any outside value is: the file named through core's reading (no `..`, no upload), the place's address only as `/projects/<key>`, `/chat` or `/space`, task keys and ids by their patterns and the whole value at most 8,192 characters, and a target changed by hand into something else is dropped; opening adds a history entry so the browser's Back closes the viewer, ‹ back closes it by going back so Back afterwards does not reopen it, opening another file from inside the viewer replaces the entry, and a copied address opens the same file in the same viewer with ‹ back leaving the page under it. A task's stable link naming a file or an attachment opens the viewer over the task, swapping its parameters for `?open=` in the same entry. · fulfils AC-450c
- **SC-1871** — The viewer's header is the same for every file: ‹ back named *Conversation* for a file opened from one, the task's key for one opened from a task, else the title of the page under the viewer; the file's folder over its name; the time it changed from the open answer's version (*Changed today 07:39*, *Changed yesterday*, then a weekday or date), the same in every place a file opens; and ⋯ with *Copy link*, *Copy path*, *Show in Files* (where the file has a place's Files tool) and *Mention*. *Copy link* gives the place's stable link, never the conversation's or a ticket; a word after an action (*Link copied.*) shows under the actions for four seconds and never in the facts line; an attachment's ⋯ gives *Mention* and *Copy path* as `attachments/<key>/<name>`; *Show in Files* opens the place's Files tool on the file. · fulfils AC-450b, AC-393a, AC-393d
- **SC-1872** — Full screen, a button in the header (not on a phone, where the viewer already is the whole screen), covers everything with the same frame and its own header — ‹ *Back to the viewer*, the title, Download, ⋯ and ✕ — without opening the file again, and any of the three, or Escape, puts it back; with a ⋯ menu open Escape closes the menu only and the next Escape closes full screen. Its ⋯ has *Show in Files*: opened where it was mentioned it opens the file in its place's Files tool, and in the Files tool, which has no other to go to, it leaves full screen and shows the folder beside the file. · fulfils AC-450d
- **SC-1873** — A file's folder line starts with the place it belongs to when the page under the viewer is not that place's, judged by the page and not by the conversation the file came from: a file of Your space reads *Your space › Files › …* unless the page is `/space` or `/chat`, a project's file reads *<project name> › Files › …* unless the page is any `/projects/…` page, and a file with no known project name has no prefix; a task's working file reads *<task key> › Working files › …*, a workspace's file starts with the workspace's name, a knowledge page with *Knowledge* and an artefact with *Artefacts*. · fulfils AC-450e
- **SC-1874** — A file asked for by a path that names nothing there says *There's no file at <path> in <where>.*, where *<where>* is *Your space*, *<project>'s files* (*Files* until the project's name is known), *<task key>'s working files*, *Artefacts*, *Knowledge* or the workspace's name, and offers *Open <folder>* (the place's Files tool at that folder) or, for a task's working file that the place's own files hold at the same path, *Open Files › …*; a missing attachment says *This attachment isn't on <key> any more.*; *This file isn't here any more.* is kept for a file the Files tool or a workspace listed or a stable link names (**SC-1538**). · fulfils AC-453b, AC-393a
- **SC-1875** — While a file is open, the chat sits beside it at every width the two fit. On a medium window (768 to 1,279 px) the docked chat takes the wide window's place beside the viewer, which keeps the main area's rest of the width, the resize handle works there, and a click in the file does not put the chat away; at 1,280 px and wider nothing changes, and below 768 px the viewer is the whole screen over the full-screen chat. A chat that was expanded returns to docked while a file is open, and *Over the page* is greyed with the reason *Close the file to put the chat over the page.* and does nothing. · fulfils AC-451a
- **SC-1876** — While a file is open the chat's minimise control, or ⌘J (Ctrl+J), folds it to its bar so the file takes the full width, its button puts the chat back beside the file and never over it, and the choice holds from file to file; a chat minimised before the file opened stays minimised and does not open when a file does. Closing the viewer puts the chat back as it was before the file opened, at every width, an expanded chat expanded again; going to another page from the viewer (*Show in Files*, a link) follows that page's rule and puts nothing back. · fulfils AC-451b, AC-451c
- **SC-1877** — In a Files tool or a workspace in the Workspaces tool, opening a file folds the folders away: the file takes the whole tool with *‹ <folder>* (the workspace's name for a file at the top) back to the list and ▤ to show or hide the folder beside it; a file the place opened on by itself (a README) keeps the folder beside it, and the workspace's full-screen view keeps its tree. The tree lists folders first, then files, each by name with numbers in order and its icon; the open file's row is marked with the selected fill, the folders on the way to it are open and its row scrolls into view. · fulfils AC-455a, AC-455b
- **SC-1878** — *Search files* in a Files tool or a workspace finds files whose name holds what is typed, at any depth, listing each with its folder and opening it, and says *No files match “…”.* and never that the folder is empty; hq checks the person may read the place before its site is asked, asks nothing of the site for a place they may not read, reads nothing and signs nothing, and refuses a request with no place, or a name of no characters or over the limit, saying nothing was changed. · fulfils AC-455c
- **SC-1879** — A task's attachments are a section of their own, *Attachments*, under its description: each is a file row with its icon, name, kind, size and when it was attached, the open one marked, opening in the viewer with ‹ back named for the task and Download in the viewer's header (no download on the row); with none it says *No files attached yet.* with *Attach file* beside the words; a Viewer's *Attach file* is greyed with the reason. · fulfils AC-450f
- **SC-1880** — A chip for a file that is gone is quiet and struck through, says *Not here any more* or, for one hq cannot find, *Can't be found* in place of its kind, and still opens the viewer, which says where it looked (**SC-1874**); a chip for a hard-linked file is not struck through, since the file is there, and opens the viewer, which says why it is not shown (**SC-1881**); a chip whose written text is its kind does not say the kind twice. · fulfils AC-453c
- **SC-1881** — A hard-linked file is listed like any other and opening it answers `linked`, signing no ticket: the frame says *This file can't be opened. It's linked to another file elsewhere on the machine, so Agenthix can't be sure it's safe to show you. Ask the agent to save it again as an ordinary copy.* with *Ask the agent* and no download; *Ask the agent* puts `Please save <the file as the agent names it> again as an ordinary copy.` in the open message box for the person to send, and sends nothing; with no message box open it copies the request and says so for a moment. A chip's facts answer `linked` for it too, a file that is there. · fulfils AC-453a
- **SC-1882** — A file of a workspace with Git opens through hq's one open action, naming the commit it is read at — the branch's tip, `main`'s tip, or the older version's commit — and is drawn by the same frame and renderers as every other file, with its states, Download and, on a narrow view, ‹ back named for the folder (the workspace's name at the top); an image is drawn as an image and a PDF by its viewer; the file opens again when the commit in view moves, waits showing the frame loading while none is known, and when the file is not at that commit the frame says *It isn't on <branch> at v<n>.* with ‹ back. Both tickets name the commit and the file's blob id, and what hq answers is as for any file: no byte passes through hq-server. The view's own banner for a version that is not one of `main`'s, and the line for a branch whose changes failed to read, stay as they were. · fulfils AC-454a, AC-454b, AC-412c
- **SC-1883** — A file at a commit is never asked of a site that speaks a site protocol older than 1.18, which would read it as the file on disk without saying so: the open answers `site-unavailable` naming the site, signing nothing, and the frame says *<site> can't show a past version of a file until it is upgraded. An admin can upgrade it to the newest release.* with *Try again*; a chip asks the older site nothing about that file and answers it unavailable, the site's other files still asked; a file on disk is asked of a site of any version. · fulfils AC-454c

## The design system's messages, the chat panel, a call's buttons, the reveal and the follow (added 2026-11-11)

Written at the close of the unit *a softer, more polished design system*, from the finished code. The design system's states, the shared message pieces and the guard test are in [`ui.md`](ui.md) (**SC-799**, **SC-1900** to **SC-1908**), the Fleet Console's messages in [`console-web.md`](console-web.md) (**SC-1909**), and the words heard of a stopped reply in [`core.md`](core.md) (**SC-1922**). Corrected above at this close: **SC-722**, **SC-943**, **SC-952**, **SC-994**, **SC-995**, **SC-997**, **SC-1000**, **SC-1001**, **SC-1007**, **SC-1008**, **SC-1032** and **SC-1033**; **SC-222** retired.

### Messages never in the content

- **SC-1910** — A failure that needs the person's attention opens the attention dialog in the centre of the screen and is never a line in the page, which keeps its layout: a read HQ did not answer is titled *<what> could not be read* and says *HQ did not answer. Nothing was changed.*, with *Try again* where there is a way, the place it would have filled left empty; sites that did not answer a list are named in it, a line each; HQ read-only while it moves is said once with only Close; voice's silence warning and why voice ended are said in it (**SC-1000**). A result the screen already shows gets no message. · fulfils AC-457a
- **SC-1911** — A refusal known in advance is said by the control it refuses, never by a line beside it: greyed to 40 %, still reached by Tab, the reason in its tooltip and as its accessible description, and a press, Enter or Space opens the attention dialog with the full explanation and any way on, the control's own action never running. In hq this is the message box of a closed conversation (**SC-943**), of a conversation whose access cannot answer (**SC-952**), and Home's or a new conversation's box for someone who cannot talk to agents yet (**SC-722**), and the Start voice button, greyed with hq's reason or with the page's need of https (**SC-994**). · fulfils AC-457b
- **SC-1912** — What stays in the page: a form field's own error is said under that field, announced and tied to it (a provider's key refused, **SC-1848**); a page that is itself the refusal — an access gate, or a thing that is not there or not the person's — says HQ's own words as the page, a 401, 403 or 404 never worded as HQ not answering, and only a read that got no answer opens the dialog (the Library's asset page, a skill proposal and a task-template proposal included); what is part of the thing shown (a run's failure, a merge's clashes, a page's problems) stays drawn in it. · fulfils AC-457c, AC-457d

### A reply revealed as it appears

- **SC-1913** — In voice a reply is revealed as one position moving through its text on a critically damped spring, which eases into each hold and out of it and never passes its target, each letter fading in over 280 ms from the moment the position passes it, never word by word. The target is where the voice will be 600 ms from now: it glides through each word over the time the word is said (to each word's end where hq sends it), holds between words, runs on into the next sentence once the lead passes the end of this one, and never goes past the sentences hq has sent; before the voice begins nothing is shown. The text is laid out no further than the end of the sentence being revealed, so nothing beyond it shows, and what an interruption keeps is what was heard, behind the lead (**SC-997**). · fulfils AC-460a, AC-460b, AC-325b
- **SC-1914** — In typed chat the same reveal follows what has arrived at a steady reading pace of 200 characters a second, speeding up when it is more than 1.2 s behind, so a long burst never trails, and follows a slow stream as it comes; a word still arriving waits until it is whole. A reply loaded whole, as on opening a conversation, shows at once; a code block is shown whole, never letter by letter, and a table stays a table; text already shown is never revealed again when its block is parsed again; the stored reply that takes a stream's place carries on from where the reveal was, and never takes over another reply's. · fulfils AC-460a, AC-460c
- **SC-1915** — Under reduced motion (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`) nothing eases or fades: in voice the reply shows at once up to the reveal's target, in typed chat to the end of what has arrived, and the thread's follow jumps to the reading line. · fulfils AC-460d

### The thread follows the revealed line

- **SC-1916** — The thread follows the line being revealed and glides with it: the point followed moves along the revealed line with the reveal, from the line's top to its bottom, and the view moves at the reveal's own pace, learnt as it goes, easing out what is left on a critically damped spring, so it neither steps a line at a time nor starts and stops with each line, never moves back up by itself and never passes the point. In typed chat the point is held one line above the usual gap over the message box (the line a reply's buttons will take, 32 px, 44 px below the `lg` width, measured from the buttons already drawn), so the newest line sits just above the box; in voice it is held 3 lines (25 px each) above the box. The message box's top is taken under the live line, which floats over that room. It never scrolls past the end and does not scroll a thread that fits. With no reveal under way it holds the end of the thread, and the person's own words below a reply, sent or still being said, are followed as they grow; a reply that has finished revealing is not followed again, one voice moved on from included. The first follow on opening a conversation jumps. · fulfils AC-461a
- **SC-1917** — Only the person, scrolling up, lets go of the follow: a wheel up, a touch, ArrowUp, PageUp or Home, or a press on the scroll bar; the thread's own scrolling and the person scrolling down never let go. While let go nothing pulls the view back, and the round ↓ above the box shows, with a dot once a new answer has arrived meanwhile; pressing ↓ follows again, and so does scrolling down until the revealed line is in view, even behind the box, once that scroll has rested. · fulfils AC-461b
- **SC-1918** — The follow never fights the person's scroll: while a scroll that is not the follow's own, a wheel down or a key that scrolls down (ArrowDown, PageDown, End, Space) has not rested for 150 ms, a fling's momentum included, the follow moves nothing; then it carries on from where the view stopped, never taking it back up, and moves again once the revealed line passes it. · fulfils AC-461c
- **SC-1919** — Taking text away under the followed line, as an interruption's cut does (**SC-997**), leaves the view where it is until the line moves below that point again, the hold released when the person scrolls, presses ↓ or the view first jumps into place; switching between typed chat and voice moves nothing: a reply keeps the room and reading line of the way it started (**SC-1923**) until it has ended, the room at rest is the same in both, and the box keeps its height. · fulfils AC-461d
- **SC-1920** — The thread's scroll bar is always shown, not only while the view moves: a thin bar in the strong border's colour (the muted text colour on hover) on a clear track, its gutter kept so the content never shifts; Firefox gets its standard thin bar in the same colours, following the system's setting for overlay scroll bars. · fulfils AC-461e
- **SC-1923** — The room the thread keeps under its last line, beyond the message box floating over it, is the gap between two messages (20 px) at rest, so a conversation opens, and settles, with its last message just above the box. From the moment a reply begins until it has been shown to its end — while its turn runs or streams, while it is still being revealed, typed or with the voice, and while it waits for its voice — it is, in typed chat, one line for the reply's buttons and the usual gap, and in voice the 3 reading lines (**SC-1916**); growing it moves nothing on screen. While a reply is revealed, what is laid out ahead of the reveal is clipped below that room, so scrolling to the end of the thread shows the revealed line, its room and nothing more, never the space of words still to come. A reply's buttons appear only once it has been revealed to its end, typed chat included, and when they do they take their height from the room in the same frame, never leaving less than the gap between messages, so the view and the thread's height stay as they were and nothing scrolls: a typed reply is then left with the usual gap. What is left of a spoken reply's room goes all at once, the first time it is out of sight below the view: the person scrolls up past it (at once when they had already scrolled up), or the thread is hidden or opened again; it never goes while the view rests at the end of the thread. Sending again makes the room whole again. Nothing about the room eases. · fulfils AC-461f

### The person's words in a call

- **SC-1921** — In a call the words the person is saying are their ordinary grey bubble at the end of the thread, laid out as a sent message is — the same bubble, byline (*You · time*, the time the words began) and place, never an outline stand-in — and once sent the bubble stays until the message holding them lands in its place (a new message of theirs, or their last one growing when the words join it), so it becomes the sent message without a gap or a jump; words whose message never lands go after 4 s at most, and at once when the call ends. The voice playground draws the person's entries and the words being said with the same bubble, at the end of its transcript, and its status line says only *Listening* or *Muted*. · fulfils AC-462a, AC-462b
